```
        ▲                    ▲                    ▲
        │                    │                    │
   Substrate Registry   Decision Trace Store   Fault Injection Harness
```

---

## 3. Layer 1 — Substrate Contract and Registry

### 3.1 The Substrate Contract

Every module must declare this contract before registration. The contract is machine-readable and versioned.

```yaml
SubstrateContract:
  identity:
    module_id: string            # unique, immutable
    version: semver
    author: string
    license: string
    role: enum[perception, prediction, sentinel, solver, verifier,
               governance, feature_extractor, memory, actuator]
  
  perception_signature:
    modalities: [enum]           # dense_vector, temporal_stream, spike_train,
                                 # symbol_graph, wavelet_coeffs, physical_sensor,
                                 # hypervector, tabular, text
    shapes: map[modality -> tensor_shape]
    sampling_requirements: map[modality -> {rate, window, sync}]
  
  emission_signature:
    output_form: enum[hypervector, spike_train, energy_gradient,
                      symbolic_triple, probability_interval, scalar,
                      assignment_certificate, feature_vector]
    shape: tensor_shape
    uncertainty: enum[none, conformal_interval, ensemble_spread,
                       energy_landscape, symbolic_proof]
    determinism: enum[deterministic, seeded, stochastic]
  
  metabolic_profile:
    latency_p50_ms: float
    latency_p95_ms: float
    energy_per_inference_j: float
    memory_footprint_mb: float
    requires_gradient_training: bool
    requires_gpu: bool
    requires_special_hardware: enum[none, neuromorphic, event_camera, physical]
    max_concurrent_invocations: int
    metabolic_budget_class: enum[lean, standard, heavy, research]
  
  failure_signature:
    failure_modes:
      - name: string
        signal: string           # observable indicator
        detection_method: string
        expected_degradation: enum[silent_accuracy_loss, spike_silence,
                                   energy_divergence, symbol_grounding_failure,
                                   calibration_drift, timeout, crash]
    health_probe: string         # how to query health
    recovery_procedure: string
  
  safety:
    reliability_tier: enum[T0_deterministic, T1_calibrated,
                           T2_learned_calibrated, T3_learned_uncalibrated]
    may_actuate: bool
    requires_causal_certificate: bool
    requires_cross_check: bool
    max_safety_tier: enum[critical, high, medium, low, research]
  
  lifecycle:
    hot_swap_supported: bool
    rollback_artifact: string
    shadow_validation_required: bool
    promotion_criteria: string
```

### 3.2 The Registry

The **Substrate Registry** is the authoritative catalog of all modules. It exposes:

- `register(contract) -> module_handle`
- `deregister(module_id, drain_policy)`
- `query(capability_filter) -> [module_handle]`
- `health(module_id) -> HealthReport`
- `promote(module_id, from_mode, to_mode)` — requires shadow validation evidence
- `hot_swap(module_id, new_version, strategy)`

The registry is append-only and auditable. Every registration, promotion, and swap is logged with a checksum.

### 3.3 Module Lifecycle States

```
PROPOSED → SANDBOXED → SHADOW_VALIDATED → EXPLORATION → DELIBERATION → DEPRECATED → RETIRED
```

A module in `EXPLORATION` may read from the workspace but may not write bindings that affect control. A module in `DELIBERATION` may participate in production dispatch. Promotion from `EXPLORATION` to `DELIBERATION` requires:

1. Passing all evaluation gates (§12)
2. Shadow validation: N production-like invocations with outputs compared to incumbent
3. A causal review if the module can influence actuation
4. A signed promotion record

---

## 4. Layer 2 — VSA Global Workspace

### 4.1 Role

The workspace is the **circulatory system**. It is a bounded, garbage-collected hypervector store. Every module reads from it and writes to it. There are no direct module-to-module channels.

### 4.2 Representation

- **Default dimensionality:** 10,000 bits (binary) or 10,000-dim real-valued, configurable per deployment.
- **Operations:** `bind(a, b)`, `bundle(a, b, ...)`, `permute(a, k)`, `unbind(c, role)`, `similarity(a, b)` (cosine or Hamming).
- **Role/filler discipline:** every binding carries a role vector and a filler vector. Retrieval is `unbind(binding, role)` followed by similarity search.

### 4.3 Workspace Entry Schema

```yaml
WorkspaceBinding:
  binding_id: uuid
  hypervector: binary|float[d]
  role: hypervector
  filler: hypervector
  provenance:
    module_id: string
    version: semver
    timestamp: datetime
    task_id: uuid
  salience:
    score: float[0,1]
    decay_rate: float          # per consolidation cycle
    fidelity_tier: enum[full, compressed, summarized]
  safety:
    affects_control: bool
    requires_verification: bool
    verification_status: enum[none, pending, passed, failed]
  tags: [string]
```

### 4.4 Memory Model

- **Bounded cap:** hard memory ceiling per workspace instance.
- **Salience-driven compaction:** low-salience bindings are superposed and compressed; high-salience bindings (recent failures, active control state, current task spec, causal certificates) are retained at full fidelity.
- **Garbage collection:** triggered by GC pressure metric or consolidation cycle. Compaction merges bindings with similarity above a threshold and salience below a threshold.
- **Query latency SLO:** p95 query latency must remain below a declared threshold; if breached, the control loop triggers workspace degradation diagnosis.

### 4.5 Query Semantics

- `query_by_role(role_cue) -> [binding]` — unbind and rank by similarity.
- `query_by_tag(tags) -> [binding]`
- `query_by_provenance(module_id, time_range) -> [binding]`
- `query_similar(hypervector, k) -> [(binding, score)]`

### 4.6 Why VSA

The workspace is the theoretical license for cross-substrate communication. Attention has been formally reinterpreted as approximate VSA binding/unbinding; a neural encoder and a symbolic reasoner are doing approximate and exact binding respectively over the same algebra. This is why the workspace can be the common currency without a bespoke adapter per module pair.

---

## 5. Layer 3 — Structural Router

### 5.1 Role

The router is the **thalamus**. It does not ask "which module has the highest gating logit?" It answers three structural questions in sequence.

### 5.2 Task Descriptor

Every task entering Organism carries a descriptor:

```yaml
TaskDescriptor:
  task_id: uuid
  form: enum[temporal_prediction, pattern_recognition, anomaly_detection,
              constrained_allocation, causal_inference, symbolic_reasoning,
              signal_analysis, feature_extraction, retrieval, generation,
              calibration, verification]
  input_modalities: [enum]
  output_form_required: enum
  safety_tier: enum[critical, high, medium, low, research]
  deadline_ms: float
  energy_budget_j: float
  requires_uncertainty: bool
  requires_interpretability: bool
  requires_determinism: bool
  metadata: map
```

### 5.3 Routing Decision Tree

**Question 1 — Task form.** Map `form` to a set of candidate module roles via a static, auditable decision tree. This tree is a configuration artifact, versioned and diffable. It is not a learned model.

**Question 2 — Reliability tier.** Filter candidates by `contract.safety.reliability_tier >= task.safety_tier` requirement. This is a **hard constraint**. A T3 learned module cannot serve a `critical` decision.

**Question 3 — Metabolic state.** Consult the current physiological state (thermal, power, memory, module health). Re-rank remaining candidates. If the nominally best module is degraded, prefer the next candidate.

**Output:** a `DispatchPlan`:

```yaml
DispatchPlan:
  task_id: uuid
  primary: module_handle
  fallbacks: [module_handle]   # ordered
  rationale: string            # human-readable decision path
  constraints_applied: [string]
  degradation_level: enum[L0,L1,L2,L3,L4]
  cross_check_required: bool
  cross_check_module: module_handle | null
```

### 5.4 Auditability

The router's decision tree is a plain data structure. Every dispatch emits the path taken. There is no black-box gating network in the control path. A learned meta-router may be used **only** to propose tree updates offline, which are then reviewed and versioned.

### 5.5 Routing Signals from Failure

If a module returns an infeasibility certificate (e.g., energy solver finds no feasible state), the router treats it as a **routing signal**, not an error: it tries the next fallback or relaxes a constraint per a declared policy.

---

## 6. Layer 4 — Substrate Modules

Organism is algorithm-agnostic. The modules below are the reference ecology; any algorithm that implements the contract can join. Each entry states its role, integration point, and the production constraint that keeps it honest.

### 6.1 Neural (gradient-trained) module
- **Role:** pattern recognition, differentiable representation learning, candidate generation.
- **Integration:** writes `bind(feature_vector, role_encoder)` to workspace.
- **Constraint:** untrusted (T2/T3). May not be sole authority for control. Requires conformal calibration for any safety-tier above `low`. Must never be routed raw perception if a cheaper front-end suffices.

### 6.2 HDC/VSA module
- **Role:** symbolic integration, robust fingerprinting, interpretable retrieval, workspace-native composition.
- **Integration:** binds features from other modules; serves as Tier-1 fallback when neural modules exhibit silent accuracy loss.
- **Constraint:** never route raw high-dimensional perception directly to HDC. HDC receives already-extracted features. Its value is composition and retrieval, not feature extraction.

### 6.3 Reservoir computing module
- **Role:** fixed-dynamics temporal prediction, stream anomaly detection, ultra-low-energy readout.
- **Integration:** front-end featurizer; readout trained by kernel module or ridge regression; periodic projection of reservoir state into workspace as temporal hypervector.
- **Constraint:** per-device-class configuration library, selected at device join, not retuned at runtime. Level-2 degradation target for temporal decisions (fails by underfitting, not confident hallucination).

### 6.4 Spiking neural network (SNN) module
- **Role:** always-on sparse anomaly detection, event triggering, wake-up signaling.
- **Integration:** emits spike-rate salience scalar tagged with sensor modality; router escalates on threshold crossing.
- **Constraint:** declared **non-actuating**. Gated on hardware capability — registered but not dispatched to production control on general-purpose hardware.

### 6.5 Kernel module
- **Role:** closed-form/convex prediction for mid-small-sample high-reliability tasks; readout trainer for reservoir; deterministic fallback for neural regressors.
- **Integration:** consumes workspace features; emits prediction with explicit generalization bound or conformal interval.
- **Constraint:** support set capped (coreset or random Fourier features), retrained periodically. Uncapped kernel becomes memory liability.

### 6.6 Energy-based / Modern Hopfield module
- **Role:** constrained combinatorial allocation via energy-landscape convergence; associative memory for stable regimes.
- **Integration:** solver, not predictor. Receives task spec + hard constraints + learned cost; runs dynamical system to convergence; writes `bind(assignment, constraint_satisfaction_certificate)`.
- **Constraint:** multiple restarts with perturbation; returns best feasible state plus infeasibility certificate if none found. Infeasibility is a routing signal.

### 6.7 Neurosymbolic / Causal module
- **Role:** causal verification of cross-substrate actions, root-cause classification, interpretable explanation, safety-shield skeleton.
- **Integration:** performs three-level verification — observational, interventional (`do(action)`), counterfactual — before any cross-substrate action executes. Returns recognition score + causal explanation chain.
- **Constraint:** causal discovery runs incrementally on a rolling window; graph is versioned and diffed. A change to a safety-critical edge triggers deliberation-mode review.

### 6.8 Spectral learning module
- **Role:** multi-scale temporal/spatial feature extraction in wavelet domain without quadratic attention cost.
- **Integration:** front-end for telemetry and long-window signals; emits compact spectral feature vector to workspace.
- **Constraint:** feature extractor and lightweight classifier only. Not routed to compositional reasoning or long-range symbolic dependencies.

### 6.9 Local learning module (FF / PC / EP / FA)
- **Role:** on-device continual personalization without backpropagation; local credit assignment.
- **Integration:** updates its own weights from local signals; writes updated state summary to workspace.
- **Constraint:** operates in a resource sandbox; updates are checkpointed and rollback-able.

### 6.10 Physical / molecular module (research tier)
- **Role:** experimental computation on physical dynamics.
- **Integration:** registered with the same contract, but metabolic profile declares non-determinism, reset cost, limited observability.
- **Constraint:** cannot enter critical control path. Exploration mode only. Outputs treated as hypotheses to be validated by digital modules.

### 6.11 Deterministic rule engine
- **Role:** L4 refuge. Fixed conservative rules, no learned parameters.
- **Integration:** reads workspace; emits decisions with no uncertainty (by construction).
- **Constraint:** always available. Never demoted. This is the floor of the degradation gradient.

---

## 7. Layer 5 — Homeostatic Control Loop

Runs at four cadences. This is how Organism stays alive.

### 7.1 Perception (every telemetry window)
Collect: per-module health signals; conformal coverage per calibrated module; workspace integrity (query latency, retrieval similarity distribution, GC pressure); task queue composition (safety-tier distribution, deadline pressure); resource envelope (thermal, power, memory, energy budget).

### 7.2 Diagnosis (every N windows)
- **Calibration drift:** empirical conformal coverage below guarantee → demote one level.
- **Structural mismatch:** task queue dominated by a form the current mix is poorly matched to → router dispatch table is stale; flag for metamodel update.
- **Workspace degradation:** retrieval similarity decaying or GC frequency rising → compaction or salience-threshold adjustment.

### 7.3 Response (event-driven)
Pre-compiled policies, not learned actions:
- `demote(module, levels)` → update DispatchPlan, widen shield margin, log.
- `enter_exploration(window_budget)` → bounded window, speculative configs allowed, hard return checkpoint.
- `trigger_synthesis(spec)` → emit to metamodel.

### 7.4 Consolidation (offline cadence)
Update: per-device-class reservoir config library; kernel coreset; causal graph edge-confidence; router decision tree (via offline meta-learning from routing outcomes, reviewed before deployment). Consolidation is where Organism learns its own physiology. Without it, the loop oscillates.

---

## 8. Layer 6 — Degradation Physiology

| Level | Trigger | Active modules | Safety posture | Capability retained |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **L0 Homeostasis** | All health green; conformal coverage within tolerance | Full ecology; neural leads perception | Normal shields; causal verification on actuation | Full |
| **L1 Metabolic shift** | Neural thermal-throttled or bounded calibration drift | Neural demoted; reservoir + kernel lead temporal; HDC retains continuity | Shields widened by calibrated margin; causal verification unchanged | Full control, reduced prediction precision |
| **L2 Substrate substitution** | Neural conformal intervals exceed tolerance; HDC/reservoir degraded but kernel healthy | Kernel + spectral lead; energy solver handles allocation; SNN sentinel remains | Conservative bounds; any control action requires causal certificate | Core function; no novel optimization |
| **L3 Symbolic retrieval** | Predictive modules unreliable; workspace intact | HDC retrieval only; returns nearest historical decision pattern with similarity score | Act only if similarity exceeds strict threshold; otherwise hold | Safe hold and replay of known-good patterns |
| **L4 Deterministic refuge** | Workspace corruption, Byzantine suspicion, or catastrophic failure | Deterministic rule engine only; no learned module in loop | Fixed conservative rules; human escalation | Safe idle; no adaptation |

**Hysteresis:** transition up requires sustained health (M consecutive windows); transition down requires sustained degradation. Thresholds are tunable per deployment but must be explicit in config. Recovery to a higher level is slower than degradation to a lower level — the system is quick to protect, slow to trust again.

**Mandatory fault injection:** every transition (L0→L1, L1→L2, L2→L3, L3→L4 and recovery) must be exercised under load: kill neural runtime, corrupt a workspace segment, simulate Byzantine module output, saturate the workspace. Measure recovery time to a stable level. Tune hysteresis from measured data.

---

## 9. Layer 7 — Metamodel (Reproductive System)

When the current ecology cannot meet a task demand, Organism grows a new module.

- **Instant adapter synthesis** (hypernetwork): new readout for an existing reservoir, new binding role for the workspace — seconds.
- **Rapid architecture synthesis** (zero-shot NAS): new spectral front-end or SNN topology for a device class — 30–120 seconds.
- **Pipeline synthesis** (lightweight AutoML): complete new module pipeline for a novel task form — 1–5 minutes.

**Reproductive constraint (immune response):** every synthesized module is born `PROPOSED`, enters `SANDBOXED`, runs in exploration mode only, cannot actuate, and must pass the same contract declaration and shadow validation as any other module. Organism does not accept a new organ without an immune response.

---

## 10. Cross-Cutting Concerns

### 10.1 Observability — Decision Trace
For every dispatch, emit:
- which module was dispatched and why (structural routing path)
- the input binding (unbound from workspace)
- the output and its uncertainty interval
- causal verification result (if cross-boundary)
- active degradation level and whether the shield intervened

The Decision Trace Store is the organism's memory of its own reasoning and the raw material for consolidation. Without it, a multi-module organism is unauditable.

### 10.2 Resource governance
Each module runs in its own cgroup/container sandbox. Workspace runs in a dedicated memory arena with a hard cap. Neural modules run in GPU MIG slices or time-sliced partitions. The scheduler enforces a **metabolic budget**: no module exceeds its declared share, even if it is best for the current task. This prevents a single module from starving the ecology.

### 10.3 Trust and Byzantine tolerance
- **Trusted:** causal verifier, deterministic rule engine, workspace integrity layer. Simple, auditable, no learned parameters.
- **Untrusted:** all learned modules.
- **Single-device equivalent of BFT:** output cross-checking. A critical action proposed by a learned module must be independently verified by a non-learned module (kernel, symbolic, or deterministic) before execution.
- **Distributed Organism (future):** triple-redundant state with content-addressed anchoring and `N ≥ 3f + 1`.

### 10.4 Lifecycle and versioning
Every artifact — HDC encoder, reservoir config, kernel coreset, causal graph, spectral basis, SNN weights, router decision tree, contract — is versioned, checksummed, and rollback-able. A module upgrade is a **metabolic transplant**: hot-swap while others continue, new module enters at lowest trust and earns promotion through shadow validation.

### 10.5 Security
- Module artifacts signed; registry enforces signature check on registration and promotion.
- Workspace bindings are provenance-tagged; queries can filter by trusted provenance.
- Control-affecting bindings require a verification status before they can be consumed by an actuating module.
- Exploration-mode modules cannot write control-affecting bindings; the workspace rejects them.

---

## 11. Build Sequence

Do not instantiate the full ecology at once. Failure modes are emergent; they appear only when modules interact. Build in this order:

1. **Workspace + contract + health monitor.** Two modules only: one learned (if available) and the deterministic rule engine. Prove workspace queryability under load and health monitor detection of simulated learned-module failure.
2. **Reservoir + kernel substitution path.** Prove L1→L2: throttle/corrupt the learned module, verify degradation to reservoir+kernel with bounded capability loss.
3. **Causal verification + energy solver.** Prove no energy-solver assignment reaches actuation without a causal certificate; prove infeasibility is handled as a routing signal.
4. **Spectral + SNN sentinel.** Prove sentinel wakes heavier modules without false-alarm flooding.
5. **Metamodel reproductive loop.** Prove a diagnosed deficiency produces a new adapter/architecture that passes shadow validation and enters the ecology.
6. **Fault injection at scale.** Full ecology under simulated hardware degradation, Byzantine outputs, workspace saturation, partition events. Measure recovery time per level; tune hysteresis.

**Governing principle:** an ecology is only as stable as its weakest transition. Organism is proven by the smoothness and speed of its degradation and its ability to return to homeostasis without human intervention — not by best-case performance.

---

## 12. Evaluation Gates

A module may enter the production routing table only when it significantly outperforms the incumbent on at least two dimensions and shows no catastrophic degradation on the rest:

1. **In-distribution performance** vs. incumbent on the same test set.
2. **OOD degradation curve** under hardware degradation, distribution shift, noise.
3. **Energy ratio** (J/inference, J/training-sample) on target hardware.
4. **Calibration and uncertainty** — conformal coverage retention. No uncertainty estimate → no safety-critical routing.
5. **Sample efficiency** — labeled samples to target performance.
6. **Interpretability** — decisions trace to input features or symbolic structure, not only hidden activations.
7. **Failure-mode independence** — does it fail differently from the incumbent, rather than collapsing simultaneously? This is the core value of the heterogeneous combination.

---

## 13. Open Design Decisions and Next Artifacts

The specification above is buildable but leaves several decisions that should be made explicitly before implementation:

| # | Decision | Options |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Workspace hypervector type | Binary 10k-bit vs. real 10k-dim vs. complex |
| 2 | Workspace persistence | In-memory only vs. durable with snapshot/replay |
| 3 | Router implementation | Static decision tree (recommended) vs. offline-learned tree with review gate |
| 4 | Cross-check policy | Which safety tiers require cross-check; which modules may serve as cross-checkers |
| 5 | Hysteresis defaults | M-window counts and thresholds per level |
| 6 | Metamodel scope | Which synthesis methods are in v1 vs. later |
| 7 | Distributed vs. single-device | Whether v1 targets one device or a cluster |
| 8 | Contract transport | JSON Schema vs. Protobuf vs. IDL with codegen |

**Proposed next artifacts (pick one and I will produce it):**

- **A. Substrate Contract IDL + Registry API** — full schema, codegen targets, registration/promotion/hot-swap endpoints.
- **B. Degradation State Machine + Fault-Injection Test Plan** — formal states, triggers, hysteresis, test matrix, recovery SLOs.
- **C. Router Decision Tree Specification** — task metadata fields, form→role mapping, reliability-tier constraints, metabolic re-ranking rules.
- **D. Workspace Memory Model** — salience scoring, decay, compaction/GC algorithm, query planner, integrity checks.
- **E. Organism v0.1 Reference Implementation Plan** — repo layout, module SDK, two-module bootstrap, CI gates.

Which artifact do you want first — and should Organism v1 target a single device or a cluster?

# Response to Organism v0.1 — Peer Review, Decisions, and Artifacts A & B

You've built something unusually disciplined. The axioms in §1 are the right axioms, the L0–L4 physiology is the right failure model, and treating the metamodel as a reproductive system with an immune response is the correct framing. I'm not going to rewrite what's working. I'm going to flag the places where the spec as written cannot be built at performance or cannot be audited at runtime, decide the eight items in §13, and then deliver the two artifacts that everything else depends on.

I've read v0.1 as a spec you intend to implement, not as a manifesto. My review assumes that.

---

## 1. Verdict

**Ship-ready in structure, not yet in detail.** The layer decomposition is correct and the contract-first discipline is correct. Three things block implementation:

1. Axiom 2 as written ("workspace or nothing") is **unbuildable at performance** and will be quietly violated by the first engineering team unless refined.
2. The §8 degradation triggers are prose, not conditions — they cannot be tested, and a degradation path you can't test is a degradation path you don't have.
3. The contract schema in §3.1 is under-specified in exactly the places that matter for enforcement (health probes, capability scoping, budget claims).

Everything else is either correct as written or correctable in the artifacts below.

---

## 2. Eight Corrections Required Before Implementation

### 2.1 Axiom 2 is too strict — add typed channels as a declared, logged path

Every cross-module communication through VSA means every inference pays an unbind/rebind cost, and VSA bindings are **lossy by construction**. You cannot route a 512-channel spectral feature vector through a 10,000-bit binary hypervector without destroying information. This is not a performance concern — it's a correctness concern.

**Correction:** Distinguish two paths, both declared in the contract:

- **Semantic state** (task specs, decisions, causal certificates, control-affecting claims, retrieval keys) → VSA workspace, as specified. This is what makes the organism auditable.
- **Typed data channels** (feature tensors between adjacent modules, spike trains, raw telemetry windows) → direct typed pipe, declared in the contract's `ChannelDeclarations`, **logged for the Decision Trace**, and gated by the same capability scoping as the workspace.

The axiom becomes: *"No semantic state may bypass the workspace. No data may flow on an undeclared channel. All declared channels are logged."* This preserves auditability while letting the spectral front-end hand a feature vector to a classifier without round-tripping through binary VSA.

### 2.2 Workspace determinism requires snapshot semantics

The spec requires "every decision is reconstructible" (Axiom 6) but the workspace has no snapshot model. Query results depend on insertion order, GC timing, and compaction. A Decision Trace that records *which binding was read* is worthless if you can't reconstruct the workspace state at the moment of the read.

**Correction:** Workspace is **tiered**:

- **Hot tier:** in-memory, mutable, serves queries.
- **Snapshot tier:** immutable point-in-time snapshot written at every consolidation cycle (and on-demand before any L2→L3 transition). Content-addressed. This is what replay reads from.
- **Replay semantics:** replay = snapshot + Decision Trace entries + input stream. If a decision cannot be reconstructed from these three, it is a bug in the workspace, not in the trace.

Snapshot cost is bounded by the salience compaction you already specified — you're not snapshotting the whole store, you're snapshotting the full-fidelity tier plus compressed representations of the rest.

### 2.3 The contract needs typed health probes, not strings

`health_probe: string` in §3.1 means the runtime cannot call it. It has to know the protocol.

**Correction:** `HealthProbe` is a typed union: HTTP endpoint, registered RPC, or event stream (for event-driven modules like SNN sentinels that don't answer queries). The runtime polls via the declared protocol; the module declares its **detection latency budget** (how long before it must be detected as failed). This is what makes the L0→L1 transition testable.

### 2.4 Cross-check needs a justification-token mechanism

§10.3 says "a critical action proposed by a learned module must be independently verified by a non-learned module." But **what does the verifier verify?** If it recomputes the decision, you've doubled your cost and the two modules may disagree for benign reasons. If it just says "looks fine," you've added a rubber stamp.

**Correction:** The proposing module must emit a **machine-checkable justification token** alongside its action. The cross-checker verifies the token, not the action. For a causal verifier, the token is a `do()`-calculus certificate. For a kernel fallback, it's a generalization-bound certificate. For a deterministic rule engine, it's a rule-trace. This is the only form of cross-check that is both cheap and meaningful.

This requires the contract to declare `JustificationSpec`: what kind of justification the module emits, what format, what fields must be present, and which module roles can verify it. Non-actuating modules declare `none`.

### 2.5 The reliability tier lattice must be specified, not implied

The spec has `ReliabilityTier {T0, T1, T2, T3}` and `MaxSafetyTier {critical, high, medium, low, research}` and says "Filter candidates by `contract.safety.reliability_tier >= task.safety_tier`." But these are **different enumerations**. The comparison is undefined.

**Correction:** Fix the ordering once, in the contract:

```
ReliabilityTier ordering (higher = more trusted):
  T0_deterministic > T1_calibrated_nonlearned > T2_learned_calibrated > T3_learned_uncalibrated

MaxSafetyTier ordering (higher = more demanding):
  critical > high > medium > low > research

Eligibility: module may serve task iff TIER_RANK(module.reliability) >= REQUIRED_TIER(task.safety)
  where REQUIRED_TIER maps:
    critical  → T0 or T1
    high      → T1 or T2
    medium    → T2 or T3
    low       → T3 or any
    research  → any
```

Note that **T2 and T3 learned modules cannot serve critical tasks at all**, even with cross-check. This should be explicit: cross-check is a *safety net*, not a *trust upgrade*. A learned module with cross-check is still a learned module; cross-check reduces the blast radius of its errors but does not make it T1.

### 2.6 Metabolic budget enforcement needs an arbitration spec

§10.2 says "no module exceeds its declared share, even if it is best for the current task." But what happens when two lean modules both want CPU and the sum exceeds capacity? What is preemptible? What is guaranteed?

**Correction:** Add to §10.2:

- **Guaranteed floor:** every module gets `min(declared_share, floor_guarantee)` where `floor_guarantee` is a deployment config.
- **Fair-share above floor:** remaining capacity allocated by max-min fairness within budget class.
- **Preemption rules:** heavy modules are preemptible by lean modules within a declared preemption window; exploration-mode modules are always preemptible; deliberation-mode deterministic modules are never preemptible.
- **Starvation check:** if any module receives less than 50% of its declared share for more than `starvation_window` (default 60s), the control loop emits a diagnosis event. This is the signal that the ecology is over-subscribed.

### 2.7 Workspace needs capability scoping

As written, every module can read every binding and write to any role. This violates the trust model: a compromised or buggy module can pollute the workspace with bindings that a downstream actuating module will consume.

**Correction:** Add `CapabilityScopes` to the contract: read_roles, write_roles, read_tags, write_tags, allowed_channels, and `can_emit_control_affecting` (bool). The workspace rejects writes outside scope. Exploration-mode modules have `can_emit_control_affecting = false` unconditionally — this enforces §10.5 as a mechanism, not a policy.

### 2.8 Evaluation gates must be role-specific

§12 says a module must "significantly outperform the incumbent on at least two dimensions." Two dimensions *of what*? A causal verifier cannot possibly outperform a neural model on in-distribution accuracy — that's not its job. As written, the gate either blocks every verifier or is trivially satisfiable.

**Correction:** Gate criteria are **role-conditioned**:

| Role | Required 2+ dimensions | Never required |
|---|---|---|
| perception, prediction | in-distribution performance, OOD degradation | failure independence (compared to itself) |
| sentinel | energy ratio, detection latency | in-distribution accuracy |
| solver | feasibility rate, energy ratio, certificate validity | in-distribution accuracy |
| verifier, governance | failure-mode independence, interpretability, auditability | latency vs. neural |
| feature_extractor | energy ratio, feature fidelity, latency | end-to-end accuracy |
| actuator | determinism, safety compliance, rollback latency | any accuracy |

This is a table in the contract as `required_gates` per role, not a paragraph in the spec.

---

## 3. Decisions on §13

All eight, with rationale. These are decisions, not options — implement them unless you can show me a specific failure case.

| # | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Binary 10,000-bit** hypervectors for workspace storage. Real-valued computation permitted inside a module; quantized to binary at write boundary. | Binary Hamming similarity is `O(d/64)` word operations — deterministic, bit-exact, and matches the published HDC literature you're building on. The workspace is the audit substrate, not the compute substrate. Real-valued workspace would introduce floating-point non-determinism into the Decision Trace, defeating Axiom 6. |
| 2 | **Durable with tiered snapshot/replay.** Hot tier in memory; immutable content-addressed snapshot at consolidation + before any L2→L3 transition; replay reads from snapshot. | Without snapshots, Axiom 6 is unenforceable. See correction §2.2. |
| 3 | **Static decision tree** as a versioned config artifact. Learned meta-router is offline proposer only, reviewed before deployment. | Confirmed as-is. The control path must be auditable; a learned gate is not. |
| 4 | **Cross-check is by safety tier × action class**, and the mechanism is a justification token (see correction §2.4). Critical + hardware-actuating → must have T0/T1 cross-checker that verifies a token; the proposing module must include the token in its emission. Cross-check is not a trust upgrade (see §2.5). | This makes §10.3 a mechanism instead of a policy. |
| 5 | **Time-based hysteresis, not count-based.** Degradation confirm window `T_down`, recovery confirm window `T_up`, with `T_up ≥ 5·T_down` per transition. Defaults below (Artifact B §B.4). | Count-based hysteresis breaks under variable telemetry cadence (which the cluster will have). Time-based is reproducible and testable. |
| 6 | **Metamodel v1 = hypernetwork adapter synthesis + one tabular pipeline synthesizer.** Zero-shot NAS deferred to v2. | v1 must prove the *promotion path* works end-to-end (synthesize → shadow → promote → serve). Adding NAS before the promotion path is validated multiplies failure modes for no benefit. |
| 7 | **Single-device first.** Cluster extension interface defined in the contract (transport-agnostic modules, addressable registry) but not implemented until the single-device ecology passes the fault-injection matrix. | Distributed brings Byzantine tolerance, clock sync, network partition, and state replication — all orthogonal to contract validation. Building them together means you can't tell a contract bug from a network bug. |
| 8 | **Protobuf as source of truth, JSON Schema as generated mirror.** Proto3 for runtime codegen (Python, Rust, Kotlin, C++), JSON Schema generated for CI validation and human review. | Single source of truth prevents schema drift. Protobuf gives fast typed serialization for the health probe and channel transport; JSON Schema gives readable diffs for contract review. |

---

## 4. Artifact A — Substrate Contract v0.2 + Registry API

This supersedes §3.1 of v0.1. It is the proto3 source of truth. JSON Schema is generated from this (do not hand-maintain both).

### A.1 Contract definition

```proto
syntax = "proto3";
package organism.v1;

// =====================================================================
// SubstrateContract — the four signatures plus capabilities, justification,
// and lifecycle metadata. Every module must register this before dispatch.
// =====================================================================
message SubstrateContract {
  Identity identity                    = 1;
  PerceptionSignature perception       = 2;
  EmissionSignature emission           = 3;
  MetabolicProfile metabolic           = 4;
  FailureSignature failure             = 5;
  SafetyDeclaration safety             = 6;
  Lifecycle lifecycle                  = 7;

  // v0.2 additions
  CapabilityScopes capabilities        = 8;
  repeated Channel channels            = 9;
  JustificationSpec justification      = 10;
}

// ----- Identity ------------------------------------------------------
message Identity {
  string module_id       = 1;  // UUIDv7, immutable
  string version         = 2;  // semver
  string author          = 3;
  string license         = 4;
  ModuleRole role        = 5;
  bytes  artifact_digest = 6;  // SHA-256 of the artifact bundle
  repeated bytes signatures_b64 = 7;  // author + promotor signatures
}

enum ModuleRole {
  ROLE_UNSPECIFIED       = 0;
  ROLE_PERCEPTION        = 1;
  ROLE_PREDICTION        = 2;
  ROLE_SENTINEL          = 3;
  ROLE_SOLVER            = 4;
  ROLE_VERIFIER          = 5;
  ROLE_GOVERNANCE        = 6;
  ROLE_FEATURE_EXTRACTOR = 7;
  ROLE_MEMORY            = 8;
  ROLE_ACTUATOR          = 9;
  ROLE_FRONTEND          = 10;  // spectral, reservoir — extract, don't decide
}

// ----- Perception ----------------------------------------------------
message PerceptionSignature {
  repeated Modality modalities                = 1;
  map<string, Shape> shapes                   = 2;  // modality → tensor shape
  map<string, SamplingRequirement> sampling   = 3;
}

enum Modality {
  MODALITY_UNSPECIFIED   = 0;
  MODALITY_DENSE_VECTOR  = 1;
  MODALITY_TEMPORAL_STREAM = 2;
  MODALITY_SPIKE_TRAIN   = 3;
  MODALITY_SYMBOL_GRAPH  = 4;
  MODALITY_WAVELET_COEFFS = 5;
  MODALITY_PHYSICAL_SENSOR = 6;
  MODALITY_HYPERVECTOR   = 7;
  MODALITY_TABULAR       = 8;
  MODALITY_TEXT          = 9;
  MODALITY_IMAGE         = 10;
}

message Shape {
  repeated int64 dims = 1;
  DataType dtype = 2;      // fp32, fp16, int8, binary, bool
}

message SamplingRequirement {
  float rate_hz      = 1;
  int64 window_len   = 2;
  bool  requires_sync = 3;
}

// ----- Emission ------------------------------------------------------
message EmissionSignature {
  OutputForm      output_form           = 1;
  Shape           shape                 = 2;
  UncertaintyKind uncertainty           = 3;
  Determinism     determinism           = 4;
  repeated string requires_justification_for = 5;  // action classes needing a token
}

enum OutputForm {
  OUTPUT_UNSPECIFIED       = 0;
  OUTPUT_HYPERVECTOR       = 1;
  OUTPUT_SPIKE_TRAIN       = 2;
  OUTPUT_ENERGY_GRADIENT   = 3;
  OUTPUT_SYMBOLIC_TRIPLE   = 4;
  OUTPUT_PROBABILITY_INTERVAL = 5;
  OUTPUT_SCALAR            = 6;
  OUTPUT_ASSIGNMENT_CERTIFICATE = 7;
  OUTPUT_FEATURE_VECTOR    = 8;
  OUTPUT_JUSTIFICATION_TOKEN = 9;  // v0.2: first-class
}

enum UncertaintyKind {
  UNCERTAINTY_UNSPECIFIED     = 0;
  UNCERTAINTY_NONE            = 1;
  UNCERTAINTY_CONFORMAL_INTERVAL = 2;
  UNCERTAINTY_ENSEMBLE_SPREAD = 3;
  UNCERTAINTY_ENERGY_LANDSCAPE = 4;
  UNCERTAINTY_SYMBOLIC_PROOF  = 5;
}

enum Determinism {
  DETERMINISM_UNSPECIFIED = 0;
  DETERMINISM_DETERMINISTIC = 1;
  DETERMINISM_SEEDED        = 2;
  DETERMINISM_STOCHASTIC    = 3;
}

// ----- Metabolic profile --------------------------------------------
message MetabolicProfile {
  float latency_p50_ms   = 1;
  float latency_p95_ms   = 2;
  float latency_p99_ms   = 3;
  float energy_per_inference_j = 4;
  float memory_footprint_mb    = 5;
  float memory_peak_mb         = 6;
  bool  requires_gradient_training = 7;
  bool  requires_gpu               = 8;
  HardwareRequirement requires_special_hardware = 9;
  int32 max_concurrent_invocations = 10;
  BudgetClass metabolic_budget_class = 11;
  ResourceVector declared_share       = 12;
}

message ResourceVector {
  float cpu_cores    = 1;
  float gpu_fraction = 2;
  float memory_mb    = 3;
  float network_mbps = 4;
  float energy_watts = 5;
}

enum HardwareRequirement {
  HW_UNSPECIFIED   = 0;
  HW_NONE          = 1;
  HW_NEUROMORPHIC  = 2;
  HW_EVENT_CAMERA  = 3;
  HW_PHYSICAL_SUBSTRATE = 4;
  HW_TPU_NPU       = 5;
}

enum BudgetClass {
  BUDGET_UNSPECIFIED = 0;
  BUDGET_LEAN        = 1;
  BUDGET_STANDARD    = 2;
  BUDGET_HEAVY       = 3;
  BUDGET_RESEARCH    = 4;
}

// ----- Failure -------------------------------------------------------
message FailureSignature {
  repeated FailureMode modes   = 1;
  HealthProbe probe            = 2;  // typed, callable
  RecoveryProcedure recovery   = 3;  // typed
}

message FailureMode {
  string name                = 1;
  string observable_signal   = 2;  // metric name or event topic
  DetectionMethod detection  = 3;
  DegradationKind expected_degradation = 4;
  float detection_latency_budget_ms    = 5;  // must detect within this
}

message DetectionMethod {
  oneof kind {
    ThresholdDetection threshold   = 1;
    StatisticalDetection stats     = 2;
    HeartbeatDetection heartbeat   = 3;
    BehavioralDetection behavior   = 4;  // e.g., anomaly rate over window
  }
}

enum DegradationKind {
  DEGRADE_UNSPECIFIED = 0;
  DEGRADE_SILENT_ACCURACY_LOSS = 1;
  DEGRADE_SPIKE_SILENCE        = 2;
  DEGRADE_ENERGY_DIVERGENCE    = 3;
  DEGRADE_SYMBOL_GROUNDING_FAILURE = 4;
  DEGRADE_CALIBRATION_DRIFT    = 5;
  DEGRADE_TIMEOUT              = 6;
  DEGRADE_CRASH                = 7;
}

message HealthProbe {
  oneof kind {
    HealthHttp     http     = 1;  // GET endpoint returning HealthReport
    HealthRpc      rpc      = 2;  // registered gRPC method
    HealthStream   stream   = 3;  // subscribe to health event topic
  }
}

message HealthHttp {
  string url               = 1;
  string expected_status   = 2;
  int32  timeout_ms        = 3;
}

message HealthRpc {
  string method_fqn        = 1;
  int32  timeout_ms        = 2;
}

message HealthStream {
  string topic             = 1;
  int32  expected_period_ms = 2;
  int32  stall_timeout_ms  = 3;
}

message RecoveryProcedure {
  RecoveryKind kind          = 1;
  int32 max_attempts         = 2;
  int32 backoff_ms_initial   = 3;
  float backoff_multiplier   = 4;
  int32 backoff_ms_max       = 5;
  repeated string preconditions = 6;
}

enum RecoveryKind {
  RECOVERY_UNSPECIFIED   = 0;
  RECOVERY_RESTART       = 1;
  RECOVERY_RESTORE_SNAPSHOT = 2;
  RECOVERY_DEMOTE        = 3;
  RECOVERY_HOT_SWAP      = 4;
  RECOVERY_MANUAL        = 5;
}

// ----- Safety --------------------------------------------------------
message SafetyDeclaration {
  ReliabilityTier reliability_tier           = 1;
  bool may_actuate                           = 2;
  bool requires_causal_certificate           = 3;
  bool requires_cross_check                  = 4;
  repeated string cross_check_eligible_roles = 5;  // roles, not module IDs
  MaxSafetyTier max_safety_tier              = 6;
}

enum ReliabilityTier {
  TIER_UNSPECIFIED              = 0;
  TIER_T0_DETERMINISTIC         = 1;
  TIER_T1_CALIBRATED_NONLEARNED = 2;
  TIER_T2_LEARNED_CALIBRATED    = 3;
  TIER_T3_LEARNED_UNCALIBRATED  = 4;
}

enum MaxSafetyTier {
  SAFETY_UNSPECIFIED = 0;
  SAFETY_RESEARCH    = 1;
  SAFETY_LOW         = 2;
  SAFETY_MEDIUM      = 3;
  SAFETY_HIGH        = 4;
  SAFETY_CRITICAL    = 5;
}

// ----- Capabilities (v0.2) ------------------------------------------
message CapabilityScopes {
  repeated string read_roles  = 1;
  repeated string write_roles = 2;
  repeated string read_tags   = 3;
  repeated string write_tags  = 4;
  bool can_emit_control_affecting = 5;
  repeated string allowed_channels = 6;
}

// ----- Channels (v0.2) ----------------------------------------------
message Channel {
  string channel_id              = 1;
  string producer_module         = 2;
  repeated string consumer_modules = 3;
  Modality payload_modality      = 4;
  Shape    payload_shape         = 5;
  Delivery delivery              = 6;
  bool     logged_for_trace      = 7;  // MUST be true if any consumer can actuate
}

enum Delivery {
  DELIVERY_UNSPECIFIED    = 0;
  DELIVERY_AT_MOST_ONCE   = 1;
  DELIVERY_AT_LEAST_ONCE  = 2;
  DELIVERY_EXACTLY_ONCE   = 3;
  DELIVERY_STREAMING      = 4;
}

// ----- Justification (v0.2) -----------------------------------------
message JustificationSpec {
  JustificationKind kind    = 1;
  string format             = 2;  // "smt-lib2", "typed-json:cert/v1", ...
  repeated string required_fields = 3;
  repeated ModuleRole checkable_by = 4;
}

enum JustificationKind {
  JUSTIFICATION_UNSPECIFIED    = 0;
  JUSTIFICATION_NONE           = 1;
  JUSTIFICATION_CAUSAL_CERT    = 2;
  JUSTIFICATION_KERNEL_BOUND   = 3;
  JUSTIFICATION_RULE_TRACE     = 4;
  JUSTIFICATION_ENERGY_CERT    = 5;
  JUSTIFICATION_SYMBOLIC_PROOF = 6;
}

// ----- Lifecycle -----------------------------------------------------
message Lifecycle {
  bool hot_swap_supported             = 1;
  bytes rollback_artifact_digest      = 2;
  bool shadow_validation_required     = 3;
  PromotionCriteria promotion         = 4;
  repeated RuntimeId disallowed_runtimes = 5;  // e.g., SNN on general CPU
}

message PromotionCriteria {
  int32 shadow_invocations_min           = 1;
  float max_incumbent_error_delta        = 2;
  repeated string required_gates         = 3;  // role-conditioned (see §2.8)
  ModuleRole approver_role               = 4;
  bool causal_review_required            = 5;
}

enum RuntimeId {
  RUNTIME_UNSPECIFIED  = 0;
  RUNTIME_CPU_GENERAL  = 1;
  RUNTIME_GPU_CUDA     = 2;
  RUNTIME_GPU_ROCM     = 3;
  RUNTIME_GPU_METAL    = 4;
  RUNTIME_NPU          = 5;
  RUNTIME_NEUROMORPHIC = 6;
  RUNTIME_WASM         = 7;
}
```

### A.2 Registry API

```proto
package organism.v1.registry;

import "organism/v1/contract.proto";

service SubstrateRegistry {
  rpc Register       (RegisterRequest)        returns (RegisterResponse);
  rpc Deregister     (DeregisterRequest)      returns (DeregisterResponse);
  rpc Query          (QueryRequest)           returns (QueryResponse);
  rpc GetContract    (ContractRequest)        returns (organism.v1.SubstrateContract);
  rpc Health         (HealthRequest)          returns (HealthReport);
  rpc Promote        (PromoteRequest)         returns (PromoteResponse);
  rpc HotSwap        (HotSwapRequest)         returns (HotSwapResponse);
  rpc ListVersions   (ListVersionsRequest)    returns (ListVersionsResponse);
  rpc DiffContracts  (DiffRequest)            returns (DiffResponse);
  rpc GetAuditLog    (AuditLogRequest)        returns (stream AuditLogEntry);
}

enum ModuleLifecycleState {
  LIFECYCLE_UNSPECIFIED      = 0;
  LIFECYCLE_PROPOSED         = 1;
  LIFECYCLE_SANDBOXED        = 2;
  LIFECYCLE_SHADOW_VALIDATED = 3;
  LIFECYCLE_EXPLORATION      = 4;
  LIFECYCLE_DELIBERATION     = 5;
  LIFECYCLE_DEPRECATED       = 6;
  LIFECYCLE_RETIRED          = 7;
}

message RegisterRequest {
  organism.v1.SubstrateContract contract = 1;
  bytes artifact_digest                  = 2;
  bytes author_signature                 = 3;
  repeated string promotion_evidence     = 4;  // opaque IDs, checked by registry
}

message RegisterResponse {
  string module_id                                = 1;
  ModuleLifecycleState initial_state              = 2;
  repeated ValidationIssue validation_issues      = 3;
}

message ValidationIssue {
  Severity severity   = 1;
  string code         = 2;
  string field_path   = 3;
  string message      = 4;
}

enum Severity { SEVERITY_UNSPECIFIED = 0; SEVERITY_WARNING = 1; SEVERITY_ERROR = 2; }

message DeregisterRequest {
  string module_id       = 1;
  DrainPolicy drain      = 2;  // IMMEDIATE, GRACEFUL, DRAIN_THEN_SWAP
  int32 drain_timeout_s  = 3;
}

message QueryRequest {
  CapabilityFilter filter = 1;
}

message CapabilityFilter {
  repeated organism.v1.ModuleRole roles                 = 1;
  organism.v1.ReliabilityTier min_reliability           = 2;
  organism.v1.MaxSafetyTier min_max_safety              = 3;
  organism.v1.HardwareRequirement max_hardware          = 4;
  organism.v1.BudgetClass max_budget                    = 5;
  repeated organism.v1.Modality input_modalities        = 6;
  organism.v1.OutputForm required_output                = 7;
  ModuleLifecycleState min_lifecycle_state              = 8;
}

message QueryResponse {
  repeated ModuleHandle handles = 1;
}

message ModuleHandle {
  string module_id                = 1;
  string version                  = 2;
  organism.v1.ModuleRole role     = 3;
  organism.v1.ReliabilityTier tier = 4;
  ModuleLifecycleState state      = 5;
}

message HealthReport {
  string module_id                = 1;
  HealthStatus status             = 2;
  float  score                    = 3;  // [0,1], module-defined semantics
  repeated FailureSignal active_failures = 4;
  google.protobuf.Timestamp observed_at = 5;
}

enum HealthStatus { HEALTH_UNSPECIFIED = 0; HEALTH_HEALTHY = 1; HEALTH_DEGRADED = 2; HEALTH_FAILED = 3; }

message FailureSignal {
  string failure_mode_name   = 1;
  float  observed_value      = 2;
  float  threshold           = 3;
  google.protobuf.Timestamp first_observed = 4;
}

message PromoteRequest {
  string module_id                                = 1;
  ModuleLifecycleState from_state                 = 2;
  ModuleLifecycleState to_state                   = 3;
  repeated ShadowValidationRecord shadow_records  = 4;
  bytes approver_signature                        = 5;
}

message ShadowValidationRecord {
  int32 invocations        = 1;
  float incumbent_error    = 2;
  float candidate_error    = 3;
  float p95_latency_ms     = 4;
  repeated string gates_passed = 5;
  bytes evidence_digest    = 6;
}

message HotSwapRequest {
  string module_id         = 1;
  string new_version       = 2;
  SwapStrategy strategy    = 3;
  float drain_timeout_s    = 4;
}

enum SwapStrategy { SWAP_UNSPECIFIED = 0; SWAP_BLUE_GREEN = 1; SWAP_CANARY_PERCENT = 2; SWAP_LIVE_DRAIN = 3; }

message AuditLogEntry {
  string event_type     = 1;  // REGISTER, PROMOTE, HOT_SWAP, DEREGISTER, HEALTH_FAIL
  string module_id      = 2;
  bytes  payload_digest = 3;
  google.protobuf.Timestamp at = 4;
}
```

**Registry invariants (enforce in the implementation, not just the doc):**

1. Registration rejects any contract with `safety.may_actuate = true` and `safety.reliability_tier = T3`.
2. Registration rejects any contract with `safety.may_actuate = true` and `justification.kind = NONE`.
3. Registration rejects any channel with `logged_for_trace = false` if any consumer has `may_actuate = true`.
4. Registration rejects any contract whose `capabilities.can_emit_control_affecting = true` unless `safety.requires_cross_check = true` OR `reliability_tier ∈ {T0, T1}`.
5. Promotion from `EXPLORATION` to `DELIBERATION` requires `shadow_records.invocations ≥ promotion.shadow_invocations_min` and all `required_gates` present.
6. The audit log is append-only and content-addressed; `GetAuditLog` streams from a fixed point for replay.

---

## 5. Artifact B — Degradation State Machine

This supersedes the prose in §8. It is the formal definition that must be implemented and tested.

### B.1 State variables

The machine's state is a tuple `(L, H, T_since)` where:

- `L ∈ {L0, L1, L2, L3, L4}` — current level.
- `H` — health signal vector (defined below).
- `T_since` — wall-clock time of last state transition (for hysteresis).

**Health signal vector `H`:**

| Signal | Symbol | Range | Source | Cadence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Per-module health | `h_m` | {HEALTHY, DEGRADED, FAILED} | Module health probe | telemetry window |
| Per-module conformal coverage | `c_m` | [0,1] | Rolling empirical coverage over last N decisions | consolidation |
| Thermal headroom | `thermal` | [0,1] | Platform sensor | telemetry window |
| Power headroom | `power` | [0,1] | Platform sensor | telemetry window |
| Memory pressure | `mem` | [0,1] | Workspace arena | telemetry window |
| Workspace query p95 | `q_p95` | ms | Workspace metrics | telemetry window |
| Workspace GC pressure | `gc` | [0,1] | Compaction fraction of hot tier | consolidation |
| Byzantine suspicion | `byz` | bool | Cross-check failure count > threshold | per decision |
| Trace writeable | `trace_ok` | bool | Decision Trace sink health | per write |
| Deterministic engine healthy | `det_ok` | bool | Rule engine health probe | telemetry window |
| Causal verifier available | `causal_ok` | bool | Verifier health probe | telemetry window |

**Thresholds (deployment config, defaults given):**

```
α            = 0.10   (conformal miscoverage target)
δ_1          = 0.05   (L1 calibration tolerance)
δ_2          = 0.15   (L2 calibration tolerance)
thermal_L1   = 0.20   (below → L1 candidate)
thermal_L0   = 0.50   (above → L0 recovery candidate)
q_p95_L3     = 50 ms  (above → L3 candidate)
q_p95_L4     = 250 ms (above → L4 candidate)
gc_L3        = 0.50
gc_L4        = 0.85
mem_L4       = 0.90
byz_count_L4 = 3      (consecutive cross-check failures)
```

### B.2 State definitions

Each state defines which modules are eligible for dispatch and what the safety posture is. The router's decision tree (§5 of v0.1) must be conditioned on `L`.

| Level | Eligible module roles | Actuation policy | Cross-check |
|---|---|---|---|
| **L0** | All DELIBERATION modules | Normal | Per contract |
| **L1** | All except thermal-throttled neural; reservoir + kernel lead temporal | Normal, shields widened by `2·δ_1` | Per contract |
| **L2** | Kernel, spectral, energy solver, HDC (retrieval), deterministic engine | Any actuation requires causal certificate | Mandatory for all actuation |
| **L3** | HDC retrieval + deterministic engine | Act only if retrieval similarity ≥ `s_L3` (default 0.85) | Mandatory; retrieval match itself is the certificate |
| **L4** | Deterministic engine only | Fixed conservative rules only | N/A (no learned module in loop) |

### B.3 Transition conditions (formal)

Let `confirmed(φ, T)` mean "φ has held continuously for at least T seconds." Let `sustained(φ, T)` mean the same. The distinction is only that `confirmed` is used for downward transitions (fast protection) and `sustained` for upward (slow trust).

```
Transition L0 → L1  iff
    det_ok ∧ trace_ok
    ∧ [ confirmed(thermal < thermal_L1, T_down[L1])
        ∨ confirmed(∀ m ∈ neural: c_m < 1 - α + δ_1, T_down[L1])
        ∨ confirmed(∃ m ∈ neural: h_m = DEGRADED, T_down[L1]) ]

Transition L1 → L0  iff
    sustained(thermal > thermal_L0, T_up[L1])
    ∧ sustained(∀ m ∈ neural: c_m ≥ 1 - α, T_up[L1])
    ∧ sustained(∀ m ∈ neural: h_m = HEALTHY, T_up[L1])

Transition L1 → L2  iff
    det_ok ∧ trace_ok
    ∧ [ confirmed(∀ m ∈ {neural, reservoir}: c_m < 1 - α + δ_2, T_down[L2])
        ∨ confirmed(∃ m ∈ leading_predictive: h_m = FAILED, T_down[L2])
        ∨ confirmed(¬causal_ok, T_down[L2]) ]   // can't certify actuation

Transition L2 → L1  iff
    sustained(∃ m ∈ {neural, reservoir}: c_m ≥ 1 - α, T_up[L2])
    ∧ sustained(∀ m ∈ {neural, reservoir}: h_m = HEALTHY, T_up[L2])
    ∧ sustained(causal_ok, T_up[L2])

Transition L2 → L3  iff
    det_ok ∧ trace_ok
    ∧ [ confirmed(∀ m ∈ predictive: h_m = FAILED ∨ c_m < 1 - α + δ_2, T_down[L3])
        ∨ confirmed(gc > gc_L3 ∨ q_p95 > q_p95_L3, T_down[L3]) ]

Transition L3 → L2  iff
    sustained(∃ m ∈ predictive: c_m ≥ 1 - α, T_up[L3])
    ∧ sustained(gc < gc_L3 ∧ q_p95 < q_p95_L3, T_up[L3])
    ∧ sustained(¬byz, T_up[L3])

Transition L3 → L4  iff
    confirmed(¬trace_ok, T_down[L4])
    ∨ confirmed(¬det_ok, T_down[L4])
    ∨ confirmed(gc > gc_L4 ∨ mem > mem_L4, T_down[L4])
    ∨ confirmed(q_p95 > q_p95_L4, T_down[L4])
    ∨ confirmed(byz_count ≥ byz_count_L4, T_down[L4])

Transition L4 → L3  iff
    sustained(trace_ok ∧ det_ok, T_up[L4])
    ∧ sustained(gc < gc_L3 ∧ mem < mem_L4, T_up[L4])
    ∧ sustained(q_p95 < q_p95_L3, T_up[L4])
    ∧ sustained(¬byz, T_up[L4])
    ∧ human_acknowledgment   // L4 recovery requires explicit approval by default
```

**Nested safety invariant (must be enforced):** a transition is rejected if the target state's required health signals are not *currently* satisfied. Hysteresis prevents flapping; the target-state preconditions prevent transitioning to a state the system cannot actually support.

### B.4 Hysteresis defaults

| Transition | `T_down` | `T_up` | `T_up / T_down` |
|---|---|---|---|
| L0 ↔ L1 | 5 s | 120 s | 24× |
| L1 ↔ L2 | 30 s | 300 s | 10× |
| L2 ↔ L3 | 60 s | 600 s | 10× |
| L3 ↔ L4 | 10 s | 900 s | 90× |

Rationale for the L3↔L4 asymmetry: L4 is the last refuge and is entered on the fastest trigger (10 s) but exited on the slowest (900 s + human ack) — the system is quick to protect and slow to trust itself again. This is what "hysteretic physiology" means operationally.

### B.5 Transition SLOs

Every transition has a hard latency SLO from the moment the trigger condition is first observed:

| Transition | SLO |
|---|---|
| L0→L1 | 1 telemetry window + 5 s |
| L1→L2 | 2 windows + 30 s |
| L2→L3 | 3 windows + 60 s |
| L3→L4 | 1 window + 10 s |

Missing an SLO is itself a fault-injection test failure and triggers a diagnosis event (see §7.2 of v0.1) independent of the transition's success.

### B.6 Fault-injection test matrix

**Mandatory.** A transition that has not been exercised under load is not a transition. Every cell in this matrix must be a CI test with a measured recovery time and a pass/fail assertion.

| Transition | Injection | Assertion |
|---|---|---|
| L0→L1 | Set neural module thermal > threshold | Level = L1 within SLO; Decision Trace records `trigger=thermal`, module IDs, and hysteresis window start |
| L0→L1 | Force `c_neural < 1 - α + δ_1` for `T_down[L1]` | Level = L1 within SLO; conformal coverage recomputed and logged |
| L1→L0 | Restore thermal + calibration to healthy | Level = L0 only after `T_up[L1]` sustained; no earlier transition |
| L1→L2 | Kill reservoir module | Level = L2 within SLO; router switches to kernel; no actuation without causal cert |
| L1→L2 | Corrupt causal verifier | Level = L2; all actuation blocked (can't certify) |
| L2→L1 | Restore reservoir + causal verifier | Level = L1 after `T_up[L2]`; verify no flapping (transition count within window ≤ 1) |
| L2→L3 | Saturate workspace GC pressure > `gc_L3` | Level = L3 within SLO; retrieval-only dispatch |
| L2→L3 | Fail all predictive modules | Level = L3 |
| L3→L2 | Restore one predictive module | Level = L2 after `T_up[L3]` |
| L3→L4 | Corrupt Decision Trace sink | Level = L4 within SLO (fast); all learned modules out of loop |
| L3→L4 | Inject 3 consecutive cross-check failures | Level = L4; `byz` latched |
| L3→L4 | Corrupt workspace hot tier | Level = L4; deterministic engine serves |
| L4→L3 | Restore all signals | Level = L3 only after `T_up[L4]` + human ack; verify ack is required (test fails without it) |
| L4→L4' | Rapid oscillation injection (alternate good/bad every 100 ms) | **No transition** — hysteresis holds; assert transition count = 0 |
| Any | Sudden power loss / kill -9 | On restart, level restored from last snapshot + trace replay; no skipped level |

**Assertions common to every test:**

1. Transition occurs within SLO.
2. Decision Trace contains the transition event with cause, trigger signals, and threshold values.
3. During the transition window, no control-affecting action bypassed the safety shield.
4. Recovery to L0 within declared recovery budget, without human intervention (except L4→L3).
5. No flapping — transition count within a 5-minute window after the event ≤ 1.
6. Snapshot was taken before any L2→L3 and L3→L4 transition (verify via snapshot store).

### B.7 Decision Trace entries for transitions

Every transition emits a trace entry of this shape, to be schema-defined in Artifact C:

```yaml
DegradationTraceEntry:
  entry_id: uuid
  at: datetime
  from_level: enum[L0..L4]
  to_level: enum[L0..L4]
  trigger:
    kind: enum[thermal, calibration, module_failed, workspace_pressure,
               byzantine, trace_unavailable, causal_unavailable, manual]
    evidence:
      signal_name: string
      observed_value: float
      threshold: float
      duration_observed_s: float
  hysteresis:
    window_down_s: float
    window_up_s: float      # present only for upward transitions
  active_modules_at_transition: [module_handle]
  demoted_modules: [module_handle]
  promoted_modules: [module_handle]
  workspace_snapshot_id: string | null   # required if transitioning down past L2
  safety_shield_interventions_during_window: int
  human_ack: {operator_id, at} | null    # required for L4→L3
```
