## 3. Corrections to Artifact E

Six corrections. None are structural. All can be applied in-place.

### E-1 — `justify()` returning `None` when contract declares non-NONE kind

§E.3 specifies `justify() -> JustificationToken | None`, but the contract declares `justification.kind`. If `kind != NONE` and the module returns `None`, that is a **contract violation on the emission path**, not a case for fallback logic downstream.

**Correction — §E.3:**

> If `contract.justification.kind != NONE` and `emission.output_form == OUTPUT_JUSTIFICATION_TOKEN` (i.e., the module is emitting an actuation-candidate), `justify()` returning `None` is a hard error. The SDK raises `JustificationMissing`. The runtime treats the emission as `verification_status = FAILED`, logs the violation, and the router treats the module as `h_m = DEGRADED` for the next health window (this is a failure mode, not a routing signal).

Defense in depth: the router independently verifies that a justification token is present when `justification_required != NONE` in the plan.

### E-2 — `PerceptionInput` multi-modality is unspecified

§E.3 has `handle(self, input: PerceptionInput)`, but the contract's `perception_signature.modalities` is a list. `PerceptionInput` must accommodate multiple modalities in one call.

**Correction — §E.3:**

```python
class PerceptionInput:
    modalities: dict[Modality, ModalityPayload]
    task_descriptor: TaskDescriptor
    workspace_reader: WorkspaceReader   # capability-scoped
    deadline_remaining_ms: float
```

A module that declares three modalities must handle all three present in `modalities` or raise `ModalityUnavailable` (which the router treats as a health signal, not a failure — the module may still be healthy for other modality combinations).

### E-3 — `on_drain()` hook

§E.3 says the SDK "handles graceful shutdown with drain," but the module may have in-flight state to checkpoint or buffers to flush. The drain sequence needs a module-facing hook.

**Correction — §E.3 add fifth method:**

```python
def on_drain(self, deadline: datetime) -> None: ...
```

Called by the SDK before the module's process is signalled for shutdown. The module must complete or abandon in-flight work by `deadline`. After `deadline`, the SDK may `SIGKILL`. The `on_drain` hook is optional (SDK provides a no-op default).

### E-4 — §E.4 step 8 is ambiguous and should be split

Step 8 reads: *"Corrupt one binding in the workspace hot tier → query-time checksum failure → quarantine → L4 transition (trace sink integrity check fails on the next consolidation)."*

Two distinct assertions are conflated:

1. **Binding corruption → quarantine → diagnosis event.** Per B.3, a single binding corruption does not trigger any level transition. It is a diagnosis event (`workspace_integrity_alarm`), not an L4 trigger.
2. **Trace sink corruption → L4.** Per B.3, `confirmed(¬trace_ok, T_down[L4])` triggers L4.

Conflating them means the CI test either (a) fails because binding corruption doesn't cause L4, or (b) passes only when trace sink corruption is *also* injected in a subsequent step, but the assertion is stated as one step. Both are wrong.

**Correction — §E.4 split into two steps:**

> **8a.** Corrupt one binding in the workspace hot tier → query-time checksum failure → binding quarantined → `workspace_integrity_alarm` diagnosis event emitted. **Assert level remains L0.** Trace entry records `trigger = workspace_pressure` (or a new `workspace_integrity` trigger; see E-6).
>
> **8b.** Corrupt Decision Trace sink → L4 within SLO. **Assert level = L4.** Deterministic engine serves.

This also clarifies the dependency: E.6 scenario 2 ("Corrupt one workspace binding") and scenario 5 ("Corrupt Decision Trace sink") are correctly separate, and E.4 should match.

### E-5 — E.6 scenario 1 needs to assert the full path (transient and persistent)

§E.6 scenario 1 reads: *"Kill learned module → L1 within 1 telemetry window + 5 s; router demotes; trace records `trigger=module_failed`."*

Per B.3, killing a module and leaving it dead produces **L0 → L1 (5 s) → L2 (30 s)**, because:
- L0 → L1 triggers on `h_m = DEGRADED` after `T_down[L1] = 5 s`.
- L1 → L2 triggers on `h_m = FAILED` after `T_down[L2] = 30 s`.

A test that asserts "L1 within SLO" and stops is under-specified: the system will autonomously transition to L2 30 s later, and the test does not verify this. Worse, if the test restores the module at any point after 30 s, it silently crosses L2 and the recovery assertion is testing the wrong path.

**Correction — §E.6 split scenario 1 into two:**

> **1a — transient failure.** Kill learned module, restore within `T_down[L2] = 30 s`. Assert: L1 within `1 window + 5 s`; **no** L2 transition while dead; L0 recovery after `T_up[L1] = 120 s` sustained. Assert `trigger = module_degraded` in trace (not `module_failed`).
>
> **1b — persistent failure.** Kill learned module, leave dead. Assert: L1 within `1 window + 5 s` (`trigger = module_degraded`); L2 within `T_down[L2] = 30 s` after L1 (`trigger = module_failed`); restore → L1 after `T_up[L2] = 300 s` sustained (`trigger = recovery`); L0 after `T_up[L1] = 120 s` sustained. Assert transition count over the full window ≤ 2 (one down, one up).

This is the only way to verify the two-step recovery path without false positives.

### E-6 — B.7 trigger enum needs `module_degraded` / `module_failed` distinction

B.7 defines `trigger.kind` with `module_failed` but not `module_degraded`. Since L0 → L1 uses the `DEGRADED` signal and L1 → L2 uses the `FAILED` signal (B.3), the trigger enum must distinguish them or the trace cannot tell which transition fired.

**Correction — B.7 trigger enum:**

> Replace `module_failed` with `module_degraded` and `module_failed`. Add `workspace_integrity` (for the E-4 8a case). Add `recovery` (for upward transitions when the recovery is what triggered the transition, distinct from `manual`).

Updated enum:
```
trigger.kind: enum[thermal, calibration, module_degraded, module_failed,
                   workspace_pressure, workspace_integrity,
                   byzantine, trace_unavailable, causal_unavailable,
                   recovery, manual]
```

The state machine implementation chooses the trigger kind by the **signal** that fired the transition, not by the transition direction. An upward transition triggered by sustained health is `recovery`; a downward transition triggered by a health signal is the signal's kind.

### E-7 — `trace_sink_override` missing from `CapabilityScopes` proto

Correction 1.9 requires `trace_sink_override` as a capability, but the `CapabilityScopes` message in Artifact A.1 does not contain this field. Add it.

**Correction — §A.1 `CapabilityScopes`:**

```proto
message CapabilityScopes {
  repeated string read_roles  = 1;
  repeated string write_roles = 2;
  repeated string read_tags   = 3;
  repeated string write_tags  = 4;
  bool can_emit_control_affecting = 5;
  repeated string allowed_channels = 6;
  bool can_override_trace_sink = 7;   // ADDED
}
```

Registry invariant (§A.2) gains: *"Registration rejects any contract with `can_override_trace_sink = true` unless `reliability_tier ∈ {T0, T1}`."* Rationale: overriding the trace sink is a trust-heavy capability — it can route audit records elsewhere. Only non-learned, calibrated modules may do this.

---

## 4. Artifact F — Test Harness and Fault-Injection Driver Specification v0.1

### F.1 Purpose and scope

The harness is the **executable enforcement of Axioms 6, 7, 9, and 10**. It turns the tables in B.6 and E.6 into automated, timed, replay-verified tests. A transition that is not in the harness is not tested, and a transition that is not tested is not proven.

**Scope:** single-device, v0.1. Cluster fault injection is deferred to v0.2 (it is orthogonal to the contract/workspace/router/degradation stack that F validates).

**Non-scope:** performance benchmarks (separate suite), security fuzzing (separate suite), long-soak stability (separate suite), distributed partition / Byzantine injection (v0.2). All four are explicitly listed in §F.9 so the harness does not over-claim.

### F.2 Harness architecture

The harness is a **scenario runner**, not a unit-test framework. It starts Organism in a controlled test mode, executes a scenario step-by-step, and asserts on state and timing.

```
┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                    SCENARIO RUNNER (Rust)                     │
│  ScenarioLoader → StepExecutor → AssertionEvaluator → Report │
└─────────┬──────────────────────────────┬──────────────────────┘
          │                              │
          ▼                              ▼
┌──────────────────┐          ┌──────────────────────────────┐
│  FAULT INJECTOR  │          │  OBSERVATION CHANNEL         │
│  (Rust)          │          │  · Level state               │
│  · kill, corrupt │          │  · Latest DispatchPlan       │
│  · thermal, GC   │          │  · Trace entries (streaming) │
│  · byzantine     │          │  · Health reports            │
└─────────┬────────┘          │  · Workspace metrics         │
          │                    └──────────────────────────────┘
          ▼                              ▲
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              ORGANISM UNDER TEST (test mode)                  │
│  · deterministic clock (driven by harness)                    │
│  · isolated workspace (tmpdir, WAL in tmpdir)                 │
│  · configurable hysteresis thresholds                         │
│  · test-mode registry (accepts unsigned modules with marker) │
│  · mock modules permitted                                     │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Test-mode overrides** (all disabled in production):

| Override | Purpose | Disabled by |
|---|---|---|
| Deterministic clock | Reproducible SLO measurement; `t` advances only on harness step | `ORG_TEST_MODE=1` required |
| Isolated data directory | Each scenario has its own `tmpdir` for WAL, snapshots | `ORG_TEST_MODE=1` required |
| Hysteresis override | Scenario-specified `T_down`/`T_up` values to compress test time | Only affects thresholds, not triggers |
| Registry unsigned-module allowance | Test modules without real signatures | `ORG_TEST_MODE=1` required |
| Mock modules | Deterministic fake modules for controlled failure injection | Registered as `TEST_ONLY` |
| Bypass calibration for T2 gate | Allows a stub learned module to enter T2 in tests | Recorded in trace as `test_bypass` |

**Critical:** every test-mode override writes a `TEST_MODE_ACTIVE` marker into the trace. Production traces must never contain this marker; the trace validator (a CI gate) rejects any production build that emits it.

### F.3 Scenario definition format

Scenarios are YAML documents. Schema:

```yaml
Scenario:
  id: string                    # e.g., "B1_kill_learned_transient"
  version: semver
  description: string
  category: enum[A, B, C, D, E, F, G, H, I]     # per §F.7
  preconditions:
    initial_level: L0 | L1 | L2 | L3 | L4
    required_modules: [module_id]
    workspace_state: clean | populated | saturated
    registry_state: [module lifecycle states]
  overrides:
    T_down: map[level_pair -> seconds]    # compress test time
    T_up:   map[level_pair -> seconds]
    telemetry_window_s: float
    s_L3: float
    gc_L3: float
    gc_L4: float
  steps:
    - id: string
      action: SubmitTask | InjectFault | WaitCondition | WaitDuration |
              AssertState | RestoreModule | DumpTrace | Snapshot | Replay
      args: object
      timeout_s: float
      on_timeout: fail | report
  final_assertions:
    - type: LevelTransition | PlanContent | TraceEntry | HealthSignal |
            SLO | ReplayClean | TransitionCount
      args: object
  cleanup:
    restore_all: bool
    destroy_workspace: bool
```

Every step has an `id` so assertions and reports can reference it. Every step has a `timeout_s` so scenarios cannot hang. `on_timeout` distinguishes "failing the scenario" from "recording a warning" — the latter is only used for diagnostic-only steps.

### F.4 Step actions

| Action | Args | Behavior |
|---|---|---|
| `SubmitTask` | `{task_descriptor, expected_disposition}` | Submits to the router; captures the returned plan for subsequent assertions |
| `InjectFault` | `{fault_type, target, params}` | Dispatches to the fault injector (§F.5) |
| `WaitCondition` | `{predicate, max_wait_s}` | Polls an observation channel until predicate is true or `max_wait_s` elapses |
| `WaitDuration` | `{duration_s}` | Advances the deterministic clock by `duration_s` and processes any pending events |
| `AssertState` | `{predicate, message}` | Immediate assertion; failing aborts the scenario |
| `RestoreModule` | `{module_id, version}` | Restores a killed module from the registry |
| `DumpTrace` | `{output_path, since}` | Writes the trace entries since `since` to disk for replay |
| `Snapshot` | `{label}` | Takes a workspace snapshot with the given label |
| `Replay` | `{snapshot_id, until_sequence, trace_path}` | Runs replay; aborts scenario on mismatch |

**Deterministic clock behavior:** in test mode, the control loop's `t` is not wall clock. It advances only on `WaitDuration` and on `WaitCondition` polling. This makes scenario timing exact rather than "approximately 5 seconds." SLO assertions are against the deterministic clock. Production wall-clock SLOs are validated by the performance suite (§F.9), not by this harness.

**Caveat:** the deterministic clock does not compress CPU time. A step that takes 30 CPU-seconds to compute a compaction still takes 30 CPU-seconds. The clock override compresses wall-clock hysteresis, not computation.

### F.5 Fault injection primitives

Each primitive is a Rust trait implementation. Faults are injected either into the running process (via the test-mode SDK hooks) or at the operating system level (process control, memory modification) with the harness's OS-level privileges.

| Primitive | Target | Mechanism | Determinism |
|---|---|---|---|
| `KillProcess` | module | SIGKILL | deterministic |
| `PauseProcess` | module | SIGSTOP; release via `ResumeProcess` | deterministic |
| `ResumeProcess` | module | SIGCONT | deterministic |
| `CorruptBinding` | workspace | test-mode hook in workspace write path; flip bits before fsync | deterministic |
| `CorruptWorkspaceMemory` | workspace | test-mode arena hook; flip specific offsets | deterministic |
| `CorruptOpsLogEntry` | ops log | test-mode SQLite hook; alter a row's checksum | deterministic |
| `CorruptSnapshot` | snapshot store | replace a chunk file's bytes | deterministic |
| `DelayHealthProbe` | module | test-mode health server hook; sleep before responding | deterministic |
| `FailHealthProbe` | module | test-mode health server hook; return error N times | deterministic |
| `OverrideSignal` | telemetry | test-mode signal bus hook; replace value | deterministic |
| `InjectByzantine` | module | test-mode emission hook; alter output deterministically | deterministic |
| `SaturateWorkspace` | workspace | test-mode write API; fill until GC pressure target | deterministic given target |
| `CorruptTraceSink` | trace sink | test-mode trace writer; return error N times or fail entirely | deterministic |
| `KillCausalVerifier` | verifier | SIGKILL, same as `KillProcess` but typed for the scenario language | deterministic |
| `SetGCPressure` | workspace | test-mode workspace hook; force `gc` metric | deterministic |
| `SetQ95` | workspace | test-mode workspace hook; force query p95 metric | deterministic |

**Composition:** faults are composed by sequencing `InjectFault` steps with `WaitCondition` and `WaitDuration` between them. There is no compound fault primitive; composition is the scenario author's responsibility and is auditable as a sequence of primitives.

**Rapid oscillation** (§F.7 category F) uses a dedicated primitive:

| `OscillateFault` | any | alternate between two states every `period_ms` for `duration_s` | deterministic |

`OscillateFault` is used to test no-flapping; the scenario asserts that the level transition count remains at or below the expected number (typically 0 or 1) despite the oscillation.

### F.6 Timing measurement and SLO assertions

**Clock source:** `CLOCK_MONOTONIC` in the runtime, exposed to the harness through the observation channel. In test mode, this is the deterministic clock (§F.4).

**Measurement points:**

| Timestamp | Measured at |
|---|---|
| `t_trigger` | When the fault injector completes the injection **and** the corresponding signal is observable on the observation channel. For faults that produce signals (e.g., `KillProcess` → health probe fails), `t_trigger` is when the first probe failure is observed. For faults that are state changes (e.g., `SetGCPressure`), `t_trigger` is when the workspace's `gc` metric crosses the configured threshold. |
| `t_transition_committed` | When the degradation state machine commits the transition, recorded in the `DegradationTraceEntry.at`. |
| `t_settled` | When the post-transition state has held for one full `telemetry_window_s`. |

**SLO assertions use `t_transition_committed - t_trigger`.** The SLO values are from B.5. Tolerance is a scenario-level config (`slo_tolerance_factor`, default 1.0). CI runs with tolerance 1.0; investigation runs may loosen to 1.1 to distinguish "just missed" from "actually broken."

**Report format:** every scenario emits a JSON report:

```yaml
ScenarioReport:
  scenario_id: string
  status: PASS | FAIL | ERROR | TIMEOUT
  started_at: datetime
  ended_at: datetime
  steps:
    - id: string
      status: PASS | FAIL | SKIP
      started_at: datetime
      ended_at: datetime
      detail: string
  assertions:
    - type: string
      status: PASS | FAIL
      expected: string
      actual: string
      elapsed_s: float | null      # for SLO assertions
      slo_s: float | null
  replay:
    status: PASS | FAIL
    mismatches: [ReplayMismatch]
  trace_dump_path: string | null
  test_mode_overrides: map
```

The report is machine-readable and feeds CI. The trace dump path is preserved as a CI artifact for post-mortem.

### F.7 Scenario catalog (mandatory, v0.1)

Each entry becomes a CI test. Every scenario in A–G and I is **blocking**. Scenarios in H are blocking unless marked (warn) — those are timing-sensitive and may be flaky on shared CI; they are still required, but a 2-week flake window is granted before they become hard-blocking.

**Category A — Bootstrap and happy path**

| ID | Scenario | Assertions |
|---|---|---|
| A1 | Register `deterministic_engine` and `learned_module` | Both reach `DELIBERATION`; contracts validate; audit log entries recorded |
| A2 | Submit `pattern_recognition` task at `medium` | Plan type = `DispatchPlan`; primary = `learned_module`; trace entry present |
| A3 | Submit `control_actuation` task at `high` | Plan type = `DispatchPlan`; primary = `deterministic_engine`; cross_check not required (T0 proposer) |
| A4 | Snapshot → replay A1–A3 | Zero mismatches |

**Category B — L0 ↔ L1 transitions**

| ID | Scenario | Assertions |
|---|---|---|
| B1 | `FailHealthProbe(learned_module)` for 2 windows, restore before `T_down[L2]` | L1 within SLO; **no** L2 transition; `trigger = module_degraded` |
| B2 | Restore `learned_module` after B1 | L0 only after `T_up[L1]` sustained; `trigger = recovery` |
| B3 | `OverrideSignal(calibration_coverage, 0.80)` for `T_down[L1]` | L1 within SLO; `trigger = calibration` |
| B4 | Restore calibration to 0.95 | L0 after `T_up[L1]` sustained |

**Category C — L1 ↔ L2 transitions**

| ID | Scenario | Assertions |
|---|---|---|
| C1 | `KillProcess(learned_module)` persistent | L1 within SLO (`module_degraded`); L2 within `T_down[L2]` after L1 (`module_failed`) |
| C2 | Restore `learned_module` after C1 | L1 after `T_up[L2]` sustained; L0 after `T_up[L1]` sustained; total transitions ≤ 2 |
| C3 | At L1, `KillCausalVerifier` | L2 within SLO; `trigger = causal_unavailable` |
| C4 | Restore causal verifier | L1 after `T_up[L2]` |

**Category D — L2 ↔ L3 transitions**

| ID | Scenario | Assertions |
|---|---|---|
| D1 | At L2, `SetGCPressure(0.55)` | L3 within SLO; `trigger = workspace_pressure`; snapshot taken before transition |
| D2 | Restore GC pressure | L2 after `T_up[L3]` |
| D3 | At L2, `KillProcess` all predictive modules | L3 within SLO |
| D4 | At L3, `SubmitTask(control_actuation, high)` | `DegradedDispatchPlan` with `disposition = HOLD` if no retrieval match; or `DISPATCH` with `retrieval_similarity ≥ s_L3` |

**Category E — L3 ↔ L4 transitions**

| ID | Scenario | Assertions |
|---|---|---|
| E1 | At L3, `CorruptTraceSink` | L4 within SLO (fast); `trigger = trace_unavailable`; deterministic engine serves |
| E2 | Restore trace sink, submit `human_ack`, wait `T_up[L4]` | L3 after `T_up[L4]` + human ack; without ack, remains L4 (verified by E2b) |
| E2b | Restore trace sink but no `human_ack` | Remains L4 after `T_up[L4]`; human ack required |
| E3 | At L3, `InjectByzantine` ×3 consecutive cross-check failures | L4; `byz` latched; `trigger = byzantine` |
| E4 | At L3, `CorruptBinding` on a hot-path binding | `workspace_integrity_alarm` diagnosis event; **level remains L3** (corrected per E-4) |

**Category F — No-flapping**

| ID | Scenario | Assertions |
|---|---|---|
| F1 | `OscillateFault(FailHealthProbe, period=100ms, duration=60s)` | Transition count = 0 (hysteresis holds) |
| F2 | Kill/restore `learned_module` 10× in 60 s | Transition count ≤ 2; final level L0 after settle |
| F3 | Oscillate GC pressure around `gc_L3` | Transition count ≤ 2 over the window |

**Category G — Replay and integrity**

| ID | Scenario | Assertions |
|---|---|---|
| G1 | Every A–F scenario | Replay clean (zero mismatches) |
| G2 | `CorruptSnapshot(snapshot_id)` then load | Load rejects via `merkle_root` mismatch |
| G3 | `CorruptOpsLogEntry(seq)` then replay | Replay aborts with `ReplayMismatch` at that sequence |
| G4 | Load 8k-dim snapshot into 10k-dim workspace | Rejects with `dimension_mismatch` |
| G5 | `CorruptBinding` in hot tier → next query | Binding quarantined; query returns without it; trace logs quarantine |

**Category H — Backpressure and resource**

| ID | Scenario | Assertions |
|---|---|---|
| H1 | Fill workspace to `full_tier_max` with unpinned bindings | Lowest-salience evicted; no `workspace_backpressure` yet |
| H2 | Fill workspace to `full_tier_max` with pinned bindings | `workspace_backpressure` emitted; write rejected; diagnosis event |
| H3 | Fill `pinned_cap` + 1 | `workspace_pinned_overload`; `workspace_backpressure` |
| H4 | `KillProcess` mid-`write_batch` (warn) | Recovery from ops log reconstructs; batch is either fully applied up to fsync point or not at all |

**Category I — Policy and registry**

| ID | Scenario | Assertions |
|---|---|---|
| I1 | Activate unsigned router policy | Router enters `HOLD`; `policy_unavailable` emitted |
| I2 | Activate `RETIRED` policy | Router enters `HOLD` |
| I3 | Activate `DEPRECATED` policy, wait `deprecation_grace_period` | `policy_deprecation_warning` in every plan; `policy_deprecation_expired` after grace |
| I4 | Hot-swap `deterministic_engine` to v2 | v2 serves; v1 drains; in-flight preserved |
| I5 | Hot-swap to a failing v2, detect failure | Rollback to v1 within SLO; trace records rollback |
| I6 | Register a T3 module with `may_actuate = true` | Registration rejected (contract invariant) |
| I7 | Register a module with `can_override_trace_sink = true` and `reliability_tier = T3` | Registration rejected (E-7 invariant) |
| I8 | Module emits `Emission` without `JustificationToken` when `justification.kind = RULE_TRACE` | `JustificationMissing`; emission treated as `verification_status = FAILED`; `h_m = DEGRADED` (E-1) |

### F.8 CI integration

**PR gate (fast — category A, B, F):** ~5 minutes on the reference hardware. Catches the common failure modes and the no-flapping property.

**Merge gate (full — all categories):** ~30 minutes. Runs on every merge to `main`.

**Nightly gate:** full suite on three configurations (x86_64 only, x86_64 + GPU, ARM64) to catch hardware-specific regressions.

**Reporting:**
- JUnit XML for CI-native display.
- JSON report per scenario, archived as CI artifacts.
- Trace dumps per scenario, archived for 30 days, replayable offline.
- Timing trend graph (per-scenario `t_transition_committed - t_trigger`) over the last 30 nightly runs. A monotone upward trend is a warning even if SLOs pass — it means hysteresis margins are quietly shrinking.

**Flakiness policy:** a scenario that passes on retry is **FAIL**, not pass. Flakiness indicates non-determinism in the harness, the test-mode overrides, or the code under test. The policy is enforced by running each scenario exactly once per CI run. Retries are only permitted in nightly runs where hardware jitter is expected, and a `RETRY_PASS` status is a distinct, warning-level outcome that still surfaces to the team.

**Determinism verification:** the CI runs every scenario **twice** in the same CI job and asserts byte-identical `ScenarioReport` bodies (modulo timestamps and trace dump paths). This is the enforcement of Axiom 6 at the scenario level.

### F.9 What this harness does NOT test (explicit non-claims)

The following are deliberately out of scope, and the harness must not be cited as evidence for them:

- **Distributed Organism** (cluster, BFT, state replication, clock sync) — v0.2.
- **Metamodel synthesis** (hypernetwork, NAS, AutoML pipelines, promotion path) — v0.2.
- **Physical/molecular substrates** — research tier, separate harness.
- **SNN sentinel on neuromorphic hardware** — hardware-dependent, separate harness.
- **Realistic workload performance** — separate performance suite with representative mining/scheduling workloads. SLOs measured here are for the substrate's own operations (query, transition, replay), not for end-to-end application throughput.
- **Security and adversarial inputs** — separate fuzzing and red-team suite. The harness's `InjectByzantine` primitive is a fault for the *degradation* path, not a security test.
- **Long-running stability** — separate soak test (72h+). The harness compresses test time via hysteresis override; soak tests do not.
- **Behavior under real hardware faults** (ECC errors, PCIe resets, thermal shutdown) — separate hardware-in-the-loop suite.
- **Concurrency at scale** — v0.1 supports single-producer `write_batch`; multi-writer contention is v0.2.

Any team reporting "Organism passes its tests" must qualify which harness, and the correct statement is "passes the F harness for the v0.1 scope."

### F.10 Harness invariants and CI tests for the harness itself

The harness is code; it must be tested.

| Harness invariant | Test |
|---|---|
| Deterministic clock produces identical scenario outcomes across runs | Run F-category scenarios 100× with fixed seed; all reports identical |
| Test-mode overrides never leak into production traces | Scan a production build's trace output for `TEST_MODE_ACTIVE`; must be absent |
| Fault injection primitives produce idempotent effects when injected twice | `InjectFault(KillProcess, m)` twice → module is dead once |
| SLO assertions tolerate exactly `slo_tolerance_factor` | Construct a scenario with a known-5s transition; assert pass at tolerance 1.1, fail at 1.0 |
| Replay verification catches a single bit flip | Corrupt one binding mid-scenario; replay must fail |
| Scenario timeout is enforced | Scenario with `WaitCondition(max_wait_s=10)` on an unsatisfiable predicate fails after 10s, not hangs |
| Scenario cleanup always executes | Inject a failure in step 3; assert steps 4+ skipped but cleanup ran |
| Trace dumps are replayable offline | Replay a dumped trace without the original workspace; must succeed from the snapshot referenced in the trace |

### F.11 Repo additions for the harness

```
organism/
├── harness/                      # NEW
│   ├── runner/                   # Rust: scenario runner, clock, reporters
│   ├── injector/                 # Rust: fault injection primitives
│   ├── observer/                 # Rust: observation channel, trace streaming
│   ├── assertions/               # Rust: assertion evaluators
│   └── scenarios/                # YAML scenario definitions
│       ├── A_bootstrap/
│       ├── B_L0_L1/
│       ├── C_L1_L2/
│       ├── D_L2_L3/
│       ├── E_L3_L4/
│       ├── F_no_flapping/
│       ├── G_replay_integrity/
│       ├── H_backpressure/
│       └── I_policy_registry/
├── tests/
│   └── harness/                  # F.10 harness self-tests
└── ci/
    └── harness_gates.yaml        # PR / merge / nightly gate configurations
```

### F.12 Milestones for the harness

The harness should be built incrementally, gated by the modules it tests.

| Milestone | Deliverable | Exit |
|---|---|---|
| H1 | Runner + clock + observer + reporter skeleton; scenario A1 runs | A1 passes |
| H2 | Category A + B scenarios; fault injector for `FailHealthProbe`, `OverrideSignal` | A–B green |
| H3 | Category C + D; add `KillProcess`, `KillCausalVerifier`, `SetGCPressure` | A–D green |
| H4 | Category E; add `CorruptTraceSink`, `InjectByzantine`, `CorruptBinding` | A–E green |
| H5 | Category F + G; add `OscillateFault`, snapshot/replay integration | A–G green |
| H6 | Category H + I; add `CorruptOpsLogEntry`, `CorruptSnapshot`, `SaturateWorkspace` | A–I green |
| H7 | Harness self-tests (F.10); CI gate wiring; nightly trending | F.10 green; CI wired |

### F.13 Why the harness is where the spec proves itself

Everything up to E is *declarative*: the contract says this, the state machine says that, the workspace must do the other thing. F is the first artifact that has to *execute* those declarations against a real implementation and produce a pass/fail that a human can trust.

Two things about F are non-negotiable:

1. **The scenario catalog is the completeness criterion for v0.1.** A behavior not in the catalog is not verified. If, during implementation, a team discovers a behavior that should be verified but is not in F.7, that behavior is added to F.7 before it is added to the code. The catalog is not a suggestion.

2. **The harness's non-claims (§F.9) are as important as its claims.** A "green" harness is evidence for the v0.1 scope only. The team must be disciplined about this in reporting, or "green harness" becomes a meaningless slogan.
