## 5. A correction that Addendum 2 must fix in Artifact B

**The calibration trigger inequality is sign-reversed in B.3.**

Current text: `c_m < 1 - α + δ_1` with `α = 0.10`, `δ_1 = 0.05`.

This reads: trigger when coverage < 0.95. A well-calibrated module at α=0.10 achieves empirical coverage around 0.90 (that is what the guarantee means — *at least* 0.90, with tolerance for sampling noise pushing it to 0.91–0.94). If a module's steady-state empirical coverage is 0.92, the trigger fires immediately, at nominal health. If it is 0.94, the trigger fires on any downward fluctuation of 1 percentage point — well within sampling noise at 10,000 windows.

The trigger as written is unusable. It fires on normal operation.

Correct formulations:

- **Warning (L0→L1):** `c_m < 1 - α - δ_1` (coverage drops to 0.85 with δ_1 = 0.05)
- **Critical (L1→L2):** `c_m < 1 - α - δ_2` (coverage drops to 0.75 with δ_2 = 0.15)

The subtraction direction is the only one that makes the triggers meaningful: coverage must actually degrade *below* the guarantee for the trigger to be a degradation signal. The `δ` values are then "the margin by which coverage must fall below the guarantee to fire," not "tolerated coverage above the guarantee."

The same sign error appears in the L1→L2 trigger (also `+δ_2`) and in the `c_m < 1 - α + δ_1` reference in the initial B.7 draft. Addendum 2 will state the correct formulas and I will propose the B.3 edit as an amendment to Addendum 0 (which you own). Since it's a formula correction and not a re-opening of the state machine's *conditions*, it belongs in Addendum 0 as a "B.3 sign correction" clause, and I flag it here so it is not missed.

The alternative — treating δ as an early-warning margin above the guarantee — is defensible in principle but unstable in practice, because it requires the module's steady-state empirical coverage to be known and stable, which is exactly what a calibration trigger exists to detect the absence of. `c_m < 1 - α - δ` is the only formulation that self-stabilizes.

---

# Addendum 1 — Rule-Trace Format `typed-json:rule-trace/v1`

## A1.1 Purpose

`JustificationKind.RULE_TRACE` is emitted by the deterministic engine (§6.11 of v0.1) and by any other T0/T1 governance module whose justification is a rule-chain execution. The token must be:

- **Machine-verifiable** — a cross-checker can independently recompute the fired rule from the recorded inputs.
- **Human-readable** — an operator can understand, from the trace alone, why the engine acted.
- **Deterministic** — same `(Rulebook, input)` produces a byte-identical trace modulo `trace_id`, `emitted_at`, and `signature`.
- **Bounded** — no unbounded recursion, no user-defined functions, no time-of-day reads in predicates.
- **Content-addressed** — `trace_id` is a digest of the trace body, so the token is self-verifying.

## A1.2 The `RuleTrace` object

```yaml
RuleTrace:
  format: "typed-json:rule-trace/v1"
  trace_id: sha256                       # = SHA-256(canonical body minus trace_id & signature)
  emitted_at: datetime
  engine:
    module_id: string
    module_version: semver
    contract_digest: sha256
    rulebook_id: string
    rulebook_version: semver
    rulebook_digest: sha256              # must match the registered rulebook
  input:
    task_descriptor_digest: sha256
    workspace_snapshot_id: sha256
    workspace_bindings_read:
      - binding_id: uuid
        binding_checksum: sha256         # the checksum observed at read time
        at_sequence: uint64              # ops-log sequence for replay
    signals_observed:
      - signal_name: string
        value: { kind: int64 | bool | string, value: <typed> }
        source: string
        observed_at: datetime
  evaluation:
    rule_fired: string                   # rule_id
    rule_chain: [string]                 # ordered rule_ids, fired rule last
    predicate_satisfied: string          # the boolean expression, in DSL syntax
    intermediate_results:                # one per rule in the chain
      - rule_id: string
        predicate_truth: bool
        signal_values_used: [string]
  output:
    action: ActionDescriptor             # matches the module's declared action schema
    confidence: { kind: FIXED_POINT_1E6, value: int64 }   # T0 is always 1000000
    constraints_checked: [string]        # constraint IDs, must be a subset of the rulebook's declared constraints
  signature:
    algorithm: "ed25519"
    key_id: string
    signature: bytes
```

`ActionDescriptor`, `workspace_snapshot_id`, and `workspace_bindings_read[].at_sequence` are the pieces that make replay possible. The cross-checker reads the same snapshot, re-reads the same bindings, verifies each `binding_checksum`, replays the rule evaluation, and compares the recomputed `rule_fired` to `evaluation.rule_fired`.

## A1.3 The `Rulebook` object

```yaml
Rulebook:
  rulebook_id: string
  version: semver
  digest: sha256
  declared_constraints: [string]         # constraint IDs this rulebook is allowed to cite
  predicates_allowed: [DSL_OP]           # subset of the DSL this rulebook uses
  rules:
    - rule_id: string
      priority: int                      # lower fires first
      precondition: string               # DSL expression
      action: ActionDescriptor
      constraints: [string]
      documentation: string              # human-readable, ≤ 200 chars
```

The rulebook is a registered artifact in the same registry as modules. Its lifecycle state follows the same ladder (`PROPOSED → … → DELIBERATION → … → RETIRED`). A T0 module's contract references `rulebook_digest`, and the registry rejects registration if the referenced rulebook is not in `DELIBERATION`.

## A1.4 Predicate DSL

A closed, typed, side-effect-free expression language. Operators:

| Operator | Semantics | Types |
|---|---|---|
| `AND(a, b, …)`, `OR(a, b, …)`, `NOT(a)` | Boolean connectives | bool |
| `EQ(a, b)`, `NEQ(a, b)` | Equality | any matching |
| `LT(a, b)`, `LE(a, b)`, `GT(a, b)`, `GE(a, b)` | Ordering | int64, float (quantized) |
| `IN(a, [v1, v2, …])` | Set membership | any, declared enum |
| `BETWEEN(a, lo, hi)` | Inclusive range | int64, float |
| `HAS_TAG(binding_id, tag)` | Workspace binding has tag | binding, string |
| `BINDING_EXISTS(pattern)` | Any binding matching pattern exists | pattern |
| `SIGNAL(name)` | Value of a declared observed signal | string → typed value |
| `DEADLINE_REMAINING()` | Milliseconds to task deadline | → int64 |
| `LEVEL_IN([L0, L1, …])` | Current degradation level | → bool |

**Prohibited constructs** (rejected at rulebook registration):

- Recursion, iteration, user-defined functions
- Time-of-day or wall-clock reads (all time values come from `input.signals_observed`)
- Floating point arithmetic. All numeric values are quantized to int64 at scale 10⁶ before comparison; predicate text uses integer literals.
- Assignment, mutation, or any state change
- String interpolation, regex, or any non-`EQ`/`IN`/`HAS_TAG` string operation

**Size caps** (registry invariants):

- ≤ 256 rules per rulebook
- ≤ 16 rules in a `rule_chain` (bounded depth)
- ≤ 1 KB per `precondition` text
- ≤ 64 `signals_observed` per trace

## A1.5 Determinism guarantees

Given the same `(Rulebook, input.workspace_snapshot_id, input.signals_observed, input.workspace_bindings_read)`:

1. The `rule_chain` is identical.
2. The `predicate_satisfied` text is identical.
3. The `output.action` is identical.
4. `trace_id` is identical.
5. `intermediate_results` are identical.

The only fields that vary across replay are `trace_id`, `emitted_at`, and `signature` — and `trace_id` varies only because it is derived from `emitted_at` if `emitted_at` is part of the canonical body. **Correction:** `emitted_at` must be *excluded* from the `trace_id` payload so that replay produces the same `trace_id`. `emitted_at` is included in the signed body but not in the digests. This is a small but decisive rule; without it, replay of a rule trace produces a different `trace_id` and cross-check fails for a reason that has nothing to do with the engine.

## A1.6 Verification protocol

The cross-checker (a `VERIFIER` role, T0 or T1) executes:

```
verify_rule_trace(trace, registry, workspace_snapshot):
  1. Load rulebook by trace.engine.rulebook_digest from registry
     - reject if missing or lifecycle < DELIBERATION
     - reject if rulebook.digest != trace.engine.rulebook_digest
  2. Verify trace.engine.contract_digest matches the engine's registered contract
  3. Load workspace_snapshot; verify its checksum
  4. For each binding in trace.input.workspace_bindings_read:
       - read from snapshot; compare checksum to trace's recorded checksum
       - reject on mismatch
  5. Re-evaluate trace.evaluation.rule_chain against the recorded signals:
       - for each rule in the chain, recompute predicate_truth
       - reject if any intermediate_results.predicate_truth != recomputed
  6. Recompute rule_fired; compare to trace.evaluation.rule_fired
  7. Verify trace.output.constraints_checked ⊆ rule_fired.constraints
     and ⊆ rulebook.declared_constraints
  8. Verify trace.signature with engine's registered key
  9. Compute trace_id from body (excluding trace_id, emitted_at, signature); compare
  10. Return VERIFIED | REJECTED(reason_code)
```

The cross-checker's own emission carries the verification result as an `output_form = OUTPUT_JUSTIFICATION_TOKEN` emission whose own justification is `RULE_TRACE` (the verifier's rulebook). This makes the verification itself auditable, and the chain bottoms out at the T0 verifier whose rulebook is human-authored.

---

# Addendum 2 — Conformal Calibration Protocol

## A2.1 Purpose and scope

Defines how a T2 learned module's **empirical coverage** `c_m` is measured and reported for the B.3 calibration trigger, what the calibration dataset must be, what the calibration certificate must contain, and what happens when calibration degrades or its preconditions stop holding.

A module is T2 **only for the action classes it has a valid calibration certificate for**. All other action classes are T3 by construction. This is the honest boundary: no conformal guarantee, no T2 claim.

## A2.2 Notation and the sign correction

| Symbol | Meaning | Default |
|---|---|---|
| `α` | Target miscoverage rate (declared per module) | 0.10 |
| `1 − α` | Target coverage | 0.90 |
| `c_m` | **Empirical** coverage over the rolling window (see A2.5) | — |
| `δ_1` | Warning margin below the guarantee (L0→L1 trigger) | 0.05 |
| `δ_2` | Critical margin below the guarantee (L1→L2 trigger) | 0.15 |
| `δ_recovery` | Below which T2 is demoted to T3 (not a level transition) | 0.15 |
| `N_cal` | Rolling-window size for empirical coverage | 10,000 |

**Corrected B.3 calibration triggers:**

- **L0→L1 (warning):** `c_m < 1 − α − δ_1` (default: `c_m < 0.85`)
- **L1→L2 (critical):** `c_m < 1 − α − δ_2` (default: `c_m < 0.75`)

This replaces the sign-reversed `c_m < 1 − α + δ_k` in the current B.3. The correction is not optional; the current formula fires on normal operation, as explained in §5 of the review above.

**T3 demotion (not a level transition):** if `c_m < 1 − α − δ_recovery` **sustained over two consolidation cycles**, the registry demotes the module from T2 to T3. This removes it from `high` safety-tier tasks unconditionally. The demotion is a registry lifecycle action, not a degradation level change, and does not affect the L0–L4 state machine. It is reversible only via recalibration (A2.7).

## A2.3 Calibration dataset

The module declares in its contract:

```yaml
CalibrationDeclaration:
  dataset_id: string
  dataset_digest: sha256
  training_dataset_digest: sha256      # for disjointness check
  sampling_window: {start: datetime, end: datetime}
  sampling_distribution: string        # human-readable description
  provenance_attestation: bytes        # signed by the data source
  split:
    calibration_fraction: float        # e.g., 0.7
    validation_fraction: float         # e.g., 0.3
```

**Registry invariants:**

1. `dataset_digest` and `training_dataset_digest` must be from disjoint datasets. The registry rejects registration if the datasets' `sampling_window`s overlap and the datasets share more than 1% of record hashes. The 1% tolerance exists because of legitimate near-duplicates; anything more is leakage.
2. The `provenance_attestation` must be signed by a key whose public identity is registered in the deployment's data-source trust store. This is the mechanism that makes "the module was calibrated against the declared dataset" a verifiable claim rather than a declarative one.
3. The dataset must be registered as an artifact in the registry (same lifecycle ladder) before the module can reference it.
4. `split.calibration_fraction + split.validation_fraction = 1.0`.

## A2.4 Offline calibration at registration

Split conformal procedure:

1. Compute nonconformity scores `s(x_i, y_i)` for all `(x_i, y_i)` in the calibration split. The scoring function is declared in the module contract.
2. Compute `q̂` as the `⌈(n+1)(1−α)⌉/n`-th empirical quantile of the scores, where `n` is the calibration split size. This is the standard split-conformal quantile that gives the coverage guarantee.
3. Apply the interval rule to the validation split, compute empirical coverage on the validation split.
4. **Registration gates** (all must pass):
   - `n ≥ 1000` (smaller calibration sets give coverage guarantees too loose to be meaningful)
   - Validation coverage ≥ `1 − α − tolerance` (tolerance default 0.02)
   - Interval width is bounded (`width ≤ max_width` declared in the contract); if the intervals are unbounded, coverage is trivially satisfied and the module is useless. The `max_width` gate is what prevents a degenerate calibration from being registered.
5. If gates pass, issue the `CalibrationCertificate` (A2.6). If they fail, the module may register only as T3.

## A2.5 Online coverage measurement

Maintain a rolling window `W` of the last `N_cal` predictions with their eventual ground truths. Empirical coverage:

```
c_m = (1 / |W_verified|) · Σ_{i ∈ W_verified} I(y_i ∈ interval_i)
```

where `W_verified` is the subset of `W` whose ground truths have arrived.

**Ground truth latency.** The module declares `ground_truth_latency_ms` per action class. This is the expected delay between prediction and verifiable outcome. For action classes with `ground_truth_available = false` (A2.6 below), no ground truth arrives and the class is excluded from `W_verified` entirely.

**Consequence: `c_m` lags by `ground_truth_latency_ms`.** If that latency is 10 minutes, `c_m` reflects the coverage of predictions made 10 minutes ago, not now. The module must declare this, and the calibration trigger fires on the *lagged* coverage — this is a known limitation. The alternative (compute coverage on unverified predictions) is not possible; the alternative (shorten the latency by asking the module to predict its own error) reintroduces black-box opacity. The lagged measurement is the honest option.

**Regime-change suspension.** When the ISE (or the immune layer, via an environmental-shift antigen) declares a regime change, the exchangeability precondition for the coverage guarantee is violated. `c_m` is set to an **uninformative sentinel value** of `0.50` and flagged `c_m_state = UNINFORMATIVE`. The B.3 calibration trigger does not fire on `UNINFORMATIVE` — the module is not "badly calibrated," it is "uncalibrated under the current regime." Instead:

- The router demotes the module from `high` safety-tier tasks for the duration of the suspension.
- The module's action classes remain T2 in name but are treated as T3 for routing.
- A `regime_change_suspension` diagnosis event is emitted.
- The module may recalibrate (A2.7) to exit suspension.

This is the machinery that keeps the coverage guarantee honest under distribution shift.

## A2.6 The `CalibrationCertificate`

```yaml
CalibrationCertificate:
  module_id: string
  module_version: semver
  certificate_id: sha256                  # content-addressed
  α: float
  calibration_dataset_digest: sha256
  calibration_n: int
  q̂: float                                # the calibrated threshold
  offline_validation_coverage: float
  offline_validation_tolerance: float
  max_interval_width: float
  ground_truth_latency_ms:
    by_action_class: map[string -> int]
  ground_truth_available_action_classes: [string]
  exchangeability_assumptions: [string]   # e.g., "thermal regime stable for >= 1h"
  certificate_issued_at: datetime
  certificate_expires_at: datetime        # default: 30 days from issued_at
  issuer_key_id: string
  signature: bytes
```

**Registry invariants enforced at registration:**

1. `ground_truth_available_action_classes` must be non-empty for T2 registration.
2. Every action class in the module's `emission.requires_justification_for` must be either in `ground_truth_available_action_classes` or explicitly declared `T3_ONLY` in the contract. A module cannot silently serve an uncalibrated action class under a T2 claim.
3. `certificate_expires_at > certificate_issued_at`.
4. The `issuer_key_id` must be registered in the deployment's calibration-authority trust store. The issuer is not the module author — it is the tool that ran the calibration procedure. Separation of concerns: the module author cannot self-issue a certificate.

**Certificate expiry.** At `certificate_expires_at`, the module is demoted from T2 to T3 automatically, and a `certificate_expired` diagnosis event is emitted. Renewal requires recalibration (A2.7). The default 30-day expiry prevents a module from coasting on a stale calibration; the exact value is per-deployment configurable.

## A2.7 Recalibration

A T2 module may recalibrate when:

- `c_m < 1 − α − δ_1` (warning threshold) for a sustained window
- A regime-change suspension is active
- The certificate is approaching expiry (`≤ 3 days` remaining)
- An operator explicitly requests it

Recalibration protocol:

1. The module enters `RECALIBRATING` state. It is **excluded from control-affecting dispatch** during recalibration. Prediction tasks at `medium` or below may continue on the incumbent certificate (with the router preferring fallbacks).
2. The new calibration set is drawn from recent telemetry (typically the last `N_cal` verified predictions). The provenance attestation is regenerated with the new `sampling_window`.
3. The calibration procedure (A2.4) is re-run: compute `q̂`, validate on held-out, pass registration gates.
4. A new certificate is issued with a new `certificate_id`, `module_version` is bumped (patch level), and the module returns to `DELIBERATION` at T2.
5. **Recalibration is not permitted during an active L3+ state.** The module must be at L2 or above. Rationale: in L3+, the audit substrate itself is compromised, and a calibration certificate issued under L3+ conditions may be based on corrupted data. Recalibration blocks until recovery.

Recalibration is a **reproductive event** — it uses the metamodel's synthesis path (Addendum 3's territory) to fit the calibration, though it does not synthesize a new module. The `RECALIBRATING` state is registered in the audit log with the same discipline as a hot-swap.

## A2.8 What this protocol does not do

- **It does not guarantee coverage under arbitrary distribution shift.** Exchangeability is a real assumption and the suspension mechanism is the honest acknowledgement. A module whose telemetry has shifted so far that no reasonable calibration window is representative cannot be honestly T2; the suspension and the T3 demotion are the correct outcomes.
- **It does not apply to unverifiable actions.** A learned module proposing a strategic action whose counterfactual is unknown cannot be calibrated on coverage. It is T3 by construction, forever, for that action class. This is a ceiling on the module's trustworthiness, not a bug.
- **It does not replace the evaluation gates (§12 of v0.1).** Calibration is one gate; failure-mode independence, energy ratio, and interpretability are separate gates with their own protocols. A module can have a valid calibration certificate and still fail the evaluation gates.
- **It does not calibrate the *decision*.** It calibrates the *prediction*. A well-calibrated classifier can still make bad decisions when combined with an action policy. That gap is closed by the safety shield and the router's reliability-tier filtering, not by the calibration protocol.

## 1. Disposition of Addenda 1 and 2

Both accepted. Notes in descending order of consequence.

### Addendum 1 — Rule-trace format

**Accepted.** Three notes.

**A1.4 — the quantization rule must be stated, not implied.** The DSL prohibits floating point and requires int64 at scale 10⁶, but `BETWEEN`, `LT`, `LE`, `GT`, `GE` take "float (quantized)" in the operator table. The spec must state: *all floating-point values in `signals_observed` are quantized to int64 at scale 10⁶ at the moment of observation, before predicate evaluation; the quantization function is round-half-to-even; the trace records both the original float (for human readability) and the quantized value (for replay).* Without this, two verifiers with different float-to-int rounding will disagree on the boundary, and the disagreement is a replay mismatch on a correct implementation.

**A1.4 — `DEADLINE_REMAINING()` must read from the recorded task descriptor, not the clock.** The DSL prohibits time-of-day reads; `DEADLINE_REMAINING()` is a clock read unless constrained. State explicitly: *`DEADLINE_REMAINING()` computes `task_descriptor.deadline_ms − (signals_observed[".observed_at"] − task_descriptor.submitted_at)`, where both timestamps are recorded in the trace input; it never reads the wall clock.*

**A1.6 step 10 — the T0 verifier's rulebook is human-signed, and this is where verification terminates.** The cross-checker's own emission carries a rule trace; that trace is verified by the next verifier; the chain bottoms out at a T0 verifier whose rulebook is signed by a human key registered in the deployment's authority store. State this as a normative invariant: *the verification chain must terminate at a T0 verifier whose rulebook digest matches a human-signed rulebook in `DELIBERATION`; a chain that does not terminate is a registration error, not a runtime failure.*

### Addendum 2 — Conformal calibration protocol

**Accepted, with the sign correction adopted as normative.** This is the most important correction in the entire review, and it is correct: the `+δ` formulation fires on nominal operation. The corrected form `c_m < 1 − α − δ_k` is the only formulation that self-stabilizes. It lands in Addendum 0 as a B.3 amendment.

**A2.5 — `c_m` is computed over `W_verified`, which excludes unverifiable action classes.** This is correct but should be stated as a semantic boundary, not an implementation detail: *the calibration trigger applies only to action classes with `ground_truth_available = true`; a module that serves both verifiable and unverifiable classes is T2 for the former and T3 for the latter, and the trigger evaluates only the former. The router is responsible for applying the correct tier per action class.* This is the honest boundary the protocol requires, and it needs to be stated in one place or it will be missed.

**A2.6 / A2.7 — certificate expiry during L3+.** A2.7 prohibits recalibration at L3+. But what happens if the certificate expires while the organism is at L3+? The module cannot recalibrate, and it is demoted to T3 on expiry. This is correct behavior, but it needs to be stated: *a certificate that expires during an L3+ state demotes the module to T3; recalibration is deferred until L2 or above; the module's T3 status persists through the L3+ window and is not a special case for recovery.* Otherwise, an operator will assume recovery restores T2, and it does not — recalibration is a separate step.

**A2.5 — `c_m_state = UNINFORMATIVE` sentinel.** Accepted. One clarification: the sentinel must be recorded in the trace so that a replay of a decision made during suspension shows `c_m = UNINFORMATIVE`, not a stale numeric value. Otherwise the replay will show a calibration trigger firing (or not firing) on a value that was not actually being used at decision time. The trace field is `calibration_state: {c_m: float | "UNINFORMATIVE", c_m_source: "rolling" | "suspended", reason: string | null}`.

---

## 2. Addendum 0 — B.3 Amendment

This is a normative amendment to Artifact B. It does not re-open the transition conditions; it adds the trigger mapping, the diagnosis/transition distinction, the sequencing rule, and the calibration sign correction.

### A0.1 Calibration trigger sign correction

Replace the two calibration conditions in B.3:

| Transition | Old (sign-reversed) | Corrected |
|---|---|---|
| L0 → L1 | `c_m < 1 − α + δ_1` | `c_m < 1 − α − δ_1` |
| L1 → L2 | `∀ m ∈ {neural, reservoir}: c_m < 1 − α + δ_2` | `∀ m ∈ {neural, reservoir}: c_m < 1 − α − δ_2` |

With defaults `α = 0.10`, `δ_1 = 0.05`, `δ_2 = 0.15`, the triggers fire at `c_m < 0.85` and `c_m < 0.75` respectively. The `δ` values are margins **below** the coverage guarantee, not tolerated slack above it. The old formulation fired on normal operation and is retracted.

The L3→L2 upward condition `∃ m : c_m ≥ 1 − α` is unchanged and is correct in both formulations; recovery requires the module to be back at the guarantee, not merely above the trigger threshold.

### A0.2 Trigger mapping table

Normative. The state machine records exactly one `trigger.kind` per transition; the dominant signal is recorded, and the others are recorded in `contributing_signals`.

| Transition | Signal that fired | `trigger.kind` |
|---|---|---|
| L0 → L1 | `thermal < thermal_L1` | `thermal` |
| L0 → L1 | neural `c_m < 1 − α − δ_1` | `calibration` |
| L0 → L1 | neural `h_m = DEGRADED` | `module_degraded` |
| L1 → L0 | all upward conditions sustained | `recovery` |
| L1 → L2 | predictive `c_m < 1 − α − δ_2` | `calibration` |
| L1 → L2 | leading predictive `h_m = FAILED` | `module_failed` |
| L1 → L2 | `¬causal_ok` | `causal_unavailable` |
| L2 → L1 | all upward conditions sustained | `recovery` |
| L2 → L3 | predictive `h_m = FAILED` or `c_m < 1 − α − δ_2` | `module_failed` or `calibration` |
| L2 → L3 | `gc > gc_L3` or `q_p95 > q_p95_L3` | `workspace_pressure` |
| L3 → L2 | all upward conditions sustained | `recovery` |
| L3 → L4 | `¬trace_ok` | `trace_unavailable` |
| L3 → L4 | `¬det_ok` | `module_failed` (deterministic engine) |
| L3 → L4 | `gc > gc_L4` or `mem > mem_L4` or `q_p95 > q_p95_L4` | `workspace_pressure` |
| L3 → L4 | `byz_count ≥ byz_count_L4` | `byzantine` |
| L4 → L3 | all upward conditions sustained + `human_ack` | `recovery` |

### A0.3 Dominant-signal priority

When multiple signals fire a transition in the same evaluation cycle, the machine records the **dominant** signal as `trigger.kind` and the others in `trigger.contributing_signals`. The dominant-signal priority order, highest first:

```
trace_unavailable > causal_unavailable > byzantine > module_failed
  > module_degraded > workspace_pressure > calibration > thermal
```

The `byzantine` promotion above `module_failed` is accepted as proposed: a byzantine signal means the reliability of the `module_failed` signal itself is in question, and recording `module_failed` as dominant would attribute a specific failure the trace is not entitled to attribute. This is a trace-semantics choice, not a transition-triggering choice — the transition fires regardless of which signal is dominant.

### A0.4 `workspace_integrity` is diagnosis-only

The trigger enum includes `workspace_integrity`, but no transition in A0.2 uses it. This is deliberate: a single binding corruption is a **diagnosis event**, not a transition trigger. The trace entry for a `workspace_integrity_alarm` sets `event_class = DIAGNOSIS` and does not set `from_level`/`to_level`. The `DegradationTraceEntry` schema gains `event_class: enum[TRANSITION, DIAGNOSIS]`; a diagnosis entry has `from_level = to_level = current_level` and a `diagnosis_code` field.

This closes the E-4 8a assertion: binding corruption produces a diagnosis event at L0, not an L4 transition.

### A0.5 Transition sequencing rule

Normative. The state machine is always in exactly one state and evaluates transitions only from that state.

**Rule:** the hysteresis window for a transition `L_src → L_dst` starts when the machine **enters** `L_src`, not when the underlying signal first becomes favorable. A transition whose upward conditions are already satisfied at the moment of entering the source state still waits the full `T_up[L_src→L_dst]` from entry, not from the earlier favorable-signal time.

**Corollary:** every transition that fires is recorded as its own `DegradationTraceEntry`, with its own `at` timestamp, even if the target state's upward conditions are immediately satisfied for the next transition. The L2→L1 and L1→L0 transitions are distinct entries regardless of how close their timestamps are. An implementation that batches "all satisfiable transitions" in one cycle produces one entry for two transitions and fails the E-5 1b assertion of "exactly 4 transitions, in order."

**Rationale:** without this rule, a machine entering L2 with the recovery conditions already satisfied collapses the L2→L1→L0 recovery into a single evaluation cycle, and the longer `T_up` windows become operationally meaningless. The rule is what makes the asymmetric `T_up > T_down` physiology do any work.

### A0.6 Trigger duration and contributing signals

`DegradationTraceEntry.trigger` gains two fields for replay completeness:

```yaml
trigger:
  kind: <enum as A0.2>
  duration_observed_s: float           # how long the dominant signal held before the transition fired
  contributing_signals:
    - signal_name: string
      kind: <enum as A0.2>
      observed_value: float
      threshold: float
      duration_observed_s: float
```

A replay that recomputes the transition from the recorded health vector must reproduce the same dominant signal and the same contributing set. A mismatch is a state-machine bug, not a replay bug.

---

## 3. Addendum 4 — Health Escalation Policy

This is a normative amendment to Artifact A.1. It is required by the E-5 split: without it, the `L1 → L2` transition under persistent failure is not reachable within the test's time budget, and the E-5 1b scenario cannot be honestly tested.

### A4.1 The `HealthEscalationPolicy` message

```proto
message HealthEscalationPolicy {
  EscalationKind kind                          = 1;
  int32         consecutive_probe_failures     = 2;
  int32         degraded_duration_ms           = 3;
  bool          escalate_on_probe_timeout      = 4;
  bool          escalate_on_probe_unreachable  = 5;
  int32         probe_interval_ms              = 6;
}

enum EscalationKind {
  ESCALATION_UNSPECIFIED          = 0;
  ESCALATION_NONE                 = 1;  // module self-reports FAILED only
  ESCALATION_PROBE_FAILURES       = 2;  // N consecutive probe errors → FAILED
  ESCALATION_DURATION             = 3;  // DEGRADED for N ms → FAILED
  ESCALATION_PROBE_OR_DURATION    = 4;  // either condition (recommended default)
}
```

`FailureSignature` gains field 4: `HealthEscalationPolicy escalation = 4;`.

### A4.2 Defaults

The deployment-wide default is `ESCALATION_PROBE_OR_DURATION` with:

| Field | Default | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| `consecutive_probe_failures` | 3 | Two failures could be transient; three consecutive is a pattern |
| `degraded_duration_ms` | 15_000 | Long enough to distinguish persistent degradation from a transient flap, short enough that L1→L2 is reachable within a test window |
| `escalate_on_probe_timeout` | true | A timed-out probe is a failed probe |
| `escalate_on_probe_unreachable` | true | Connection refused / process dead is a failure, not a degradation |
| `probe_interval_ms` | 5_000 | Matches the telemetry window in B.1 |

With these defaults, a killed module escalates from DEGRADED (at first probe failure) to FAILED after 3 consecutive probe failures (~15 s), which then fires `L1 → L2` after `T_down[L2] = 30 s`. This is exactly what E-5 1b requires.

### A4.3 Per-module override

Per-module override is permitted and is validated at registration. Two registry invariants:

1. `escalate_on_probe_timeout = true` requires `HealthProbe.timeout_ms < degraded_duration_ms`. A module whose probe timeout exceeds its escalation duration would never report DEGRADED before escalating, which defeats the purpose of the intermediate state.
2. `degraded_duration_ms ≥ 3 × max(probe_interval_ms, HealthProbe.expected_period_ms)`. A module whose escalation duration is shorter than three of its own probe intervals would escalate on the first or second DEGRADED report, which is a de facto `ESCALATION_PROBE_FAILURES` with a hidden count of 1 or 2.

Both are rejected at registration with `ValidationIssue{code: "escalation_policy_inconsistent"}`. They are registry invariants, not application logic, because a module that cannot articulate a consistent escalation policy cannot be trusted to report its own health.

### A4.4 How this makes E-5 1b testable

The E-5 1b scenario (persistent failure, expected `L0→L1→L2→L1→L0` = 4 transitions) requires:

- `t = 0`: module killed. First probe failure at `t = 5 s` → `h_m = DEGRADED` → L0→L1 fires at `t = 10 s` (T_down[L1] = 5 s + one telemetry window).
- `t = 15 s`: third consecutive probe failure → `h_m = FAILED` → L1→L2 fires at `t = 45 s` (T_down[L2] = 30 s after L1).
- `t = 60 s`: module restored. Health recovers to HEALTHY at `t = 60 s` → L2→L1 fires at `t = 360 s` (T_up[L2] = 300 s). L1→L0 window starts at `t = 360 s` (per A0.5) → L1→L0 fires at `t = 480 s` (T_up[L1] = 120 s).
- Total: 4 transitions, in order, with distinct trace entries.

Without Addendum 4, the `h_m = FAILED` step never occurs (the module was never re-probed after the first DEGRADED), and the scenario would time out at L1. The addendum is the missing piece.

---

## 4. Addendum 3 — SDK API Reference

Call-level reference for the Python authoring SDK and the Rust runtime SDK. Both are generated from the same proto3 source of truth (A.1); this document is the human-readable surface.

### A3.1 Python authoring SDK

```python
# organism_sdk/contract.py

@dataclass(frozen=True)
class SubstrateContract:
    identity: Identity
    perception: PerceptionSignature
    emission: EmissionSignature
    metabolic: MetabolicProfile
    failure: FailureSignature
    safety: SafetyDeclaration
    lifecycle: Lifecycle
    capabilities: CapabilityScopes
    channels: list[Channel]
    justification: JustificationSpec

    def validate(self) -> list[ValidationIssue]: ...
    def to_proto(self) -> organism.v1.SubstrateContract: ...

@dataclass(frozen=True)
class HealthEscalationPolicy:
    kind: EscalationKind
    consecutive_probe_failures: int = 3
    degraded_duration_ms: int = 15_000
    escalate_on_probe_timeout: bool = True
    escalate_on_probe_unreachable: bool = True
    probe_interval_ms: int = 5_000
```

```python
# organism_sdk/module.py

class OrganismModule(ABC):
    @abstractmethod
    def declare_contract(self) -> SubstrateContract: ...

    @abstractmethod
    def handle(self, input: PerceptionInput) -> Emission: ...

    @abstractmethod
    def health(self) -> HealthReport: ...

    def justify(self, emission: Emission) -> JustificationToken | None:
        """Return a token iff the emission's action class is in
        contract.emission.requires_justification_for. Returning None
        for a required class raises JustificationMissing."""
        return None

    def on_drain(self, deadline: datetime) -> None:
        """Optional. Complete or abandon in-flight work by deadline."""
        return
```

```python
# organism_sdk/inputs.py

class PerceptionInput:
    modalities: dict[Modality, ModalityPayload]
    task_descriptor: TaskDescriptor
    workspace_reader: WorkspaceReader   # capability-scoped
    deadline_remaining_ms: float

@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ModalityPayload:
    modality: Modality
    shape: tuple[int, ...]
    dtype: DataType
    data: bytes

class ModalityUnavailable(Exception):
    """Raised when a declared modality is not present in the input.
    Health signal if the modality is not in required_modalities;
    emission failure if it is."""
```

```python
# organism_sdk/workspace.py

class WorkspaceClient:
    def write(self, binding: BindingRecord) -> BindingWriteResult: ...
    def write_batch(self, bindings: list[BindingRecord]) -> list[BindingWriteResult]: ...
    def read_by_role(self, role_cue: Hypervector, k: int = 10) -> list[BindingRecord]: ...
    def read_by_tag(self, tags: list[str]) -> list[BindingRecord]: ...
    def read_by_binding_id(self, binding_id: UUID) -> BindingRecord | None: ...
    def read_similar(self, hv: Hypervector, k: int = 10) -> list[tuple[BindingRecord, float]]: ...
    def read_by_provenance(self, module_id: str, time_range: tuple[datetime, datetime]) -> list[BindingRecord]: ...

@dataclass(frozen=True)
class BindingWriteResult:
    binding_id: UUID
    status: Literal["ACCEPTED", "REJECTED"]
    reason_code: str | None
    reason_params: dict[str, str] | None
```

```

```

    """Raised by the SDK when justify() returns None for an action
    class in contract.emission.requires_justification_for. The
    runtime treats the emission as verification_status = FAILED."""

### A3.2 Rust runtime SDK

```rust
// organism-client/src/contract.rs

pub struct SubstrateContract {
    pub identity: Identity,
    pub perception: PerceptionSignature,
    pub emission: EmissionSignature,
    pub metabolic: MetabolicProfile,
    pub failure: FailureSignature,
    pub safety: SafetyDeclaration,
    pub lifecycle: Lifecycle,
    pub capabilities: CapabilityScopes,
    pub channels: Vec<Channel>,
    pub justification: JustificationSpec,
}

impl SubstrateContract {
    pub fn validate(&self) -> Result<(), Vec<ValidationIssue>>;
}
```

```rust
// organism-client/src/workspace.rs

#[async_trait]
pub trait WorkspaceClient: Send + Sync {
    async fn write(&self, binding: BindingRecord) -> BindingWriteResult;
    async fn write_batch(&self, bindings: Vec<BindingRecord>) -> Vec<BindingWriteResult>;
    async fn read_by_role(&self, role_cue: &Hypervector, k: usize) -> Vec<BindingRecord>;
    async fn read_by_tag(&self, tags: &[String]) -> Vec<BindingRecord>;
    async fn read_by_binding_id(&self, id: &Uuid) -> Option<BindingRecord>;
    async fn read_similar(&self, hv: &Hypervector, k: usize) -> Vec<(BindingRecord, f64)>;
    async fn read_by_provenance(&self, module_id: &str, range: (DateTime<Utc>, DateTime<Utc>)) -> Vec<BindingRecord>;
}
```

```rust
// organism-client/src/hypervector.rs

pub struct Hypervector([u64; 157]);

impl Hypervector {
    pub const DIM: usize = 10_000;

    pub fn bind(&self, other: &Self) -> Self;         // XOR
    pub fn unbind(&self, role: &Self) -> Self;        // XOR
    pub fn permute(&self, k: usize) -> Self;          // circular shift
    pub fn similarity(&self, other: &Self) -> f64;    // 1 - Hamming/D
    pub fn bundle(hvs: &[Self]) -> Self;              // majority vote, parity tie-break
    pub fn from_bytes_be(bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<Self, DecodeError>;
    pub fn to_bytes_be(&self) -> [u8; 1250];
}
```

```rust
// organism-client/src/errors.rs

#[derive(Debug, thiserror::Error)]
pub enum OrganismError {
    #[error("capability scope missing: {0}")]
    CapabilityScopeMissing(String),
    #[error("lifecycle state below minimum: {actual:?} < {required:?}")]
    LifecycleBelowMinimum { actual: LifecycleState, required: LifecycleState },
    #[error("dimension mismatch: expected {expected}, got {actual}")]
    DimensionMismatch { expected: usize, actual: usize },
    #[error("justification missing for action class: {0}")]
    JustificationMissing(String),
    #[error("registry unavailable: {0}")]
    RegistryUnavailable(String),
    #[error("workspace backpressure: {0}")]
    WorkspaceBackpressure(String),
    #[error("replay mismatch at sequence {0}")]
    ReplayMismatch(u64),
}
```

### A3.3 SDK invariants

1. The SDK must enforce capability scoping on every workspace call before dispatching to the workspace. The workspace enforces it again at the boundary; the SDK's enforcement is a fast local check that produces a clearer error.
2. The SDK must not expose the workspace's internal storage, indices, or ops log. The `WorkspaceClient` surface is the complete module-facing API.
3. The SDK must emit a trace entry for every workspace call, whether it succeeds or is rejected. Rejections are audit records.
4. The SDK's `justify()` invocation must be inside the SDK's handle path, not the module's — the SDK calls `justify()` after `handle()` returns and validates the token against the contract before emitting. A module cannot bypass the token by emitting directly.
5. The SDK's `on_drain()` must complete or abandon in-flight work by `deadline`; if it does not, the SDK records `on_drain_outcome = killed` (writing the record before SIGKILL; if the write itself is killed, the runtime writes a synthetic record).

---

## 5. Biological extension — disposition and roadmap

The biological extension is substantively strong and structurally disciplined: it correctly identifies that the current Organism has no predictive self-model, no global disposition bus, no tolerance-with-memory, no fast reflex path, no programmed death, no developmental staging, and no rest cycle. It cites real literature for each mechanism. The closing caution against biological theatre is correct.

But it is not v0.1, and it must not be folded into A–F. The discipline that produced A–F is contract-first, testable, fault-injected. The biological mechanisms are not yet at that level: they have signatures and timescales, but not fault-injection matrices, not registry invariants, not trace schemas, not replay semantics. Folding them into v0.1 now would multiply the test matrix by 3× and delay closure of the spec phase by months, for mechanisms whose value is real but unproven on this substrate.

**Disposition: accept as v0.2 roadmap, gate behind v0.1 reconciliation.**

### 5.1 What the roadmap must resolve before any biological mechanism enters a v0.2 artifact

The extension raises five structural questions that must be answered before the mechanisms become specifiable:

**1. HormoneBus vs. Axiom 2.** The extension says hormones are "regulatory state, not semantic state." That is a defensible distinction, but it is a *new* distinction that Axiom 2 does not currently make. The revised Axiom 2 says: *no semantic state may bypass the workspace; no data may flow on an undeclared channel.* A HormoneBus is a declared channel, so it passes the letter of the axiom, but it is a channel whose contents affect every module's behavior. The v0.2 artifact must state: *the HormoneBus is a declared channel; hormone readings are written to the workspace every telemetry window as a `hormone_state` binding; the bus is a low-latency mirror, not an alternative source of truth.* Without this, the HormoneBus becomes a second workspace with weaker audit guarantees.

**2. AutonomicController vs. router authority.** The extension says the autonomic controller "sets policy parameters that the router reads." But if those parameters determine preemption masks and metabolic multipliers, the autonomic controller is exercising dispatch authority under another name. The v0.2 artifact must reconcile: either the router remains the sole dispatcher and the autonomic controller only modulates *parameters the router already exposes* (preemption class, cross-check frequency, exploration budget), or the autonomic controller's decisions become workspace bindings that the router reads and traces. The first is simpler; the second is more auditable. The choice is a v0.2 decision, not a v0.1 one.

**3. ImmuneLayer placement and the audit trail.** The extension places the ImmuneLayer before the router. If the immune layer can quarantine or demote a module, those are lifecycle and routing actions, and they must go through the workspace or the audit trail has a hole. The v0.2 artifact must state: *every immune-layer decision is a workspace binding with `salience.fidelity = FULL` and `safety.affects_control = true`; the immune layer may not take lifecycle or routing actions except by writing bindings that the router and registry consume.* This is the immune system's version of Axiom 2, and it must be stated or the immune layer becomes an unaudited super-authority.

**4. Lifecycle states for apoptosis and morphogenesis.** The extension proposes `SELF_TERMINATING`, `SUCCEEDED`, and `DEVELOPMENT`. These are real additions to A.1's lifecycle enum and must be specified with the same discipline: transition conditions, registry invariants, trace entries, promotion gates. The v0.2 artifact must state: *self-termination requires `contract.lifecycle.self_termination_permitted = true`; T0 modules are forbidden from self-terminating; control-affecting modules may self-terminate only with an immediately available cross-check-capable successor; `DEVELOPMENT` is a sub-state of `EXPLORATION` and inherits its restrictions.*

**5. The test matrix.** Every biological mechanism needs fault-injection tests or it is theatrical. The v0.2 artifact must extend F.7 with categories J (interoception/allostasis), K (endocrine), L (immune), M (autonomic), N (apoptosis), O (morphogenesis). A hormone that cannot be released, measured, and replayed is not a hormone; the test matrix is what makes it one.

### 5.2 Recommended sequencing

| Phase | Artifact | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| v0.1 closure | Addenda 0, 3, 4 (this response) | — |
| v0.1 reconciliation | **Artifact R — Reconciliation Index** | A–F + 0–4 complete and consistent |
| v0.2 open | **Artifact G — Endocrine and Interoceptive Specification** (from biological extension §2–3) | The five structural questions above answered |
| v0.2 open | **Artifact H — Immune Layer Specification** (from §4) | Same |
| v0.2 open | **Artifact I — Autonomic and Apoptosis Specification** (from §5–6) | Same |
| v0.2 open | **Artifact J — Morphogenesis, Metabolism, Circadian Specification** (from §7) | Same |
| v0.2 | **F extension — biological fault-injection matrix** | G–J specified |
| v0.2 | **Reference implementation v0.2** | F extension green |

The reconciliation pass (Artifact R) is the gate to implementation, and the biological extension opens only after it. The other agent's proposal for R is correct and I endorse it: one document listing every schema field, every invariant, every fault-injection test, and every registry check, so the implementation team has one authoritative index rather than five artifacts plus four addenda. That is the artifact that closes the spec phase.

### 5.3 What to preserve from the biological extension now

Three things should be preserved immediately, as **v0.1 design constraints** rather than v0.1 mechanisms:

1. **The interoceptive gap is real.** The current control loop is reactive; it has no predictive model. Even without implementing the ISE, v0.1 should note that the L0→L1 trigger is reactive-only and that a preemptive trigger is deferred to v0.2. This is a known limitation, not a bug.
2. **The hormone-state audit requirement is correct.** Even without a HormoneBus, v0.1 should establish the principle that *any global disposition that affects routing must be recorded in the trace*, so that a decision made under stress is distinguishable in replay from one made at baseline. In v0.1, "stress" is `degradation_level` and `cortisol` is not yet a thing; the principle is what survives.
3. **The apoptosis-safety constraint is correct.** T0 deterministic modules must never be permitted to self-terminate; the L4 refuge cannot choose to die. This is a design constraint on v0.2's apoptosis protocol and should be stated now.

**Addendum 4** — the per-module override validation invariants are correct. The default values are correct. The `escalate_on_probe_unreachable` default is the source of Finding R-1 below — not because the invariant is wrong, but because its semantics contradict the E-5 1b timeline that motivated it.

**Addendum 3** — the SDK reference is correct as far as it goes. Findings R-6 through R-9 below identify schemas the SDK references but does not yet fully specify. These are SDK *completeness* issues, not design errors.

## 2. Disposition of My Addenda 1 and 2 Notes

All six accepted:

- **A1.4 quantization** — round-half-to-even, scale 10⁶, record both original float and quantized value. Correct and adopted.
- **A1.4 `DEADLINE_REMAINING()`** — uses recorded timestamps only, never the wall clock. Correct. It exposes Finding R-2 (`TaskDescriptor.submitted_at` is missing).
- **A1.6 T0 termination** — human-signed rulebook at the base of the verification chain, enforced at registration. Correct, and its registration mechanism is Finding R-9.
- **A2.5 semantic boundary** — action-class split stated in one place. Correct.
- **A2.6/7 certificate expiry at L3+** — T3 status persists through L3+; recalibration separate. Correct.
- **A2.5 UNINFORMATIVE** — trace field `calibration_state: {c_m, c_m_source, reason}`. Correct. Replay semantics for suspended states is Finding R-12.

## 3. Biological Extension — Disposition and Sixth Question

The disposition is correct. Accept v0.2 gating behind Artifact R, accept the five structural questions as the entry gate to G–J, accept the three immediate v0.1 design constraints (interoceptive gap is a documented limitation; global disposition affecting routing must appear in the trace; T0 modules must never self-terminate).

**Sixth structural question to add to the gate:**

> **6. HormoneBus vs. Axiom 6 (reconstructibility).** The mirror pattern — bus as low-latency cache of a workspace `hormone_state` binding — solves audit in principle but not in latency. If the router reads from the bus at time `t`, the bus may lag the workspace binding by up to one telemetry window, so the router's decision at `t` reflects hormone state from `t − staleness`. Axiom 6 requires the decision to be reconstructible. The trace must therefore record the bus reading *as the router saw it*, including a `staleness_ms` field, and replay must reconstruct decision-correctness against that recorded staleness, not against the workspace state at `t`. Without this, either Axiom 6 is violated (bus reads are unattested) or Axiom 2 is violated (bus becomes an alternative source of truth). The v0.2 artifact must state which of the three resolutions in §3.1 of the biological extension is adopted, and specify the trace fields accordingly.

This is a superset of their question 1; it is the specific mechanism question that has to be resolved, not just the axiom-compatibility question.

---

**R-1 — `escalate_on_probe_unreachable` semantics contradict the E-5 1b timeline**

**Severity:** Blocking.
**Manifests in:** A4 §A4.2, A4 §A4.4, E §E.6 scenario 1b.
**Description:** A4.2 declares `escalate_on_probe_unreachable = true` by default, with the rationale "Connection refused / process dead is a failure, not a degradation." A4.4's worked timeline for E-5 1b assumes the opposite: a killed module reports `DEGRADED` at first probe failure (t=5s) and `FAILED` after three consecutive failures (t=15s). If `escalate_on_probe_unreachable = true`, then at t=5s the module is already `FAILED` (unreachable), and the L0→L1 trigger — which per A0.2 fires on `h_m = DEGRADED` — never fires. The system would sit at L0 with a dead module until some other signal (workspace_pressure, thermal) happens to fire a transition. This is a genuine correctness bug: a catastrophic module failure would not produce the expected L0→L1 transition.
**Resolution:** Change the `escalate_on_probe_unreachable` default to `false`. Rationale: the E-5 1b timeline is the correct behavior — a first probe failure, however severe the underlying cause, produces `DEGRADED` so the intermediate transition fires, and escalation to `FAILED` requires sustained evidence (either 3 consecutive failures or `degraded_duration_ms`). This also aligns `escalate_on_probe_unreachable` with `escalate_on_probe_timeout`, both of which should require sustained evidence to escalate. If the operator wants immediate escalation on unreachable, that is a per-module override, justified by the module's class (e.g., a critical actuator whose absence must trigger immediate escape to L4).

---

**R-2 — `TaskDescriptor.submitted_at` is missing**

**Severity:** Blocking.
**Manifests in:** C §C.2, A1 §A1.4.
**Description:** A1.4's corrected `DEADLINE_REMAINING()` formula uses `task_descriptor.submitted_at`. C.2's `TaskDescriptor` schema does not have this field.
**Resolution:** Add `submitted_at: datetime` to `TaskDescriptor`. It is the router-side submission time, distinct from `signals_observed[".observed_at"]` which is the module-side observation time. Both must be present in the trace for `DEADLINE_REMAINING()` to be recomputable from the trace alone.

---

**R-3 — `RoutingTraceEntry.calibration_state` is missing**

**Severity:** Blocking.
**Manifests in:** C §C.5, A2 §A2.5.
**Description:** A2.5 requires the trace to record `calibration_state: {c_m, c_m_source, reason}` so that a decision made under suspension is distinguishable in replay from one made at baseline. C.5's `RoutingTraceEntry` schema does not have this field.
**Resolution:** Add `calibration_state` to `RoutingTraceEntry`. The field is required when `safety_tier ∈ {high, critical}`; optional otherwise. If optional and absent, replay treats the state as `c_m_source = "unrecorded"` and refuses to verify any calibration-dependent assertion in the trace — this is the safe behavior.

---

**R-4 — `DegradationTraceEntry` schema is incomplete after A0.4 and A0.6**

**Severity:** Blocking.
**Manifests in:** B §B.7, A0 §A0.4, A0 §A0.6.
**Description:** A0.4 adds `event_class: enum[TRANSITION, DIAGNOSIS]` and `diagnosis_code` to the trace entry. A0.6 adds `trigger.duration_observed_s` and `trigger.contributing_signals`. B.7's original schema has none of these. An implementation reading B.7 in isolation would produce a trace that does not satisfy A0.4 or A0.6.
**Resolution:** B.7's schema must be treated as superseded by the following consolidated form (the authoritative schema is now this one):

```yaml
DegradationTraceEntry:
  entry_id: uuid
  at: datetime
  event_class: enum[TRANSITION, DIAGNOSIS]
  from_level: enum[L0..L4]              # = to_level for DIAGNOSIS
  to_level: enum[L0..L4]                # = from_level for DIAGNOSIS
  diagnosis_code: string | null         # required iff event_class = DIAGNOSIS
  trigger:                              # required iff event_class = TRANSITION
    kind: enum[thermal, calibration, module_degraded, module_failed,
               workspace_pressure, workspace_integrity, byzantine,
               trace_unavailable, causal_unavailable, recovery, manual]
    duration_observed_s: float
    contributing_signals:
      - signal_name: string
        kind: <same enum>
        observed_value: float
        threshold: float
        duration_observed_s: float
  hysteresis:
    window_down_s: float
    window_up_s: float                  # present only for upward transitions
  active_modules_at_transition: [module_handle]
  demoted_modules: [module_handle]
  promoted_modules: [module_handle]
  workspace_snapshot_id: string | null  # required for L2→L3 and L3→L4
  safety_shield_interventions_during_window: int
  human_ack: {operator_id, signed_at} | null   # required for L4→L3
```

---

**R-5 — `WorkspaceTraceEntry` is referenced but never specified**

**Severity:** Blocking.
**Manifests in:** D §D.5, D §D.9, A1 §A1.6, A3 §A3.1.
**Description:** `WorkspaceTraceEntry` is emitted on every write (D.5), every read (D.6), and every rejection (D.5 "rejection logging"); it is referenced by A1.6 for replay of rule-trace read sets; A3's `TraceEmitter.emit_workspace` implies it is a first-class schema. But no artifact defines its fields.
**Resolution:** Specify `WorkspaceTraceEntry` in an Addendum 5 (or as an inline section in a v0.1.1 of D). It must at minimum contain:

```yaml
WorkspaceTraceEntry:
  entry_id: uuid
  at: datetime
  operation: enum[WRITE, WRITE_BATCH, READ_BY_ROLE, READ_BY_TAG,
                  READ_BY_BINDING_ID, READ_SIMILAR, READ_BY_PROVENANCE,
                  EVICT, COMPRESS, SUMMARIZE, PROMOTE, SNAPSHOT,
                  COMPACTION_COMPLETE, REJECTION]
  module_id: string
  module_contract_digest: sha256
  binding_ids: [uuid]                    # all bindings touched by the operation
  result_digest: sha256 | null           # null for WRITE / EVICT / etc.
  rejection_reason_code: string | null   # required iff operation = REJECTION
  rejection_reason_params: map<string, string> | null
  at_sequence: uint64                    # ops-log sequence
  workspace_snapshot_id: string | null   # present iff read from a snapshot
```

`at_sequence` is the essential field: without it, a workspace trace entry cannot be positioned in the ops log, and replay of a rule-trace's read set (A1.6 step 4) cannot verify that the same bindings were read.

---

**R-6 — `FailureSignature` schema is not shown in the SDK reference**

**Severity:** Important.
**Manifests in:** A §A.1, A4 §A4.1, A3 §A3.1.
**Description:** A3.1's Python SDK shows `SubstrateContract.failure: FailureSignature` but does not show the `FailureSignature` dataclass. A4.1 adds `escalation: HealthEscalationPolicy` to `FailureSignature`. Without the dataclass shown, an implementer cannot know whether the escalation field is expected in the Python SDK surface, and whether `FailureSignature.validate()` rejects inconsistent escalation policies (A4.3's two invariants).
**Resolution:** A3.1 must show the full `FailureSignature` dataclass including `escalation` and its `validate()` behavior; A3.2's Rust SDK must do the same.

---

**R-7 — `PerceptionSignature.required_modalities` is missing**

**Severity:** Important.
**Manifests in:** A §A.1, E §E.3 (correction E-2).
**Description:** Correction E-2 established that `ModalityUnavailable` is a health signal if the missing modality is not required by the task, and an emission failure if it is. The distinction requires the module to declare which modalities are required by each task it serves. No such field exists in `PerceptionSignature`.
**Resolution:** Add `required_modalities: [Modality]` to `PerceptionSignature`, and note in E.3 that a module's `handle()` receives a `PerceptionInput` whose modalities are a superset of `required_modalities` or is authorized to raise `ModalityUnavailable` as an emission failure.

---

**R-8 — `CapabilityScopes` in SDK does not show the exploration-sink fields; `ExplorationSinkRegistry` is unspecified**

**Severity:** Important.
**Manifests in:** A §A.1, A §A.2, A3 §A3.1, disposition Q2.
**Description:** The Q2 answer adds `can_override_trace_sink`, `can_override_trace_sink_exploration`, and `exploration_trace_sink_id` to `CapabilityScopes`, along with registry invariants coupling the exploration-sink fields to a registered `ExplorationSinkRegistry` with its own lifecycle, retention, and promotion paths. The `CapabilityScopes` message in A §A.1 already has `can_override_trace_sink` from E-7; it does not have the two exploration-sink fields. The `ExplorationSinkRegistry` is referenced as an authority but is not specified as a proto service or a sink contract.
**Resolution:** (a) A §A.1 `CapabilityScopes` gains `can_override_trace_sink_exploration` and `exploration_trace_sink_id`; (b) A §A.2 gains the three registry invariants from the Q2 answer; (c) a new artifact — **Addendum 6 — Exploration Sink Registry** — specifies the sink contract (retention, access, promotion-path), the `ExplorationSinkRegistry` proto service (register / promote / list / audit), and the sink's trace-entry schema.

---

**R-9 — Justification chain termination is not enforceable at registration**

**Severity:** Important.
**Manifests in:** A1 §A1.6, A §A.2.
**Description:** A1.6 step 10 states, "the verification chain must terminate at a T0 verifier whose rulebook digest matches a human-signed rulebook in `DELIBERATION`; a chain that does not terminate is a registration error." The chain is dynamic — it depends on which module produces the initial token and which modules verify. Registration can only verify that the module's `justification.kind` and `cross_check_eligible_roles` *can* form a terminating chain, not that they *will* on every emission.
**Resolution:** Add a registration-time reachability check. For every module `m` with `justification.kind != NONE`, and for every role `r ∈ m.cross_check_eligible_roles`, check that at least one registered module in `r` either (a) has `justification.kind = NONE` (chain terminates), or (b) itself has a cross-check chain that terminates, checked recursively with a bounded depth (e.g., 4). Reject registration if no terminating chain exists. This is a graph reachability check at registration, not a runtime constraint.

---

**R-10 — Diagnosis events and the hysteresis window**

**Severity:** Important.
**Manifests in:** A0 §A0.4, A0 §A0.5.
**Description:** A0.5 specifies that the hysteresis window starts on entering the source state and that every transition is a distinct trace entry. A0.4 introduces diagnosis events as a separate event class. But nothing states whether a diagnosis event during a hysteresis window resets, extends, or is orthogonal to the window. The natural reading is "orthogonal," but the natural reading is not a normative statement.
**Resolution:** Add a clause to A0.5: "Diagnosis events do not alter the hysteresis window of any transition. A diagnosis event fired during the `T_down` or `T_up` of a transition does not reset, extend, or shorten that window, and does not accumulate toward a transition trigger unless the diagnosis's `diagnosis_code` is explicitly declared as an input signal to a B.3 condition."

---

**R-11 — L4→L3 `human_ack` is not recorded in `DegradationTraceEntry`**

**Severity:** Important.
**Manifests in:** B §B.3, A0 §A0.2, R-4 (above).
**Description:** The L4→L3 transition requires `human_ack` (B.3, A0.2). Neither `DegradationTraceEntry` as originally specified in B.7, nor `RoutingTraceEntry` as specified in C.5 (which has `human_ack: null` but is a routing trace, not a degradation trace), records where the ack lands. Without a designated recording field, the ack is either unrecorded or re-derived from an out-of-band log, defeating Axiom 6.
**Resolution:** R-4's consolidated `DegradationTraceEntry` includes `human_ack: {operator_id, signed_at} | null`. The field is required for L4→L3 and forbidden for all other transitions. `RoutingTraceEntry.human_ack` is retained only for the routing-level resumption event, and is `null` unless the routing event directly coincides with a human-ack transition.

---

**R-12 — `c_m_state = UNINFORMATIVE` and replay**

**Severity:** Non-blocking.
**Manifests in:** A2 §A2.5, R-3 (above).
**Description:** A2.5 requires the trace to record `calibration_state = UNINFORMATIVE` during suspension. R-3 adds the field. But replay's behavior is not specified: does replay recompute `c_m` from `W_verified` and compare to the trace, or does replay use the trace's recorded value and skip recomputation for suspended states?
**Resolution:** Replay uses the trace's recorded value. When `c_m_source = "suspended"`, replay does not recompute `c_m` — the trace's `c_m` is authoritative for that decision, and any calibration-trigger assertion in the trace is verified against the recorded value, not a recomputed one. When `c_m_source = "rolling"`, replay may optionally recompute `c_m` for cross-check, but the assertion uses the recorded value. This is stated in A2.5 or A2.6 (replay semantics are part of the certificate's guarantees).

---

**R-13 — `VerificationResult` is not specified**

**Severity:** Non-blocking.
**Manifests in:** A3 §A3.1, A3 §A3.2.
**Description:** `JustificationToken.verify(verifier)` returns a `VerificationResult` in A3.1; the Rust side has no equivalent. Neither the shape nor the possible values are specified. A1.6 returns `VERIFIED | REJECTED(reason_code)`, which is a candidate shape, but nothing binds the SDK to it.
**Resolution:** Define `VerificationResult { outcome: enum[VERIFIED, REJECTED], reason_code: string | null, reason_params: map<string, string>, verifier_id: string, verifier_contract_digest: sha256, verified_at: datetime }`. Bind the SDK to A1.6's outcome enum. Add a symmetric method in A3.2.

---

**R-14 — `probe_interval_ms` vs. `HealthProbe.timeout_ms`**

**Severity:** Non-blocking.
**Manifests in:** A4 §A4.2, A4 §A4.3, A §A1.1 (`HealthProbe` schema).
**Description:** A4.3 invariant 1 requires `timeout_ms < degraded_duration_ms`. No constraint relates `timeout_ms` to `probe_interval_ms` (or to `HealthProbe.expected_period_ms` for streaming probes). If `timeout_ms > probe_interval_ms`, polled probes overlap — a second probe starts before the first has timed out — and the escalation counting becomes ambiguous (does a timed-out probe count when the next probe has already started?).
**Resolution:** Add an invariant: `timeout_ms < probe_interval_ms` for polled probes, and `timeout_ms < expected_period_ms` for streaming probes. This is a simple sanity invariant, easy to add, and eliminates a class of ambiguity in the escalation counter.

---

### R.3 Open Questions (decisions not yet made)

These are not findings — they are decisions the spec does not currently need to make, but which will surface during implementation.

**OQ-1 — `ESCALATION_NONE` for T0 modules.** A4.1 defines `ESCALATION_NONE` ("module self-reports FAILED only"), but no rule states whether T0 modules should use it. A T0 module that is SIGKILLed cannot self-report; `ESCALATION_NONE` would leave the runtime unable to detect the death of the deterministic engine. Recommendation when it becomes relevant: T0 modules use `ESCALATION_PROBE_OR_DURATION` with `consecutive_probe_failures = 2` and `degraded_duration_ms = 5000` — tighter than the default, but not `ESCALATION_NONE`. This is a decision for the first implementation iteration, not for R.

**OQ-2 — `WorkspaceTraceEntry` batch semantics.** If `WorkspaceTraceEntry` is emitted per-operation (R-5), a `write_batch` of 10,000 bindings produces 10,000 trace entries. If emitted per-batch, individual binding rejections are grouped into one entry. Both are auditable; the choice affects trace volume by an order of magnitude. Recommendation: per-batch entry with an embedded `rejections: [BindingWriteResult]` array, plus a `batch_id` correlator, plus the ops-log sequence range for replay positioning.

**OQ-3 — `FailureSignature` escalation for T0 vs T2 modules.** Follow-on from OQ-1: the escalation policy is per-module in the contract, but the *defaults* are deployment-wide. Should T0 modules receive tighter defaults automatically, or must they declare an override? Recommendation: deployment provides per-role defaults (T0 = tighter, T2 = standard, T3 = loose) with per-module override, so the contract does not need to carry a field for "use the T0 default."

**OQ-4 — HormoneBus replay (v0.2, but flagged now).** As stated in §3 above, the mirror pattern's staleness must be recorded in the trace for Axiom 6 to hold. This is a v0.2 question, but it constrains the v0.1 trace schema in one respect: any trace field that records a "global disposition" reading must carry `staleness_ms`. This is a design constraint on R-3's `calibration_state` and on any future hormone fields.

**OQ-5 — `TraceEmitter.emit_*` caller contract.** A3.1/A3.2 show `emit_routing(entry)`, `emit_degradation(entry)`, etc. Does the SDK auto-populate `policy_id`, `policy_digest`, and `level` from the router's current state, or must the caller supply them? If the caller supplies them, a buggy caller can emit a trace with a stale `policy_digest`. Recommendation: SDK auto-populates these fields from the router's authoritative state, and the caller cannot override. This is a small SDK invariant to add to A3.3.

---

### R.4 Implementation Readiness Checklist

The reference implementation of A–F + A0–A4 may begin when the following are true:

| # | Condition | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | All blocking findings (R-1 through R-5) resolved by amendment | Open — requires the other agent to deliver amendments or accept resolutions |
| 2 | All important findings (R-6 through R-11) resolved or explicitly deferred with a written rationale | Open |
| 3 | Addendum 5 (WorkspaceTraceEntry) specified | Open — required by R-5 |
| 4 | Addendum 6 (ExplorationSinkRegistry) specified | Open — required by R-8 |
| 5 | Corpus consistency: every schema referenced by any artifact is defined in some artifact | Open until R-4, R-5, R-6, R-7, R-8, R-13 are closed |
| 6 | Every invariant in R.5 (Invariant Index, below) is locatable in exactly one artifact and enforceable in code | Open |
| 7 | Every fault-injection scenario in F.7 has a corresponding trace schema and assertion in R.6 (Test Index, below) | Open |
| 8 | The biological extension is formally declared out of v0.1 scope, with the six structural questions as its gate | **Closed** — accepted in §3 above |
| 9 | Registry validation rules in A.2 are complete after R-8 and R-9's additions | Open |
| 10 | All addenda are numbered, dated, and attached to the artifacts they amend | Open |

The spec phase does not close until conditions 1–3, 5, 9, and 10 are closed. Conditions 4, 6, 7, and 8 are closeable after implementation begins (but before any production candidate).

### R.5 Invariant Index

A consolidated list of every invariant the implementation must enforce, grouped by enforcement point. Each invariant's canonical source is listed; a code reviewer can trace any enforcement decision back to a specific artifact.

**Registry invariants (enforced in the registry process):**

| ID | Invariant | Source |
|---|---|---|
| REG-1 | Registration rejects `may_actuate = true` ∧ `reliability_tier = T3` | A §A.2 |
| REG-2 | Registration rejects `may_actuate = true` ∧ `justification.kind = NONE` | A §A.2 |
| REG-3 | Registration rejects `logged_for_trace = false` on channels with actuating consumers | A §A.2 |
| REG-4 | Registration rejects `can_emit_control_affecting = true` without `requires_cross_check` or `reliability_tier ∈ {T0, T1}` | A §A.2 |
| REG-5 | Promotion to `DELIBERATION` requires shadow records meeting `promotion_criteria` | A §A.2 |
| REG-6 | `can_override_trace_sink = true` requires `reliability_tier ∈ {T0, T1}` | A §A.2 (E-7) |
| REG-7 | `can_override_trace_sink_exploration = true` requires `reliability_tier ∈ {T0, T1, T2}` and a registered exploration sink ≥ `SHADOW_VALIDATED` | Q2 answer, R-8 |
| REG-8 | `escalate_on_probe_timeout = true` requires `timeout_ms < degraded_duration_ms` | A4 §A4.3 |
| REG-9 | `degraded_duration_ms ≥ 3 × max(probe_interval_ms, expected_period_ms)` | A4 §A4.3 |
| REG-10 | `timeout_ms < probe_interval_ms` (polled) or `timeout_ms < expected_period_ms` (streaming) | R-14 (new) |
| REG-11 | Calibration dataset and training dataset must be disjoint (≤ 1% record-hash overlap) | A2 §A2.3 |
| REG-12 | Provenance attestation signed by a key in the deployment's data-source trust store | A2 §A2.3 |
| REG-13 | Certificate issuer key in the deployment's calibration-authority trust store | A2 §A2.6 |
| REG-14 | Every `requires_justification_for` action class is either in `ground_truth_available_action_classes` or explicitly declared `T3_ONLY` | A2 §A2.6 |
| REG-15 | Terminating justification chain exists for every module with `justification.kind != NONE` | R-9 (new) |

**Workspace invariants (enforced in the workspace process):**

| ID | Invariant | Source |
|---|---|---|
| WS-1 | Every binding's `checksum` verified at write and re-verified at read | D §D.12 |
| WS-2 | Every write preceded by an fsynced `Write` op in the ops log | D §D.12 |
| WS-3 | No binding with `affects_control = true` in `COMPRESSED` or `SUMMARIZED` fidelity | D §D.4 |
| WS-4 | No `PENDING` binding may be evicted | D §D.4 |
| WS-5 | `snapshot_id = content_root` of snapshot body incl. `merkle_root` | D §D.8 |
| WS-6 | Loaded snapshot's `merkle_root` verifies against its tiers | D §D.10 |
| WS-7 | Workspace dimension is immutable per instance | D §D.12 |
| WS-8 | Ops log strictly ordered by sequence number | D §D.9 |
| WS-9 | Every read emits a `Read` op with `result_digest` | D §D.9 |
| WS-10 | All operations produce bit-identical results on replay with identical inputs and `t` | D §D.12 |
| WS-11 | Capability scope enforced on every read and write at the workspace boundary | D §D.5, §D.6, A §A.1 (Correction §2.7) |
| WS-12 | `write_batch` fsyncs once per batch; per-binding status returned | Q2 answer |

**Router invariants (enforced in the router process):**

| ID | Invariant | Source |
|---|---|---|
| RT-1 | Active policy in `DELIBERATION` or `DEPRECATED`; `RETIRED` policy cannot be loaded | C §C.4 (1.7) |
| RT-2 | No plan includes a module below the level's minimum lifecycle state | C §C.4 |
| RT-3 | No `DispatchPlan` fallback fails current level eligibility | C §C.4, correction 1.2 |
| RT-4 | No `critical` task dispatched to T2/T3, with or without cross-check | C §C.4, §C.3 |
| RT-5 | `cross_check_required = true` names a cross-check module whose role is in `cross_check_eligible_roles` and whose justification kind can verify the proposer's token | C §C.4 |
| RT-6 | `control_actuation` at L2+ carries `justification_required ≠ NONE` or disposition = `HOLD` | C §C.4 |
| RT-7 | Every plan records `policy_id`, `policy_digest`, full exclusion trace with `reason_code` | C §C.4, correction 1.8 |
| RT-8 | Plans deterministic given the same descriptor, policy, registry snapshot, health vector, level | C §C.4 |
| RT-9 | `control_actuation` at `high`/`medium` may be dispatched to T2 iff cross-check is valid; T3 only at `low`/`research` with cross-check | A0 §A0.2 (invariant 4a) |
| RT-10 | `requires_interpretability = true` requires role in `{VERIFIER, GOVERNANCE, MEMORY}` or interpretable output/justification | Correction 1.3 |
| RT-11 | `deadline_ms` and `energy_budget_j` are positive or `+Infinity`; `NaN` and negative rejected | Correction 1.4 |
| RT-12 | L2 `FRONTEND` candidate names a `downstream` in `{SOLVER, VERIFIER, GOVERNANCE}` and the downstream itself passes L2 eligibility | Correction 1.5 |
| RT-13 | `cross_check_required = true` ∧ `cross_check_module = null` ⇒ `disposition = HOLD` | Correction 1.6 |
| RT-14 | A plan with `trace_sink_override` set must have the capability and record the override in the trace | Correction 1.9, Q2 answer |

**Degradation invariants (enforced in the degradation state machine):**

| ID | Invariant | Source |
|---|---|---|
| DG-1 | Machine is always in exactly one state; transitions evaluated only from that state | A0 §A0.5 |
| DG-2 | Hysteresis window starts on entering the source state | A0 §A0.5 |
| DG-3 | Every transition is a distinct trace entry, even if the target state is immediately ready to transition further | A0 §A0.5 |
| DG-4 | Dominant signal recorded; priority `trace_unavailable > causal_unavailable > byzantine > module_failed > module_degraded > workspace_pressure > calibration > thermal` | A0 §A0.3 |
| DG-5 | `workspace_integrity` is diagnosis-only; `event_class = DIAGNOSIS`, `from_level = to_level` | A0 §A0.4 |
| DG-6 | Diagnosis events do not alter hysteresis windows | R-10 (new) |
| DG-7 | Calibration triggers use `c_m < 1 − α − δ_k`, not `+δ_k` | A0 §A0.1 |
| DG-8 | L4→L3 requires `human_ack`; `human_ack` required and forbidden per R-11 | A0 §A0.2, R-11 |
| DG-9 | Transition SLOs from B.5 are hard limits; SLO miss is a fault-injection failure | B §B.5 |

**Module SDK invariants (enforced in the SDK):**

| ID | Invariant | Source |
|---|---|---|
| SDK-1 | Capability scope checked locally before workspace dispatch | A3 §A3.3 |
| SDK-2 | Workspace internal storage/indices/ops log never exposed | A3 §A3.3 |
| SDK-3 | Trace entry emitted on every workspace call, including rejections | A3 §A3.3 |
| SDK-4 | `justify()` invocation inside SDK handle path; module cannot bypass token | A3 §A3.3, E-1 |
| SDK-5 | `on_drain()` completes or abandons by deadline; `killed` outcome recorded | A3 §A3.3, E-3 |
| SDK-6 | `TraceEmitter.emit_*` auto-populates `policy_id`, `policy_digest`, `level`; caller cannot override | OQ-5 recommendation |

This is the authoritative invariant index. Any invariant cited elsewhere in the corpus without an entry here is a finding — the corpus has drifted.

### R.6 Test Index

Every fault-injection scenario in F.7 has been cross-referenced against the invariants it tests. A scenario that does not test any invariant in R.5 is a finding — it means F is asserting something R does not name, or R is asserting something F does not cover.

| Scenario | Tests invariants | Trace schema used |
|---|---|---|
| A1 | REG-5, DG-1 | DegradationTraceEntry |
| A2 | RT-2, RT-7, RT-8 | RoutingTraceEntry |
| A3 | RT-4, RT-5 | RoutingTraceEntry |
| A4 | WS-5, WS-6, WS-9, WS-10 | WorkspaceTraceEntry + replay |
| B1 | DG-1, DG-2, DG-7 | DegradationTraceEntry |
| B2 | DG-1, DG-2, DG-3 | DegradationTraceEntry |
| B3 | DG-7 | DegradationTraceEntry |
| B4 | DG-7 | DegradationTraceEntry |
| C1 | DG-1, DG-2, DG-3, DG-4 | DegradationTraceEntry |
| C2 | DG-1, DG-2, DG-3 | DegradationTraceEntry |
| C3 | DG-4 | DegradationTraceEntry |
