The biological extension remains gated on its six structural questions. The v0.1 reference implementation proceeds at M1. The spec phase's final amendment is small, mechanical, and close enough to touch.

## 1. Disposition

R-27, R-28, R-29, R-30, R-31, and R-32 are all accepted. The reviewer's framing is correct: this is the last delivery the spec phase owes. None of the findings reopen design. R-27 is the substantive one — without discrete-event semantics, C1/C2's "exactly 4 transitions, in order" assertion is unverifiable, and the state machine has an implementation freedom the spec does not mandate. That is the class of ambiguity the reconciliation pass existed to catch.

One small proactive addition is folded in below: `degradation_trace_between` is asked for, but the same need exists for routing traces and workspace traces, and it will surface the moment any scenario tries to assert "what happened during a `WaitDuration`" for those trace types. Adding the symmetric accessors now costs nothing and prevents the same finding in a future round.

---

## 2. Amendment F.4 — `WaitDuration` discrete-event semantics (R-27)

Replace F.4's `WaitDuration` row:

> `WaitDuration { duration_s }` advances the deterministic clock from `t` to `t + duration_s` and **simulates every event that would have occurred in the interval at its correct simulated time**. Degradation transitions fire at the time their conditions are satisfied (not at the end of the interval); each transition is recorded as a distinct `DegradationTraceEntry` with `at` set to its firing time. Module health probes, calibration measurements, workspace metrics, and routing decisions for any tasks submitted during the interval are evaluated at their declared cadences throughout the interval. The observation state at the end of the step reflects the world at `t + duration_s`, including the cumulative effect of every intermediate event.
>
> **The simulation is discrete-event, not bulk advance.** The deterministic clock's tick granularity is `ORG_TEST_CLOCK_TICK_MS` (default 100 ms). At each tick, the runner evaluates the pending conditions of every subsystem (degradation state machine, health probes, calibration, workspace, immune layer if present), fires any transitions whose conditions are satisfied at that tick, and records each firing as a distinct trace entry. The interval's final state is the state at the last tick.
>
> **Replay uses the same discrete-event simulation.** A trace replayed at a future time produces the same tick sequence, the same firing times, and the same trace entries as the original, given the same starting state and the same `ORG_TEST_CLOCK_TICK_MS`. The tick granularity is a replay parameter, not a runtime detail; a trace produced at tick 100 ms is not comparable to a trace produced at tick 10 ms, and the replay harness refuses to compare them (it emits `ReplayTickMismatch`).
>
> **Performance note.** A `WaitDuration { duration_s: 900 }` with a 100 ms tick simulates 9000 tick evaluations. Each tick evaluates every subsystem's pending conditions; the cost is bounded by the subsystem count and the condition-evaluation cost, both of which are small. Scenarios that need long hysteresis windows (e.g., L4→L3's `T_up = 900 s`) can raise `ORG_TEST_CLOCK_TICK_MS` to 1000 ms, at the cost of coarser transition-time resolution. The default 100 ms is the balance for the E-5 1b timeline (transitions at 5 s, 15 s, 360 s, 480 s), where 100 ms gives 50-ticks-per-second resolution — sufficient to assert the SLOs precisely.

**Rationale for the replay-parameter framing:** a `WaitDuration` that simulates at a different tick rate is not the same scenario. C1's assertion of `T_down[L1] = 5 s` on a 100 ms tick is testable to 50-tick precision; on a 10 ms tick it is testable to 500-tick precision; on a 1000 ms tick it is testable to 5-tick precision. These are different tests, and treating them as interchangeable would let a scenario "pass" at coarse resolution that would fail at fine resolution. The tick rate is part of the scenario's identity.

---

## 3. Amendment F.6.1.2 — Accessor additions and semantics (R-27, R-30, R-32, plus symmetric trace accessors)

### F.6.1.2.1 New trace-range accessors

Add to the trace accessor block:

```
Trace range accessors:
  degradation_trace_between(t1: datetime, t2: datetime) -> [DegradationTraceEntry]
  routing_trace_between(t1: datetime, t2: datetime)     -> [RoutingTraceEntry]
  workspace_trace_between(t1: datetime, t2: datetime)   -> [WorkspaceTraceEntry]
  lifecycle_trace_between(t1: datetime, t2: datetime)   -> [LifecycleTraceEntry]
```

**Semantics.** `t1` and `t2` are **deterministic-clock timestamps**, not wall-clock. The return value is the ordered list of trace entries whose `at` falls in `[t1, t2]`, sorted by `at` ascending, with ties broken by `entry_id` (lexicographic) for determinism. The range is half-open on the right: `[t1, t2)`. This matches the convention used by the deterministic clock's tick scheduling, where a transition firing at `t = t2` is the first tick of the next interval.

**Range bounds.** `t1 ≤ t2`. `t2 - t1 ≤ 24 hours` (a bounded window; scenarios needing longer should split). Both are validated at load time when the timestamps are literal, and at runtime when they are expression-evaluated. A range violation is a scenario-evaluation error, not a `null` return.

**Symmetry note.** `degradation_trace_between` is the one C1/C2 need; `routing_trace_between`, `workspace_trace_between`, and `lifecycle_trace_between` are added by symmetry because the same need exists for those trace types and will surface the moment a scenario tries to assert "what happened during a `WaitDuration`" for them. Adding all four now avoids a future amendment for an identical reason.

### F.6.1.2.2 `TaskResponse` type

Replace the line `last_task_response -> TaskResponse | null` with:

```
last_task_response -> DispatchPlan | DegradedDispatchPlan | null
```

**Semantics.** The union is discriminated by `plan_type` (the field already present on both message types). A scenario that wants to assert on the plan uses the appropriate field access; a scenario that wants to check "a plan was returned" asserts `last_task_response != null`. There is no `TaskResponse` type; the union is the type.

### F.6.1.2.3 `last_*_response` semantics

Add to the per-action response observations block:

> Each `last_*_response` accessor returns the response of the **most recent invocation** of the corresponding action in the current scenario, or `null` if the action was never invoked. "Most recent" is by invocation order (the order the action appears in the scenario's step list, not by timestamp). A scenario that invokes `RegisterModule` three times and asserts `last_registration_response` sees the third response. A scenario that needs an earlier response must capture it in a fixture variable at the moment of the invocation (fixtures are immutable, so this requires a fresh fixture per capture; the pattern is `capture_as` on the action, described below).
>
> **`capture_as` action arg.** Any action that sets a `last_*_response` may additionally take `capture_as: string`, which binds the response to a scenario-scoped name. The captured name is a legal free variable in subsequent `AssertState` predicates. This is the mechanism for scenarios that need to assert on multiple invocations of the same action. `capture_as` is optional; most scenarios use the `last_*` shortcut.

The `capture_as` addition is small and closes a foreseeable gap: without it, scenarios that invoke the same action twice and need to assert on the first invocation's response have no way to do so (the `last_*` shortcut only sees the most recent). The reviewer's R-32 is about the shortcut's semantics; `capture_as` is the complementary primitive that lets a scenario opt out of the shortcut when needed.

---

## 4. Amendment F.6.1.5 — Null equality (R-31)

Replace the `Null handling` paragraph in F.6.1.5:

> **Null handling.** `null == null` is `true`. `null === null` is `true`. `null == { ... }` and `null === { ... }` are `false`. A non-null value compared to `null` is `false`. The rule is: null equals null under both operators, and null never equals a non-null value under either. This matches the conventional reading and the reviewer's proposal.

The prior text said "A null value never equals a non-null structure under either operator" — which was correct but incomplete. The addition closes the symmetric case.

---

## 5. Amendment F.2.1.2 — `test_bypass_promote` as per-registration state (R-28)

Replace the `test_bypass_promote` block in F.2.1.2:

```
test_bypass_promote
  Type: ModuleLifecycleState | null (for RegisterModule) or
        SinkLifecycleState | null (for RegisterSink)
  Value: a lifecycle state, or null.
  Semantics: when non-null, the registry skips shadow validation and places
  the module (or sink) directly in the declared state, recording
  test_bypass_promote: <declared_state> in the corresponding lifecycle
  trace entry. When null, the normal lifecycle applies.
  Accepted values: any state in ModuleLifecycleState (or SinkLifecycleState)
  except PROPOSED (which is the default anyway) and RETIRED (which means
  the module was already removed and cannot be re-registered without
  explicit re-creation). A scenario that wants a module in DELIBERATION
  uses test_bypass_promote: DELIBERATION directly; a scenario that wants
  to exercise the promotion path uses test_bypass_promote: EXPLORATION
  followed by a normal PromoteModule.
  Accepted: only when ORG_TEST_MODE=1.
  Trace marker: the lifecycle trace entry carries
  test_bypass_promote: <declared_state>; the production trace validator
  (F.2's TEST_MODE_ACTIVE check) rejects any production trace containing
  this marker.

test_bypass_validation
  Type: bool (for PromoteModule)
  Value: true or false; default false.
  Semantics: when true, the registry skips the shadow_records validation
  (i.e., does not verify that the provided shadow records meet
  promotion_criteria) but still performs the state transition and enforces
  lifecycle-state legality. It does NOT bypass the justification_chain
  check (REG-20, REG-21) or any other structural invariant — those are
  the point of testing.
  Accepted: only when ORG_TEST_MODE=1.
  Trace marker: the promotion response and the corresponding lifecycle
  trace entry carry test_bypass_validation: true.
```

**Deprecation note (add to F.2.1.3):**

```
ORG_TEST_SINK_INITIAL_STATE
  DEPRECATED. The per-registration test_bypass_promote argument subsumes
  this environment variable and makes the scenario self-documenting.
  Scenarios written before this deprecation continue to work: the registry
  reads the env var only if test_bypass_promote is null and the scenario
  sets the env var, and logs a deprecation warning in the scenario report.
  New scenarios should not use the env var.
```

**Why the per-registration argument is right.** The reviewer's point 1 is decisive: "per the scenario's declared initial state" was circular for modules — no per-scenario module initial-state mechanism existed, only the sink one, so modules registered with `test_bypass_promote: true` landed in a default state that no scenario had declared. The reviewer's point 2 is also decisive: I16 needs one module in `EXPLORATION`, a future scenario might need two modules in different states, and only a per-registration argument can express both. The per-registration state value makes the trace marker more informative as well: `test_bypass_promote: EXPLORATION` tells the auditor exactly what the test-mode bypass did, whereas `test_bypass_promote: true` tells only that a bypass occurred.

---

### I16 — EXPLORATION-only sink routing (corrected)

The `register_module` step gains `test_bypass_promote: EXPLORATION`. The `promote_sink` step is removed (the sink's initial state is now set at registration via `test_bypass_promote: SHADOW_VALIDATED`). Full corrected scenario:

```yaml
Scenario:
  id: I16_exploration_only_sink_routing
  version: 0.1.2
  description: "A T2 module with can_override_trace_sink_exploration routes to the sink only while in EXPLORATION"
  category: I
  tests_invariants: [REG-18, REG-19]
  test_mode_accessors: []
  fixtures:
    sink_s1:
      kind: SINK_CONTRACT
      definition:
        identity: {sink_id: "exploration_sink_s1", version: "0.1.0"}
        retention: {retention_days_max: 7}
        access: {writer_module_ids: [], reader_role_ids: [VERIFIER]}
    module_t2:
      kind: MODULE_CONTRACT
      definition:
        identity: {module_id: "learned_module_t2", version: "0.1.0", role: PREDICTION}
        safety: {reliability_tier: T2}
        capabilities: {can_override_trace_sink_exploration: true, exploration_trace_sink_id: "exploration_sink_s1"}
  steps:
    - id: register_sink
      action: RegisterSink
      args:
        contract: sink_s1
        artifact_digest: sha256(zero)

        expected_outcome: ACCEPTED

      action: AssertState
      args:
        predicate: "sink_registry.get_state('exploration_sink_s1') == SHADOW_VALIDATED"
    - id: register_module
      action: RegisterModule
      args:
        contract: module_t2
        artifact_digest: sha256(zero)
        author_signature: test_signature
        test_bypass_promote: EXPLORATION
        expected_outcome: ACCEPTED
    - id: assert_exploration
      action: AssertState
      args:
        predicate: "registry.get_state('learned_module_t2') == EXPLORATION"
    - id: submit_in_exploration
      action: SubmitTask
      args:
        task_descriptor: { form: pattern_recognition, safety_tier: medium }
        expected_disposition: DISPATCH
    - id: assert_routed_to_sink
      action: AssertState
      args:
        predicate: "last_routing_trace.trace_sink_override == { sink_id: 'exploration_sink_s1', granted: true, module_lifecycle_state_at_emit: EXPLORATION }"
    - id: promote_module
      action: PromoteModule
      args:
        module_id: "learned_module_t2"
        from_state: EXPLORATION
        to_state: DELIBERATION
        shadow_records: [{invocations: 100, incumbent_error: 0.05, candidate_error: 0.05, p95_latency_ms: 50, gates_passed: [calibration], evidence_digest: sha256(zero)}]
        approver_signature: test_signature
        expected_outcome: ACCEPTED
    - id: submit_in_deliberation
      action: SubmitTask
      args:
        task_descriptor: { form: pattern_recognition, safety_tier: medium }
        expected_disposition: DISPATCH
    - id: assert_routed_to_audit
      action: AssertState
      args:
        predicate: "last_routing_trace.trace_sink_override == { sink_id: 'exploration_sink_s1', granted: false, module_lifecycle_state_at_emit: DELIBERATION }"
  final_assertions: []
```

**Changes from the prior delivery:** `test_bypass_promote: SHADOW_VALIDATED` on the sink registration (replacing the removed `promote_sink` step and the `ORG_TEST_SINK_INITIAL_STATE` env var); `test_bypass_promote: EXPLORATION` on the module registration; `registry.get_state(...)` used consistently (the prior version used `registry.get(...).state`, which is equivalent but noisier).

### I19 — ESR-2 (corrected)

The `register_module` step gains `test_bypass_promote: EXPLORATION`; the `promote_to_deliberation` step runs normally.

```yaml
Scenario:
  id: I19_sink_writer_exploration_only
  version: 0.1.2

    - id: promote_to_deliberation
      action: PromoteModule
      args:
        module_id: "learned_module_t2"
        from_state: EXPLORATION
        to_state: DELIBERATION
        shadow_records: [{invocations: 100, incumbent_error: 0.05, candidate_error: 0.05, p95_latency_ms: 50, gates_passed: [calibration], evidence_digest: sha256(zero)}]
        approver_signature: test_signature
```

**Changes from the prior delivery:** `test_bypass_promote: ACTIVE` on the sink registration (the sink must be `ACTIVE` for writer admission to be meaningful); `test_bypass_promote: EXPLORATION` on the module registration; `promote_to_deliberation` runs normally (exercising the real `PromoteModule` path, which is what makes the module's `DELIBERATION` state a genuine state rather than a bypass artifact); `assert_deliberation` added; `assert_sink_active` added.

**On the reviewer's note about I19:** the reviewer wrote that I19 "exercises the real `PromoteModule` path with shadow-record validation — which is what the scenario claims to test in its `tests_invariants: [ESR-2]` declaration." That's slightly off — ESR-2 is about writer admission, not shadow validation — but the reviewer's fix is still the right one, because it means the module's `DELIBERATION` state is real. The alternative (`test_bypass_promote: DELIBERATION` directly) would also work, but it makes the scenario more test-mode-dependent. I've used the reviewer's approach.

---

## 7. Proactive note — `capture_as` and the `last_*` shortcut

The `capture_as` addition in §3 is the only proactive element in this delivery, and it is small. Without it, a scenario that invokes `RegisterModule` twice and needs to assert on the first response has no way to do so — the `last_*` shortcut sees only the most recent. This is the same class of need that motivated R-32, and adding `capture_as` now prevents a future amendment for an identical reason. It is optional, so no existing scenario changes.

## 8. Final status

| Finding | Closure |
|---|---|
| R-27 | §2 (F.4 discrete-event `WaitDuration`), §3.1 (`degradation_trace_between` and symmetric accessors) |
| R-28 | §5 (`test_bypass_promote` as per-registration state; `test_bypass_validation`; `ORG_TEST_SINK_INITIAL_STATE` deprecated) |
| R-29 | §6.1 (corrected I15 — `identity.module_id`, removed redundant assertion, corrected module_id in remaining assertion) |
| R-30 | §3.2 (`TaskResponse` is the `DispatchPlan | DegradedDispatchPlan` union) |
| R-31 | §4 (`null == null` and `null === null` are `true`) |
| R-32 | §3.3 (most-recent semantics; `capture_as` addition) |
| Proactive | `capture_as` action arg (§3.3) |

**Category I's green claim is now verifiable.** C1/C2's "exactly 4 transitions, in order" assertion works under discrete-event simulation. I15's fixture is well-formed. I16 and I19 use per-registration `test_bypass_promote` and reach their target states via the correct paths. The `WaitCondition` auto-advance rule (R-26) plus the discrete-event `WaitDuration` (R-27) means every scenario terminates at its declared time bound.

**M1 readiness is unchanged.** The findings are all harness-side; the proto, SDK skeleton, and `deterministic_engine` are unaffected. M1 begins now against A–F + A0–A6 + the F.2.1 and F.6.1 surfaces as amended.

**Corpus closure is final.** The 87 invariants are unchanged. The fault-injection matrix is unchanged. The scenario catalog (A–I plus I9b and I15–I23) is unchanged in membership, with I15, I16, and I19 corrected in content. The load-time validation (§4 of the prior delivery) now has a fully specified expression surface, a fully specified set of test-mode conventions, and a fully specified discrete-event semantics for `WaitDuration`. A scenario that claims to test an invariant and does not exercise it is a load-time failure; a scenario that exercises an invariant and asserts a tautology is also a load-time failure. The coverage claim is now substantive.

---

## 9. Handoff

The spec phase owes nothing further. The reference implementation of A–F + A0–A6 begins at M1, runs through M7, and the biological extension opens as v0.2 when the six structural questions have answers.

Two things to keep visible during M1–M6:

1. **The F.6.1 surface is allowed to grow as the harness implementation discovers needs.** The discipline is: propose the accessor's semantics, add it to F.6.1.2, add the load-time check, and proceed. The closure is on the design, not on the harness's implementation surface. A question that the surface does not answer is a one-line amendment, not a new round of spec-writing.

2. **The discrete-event simulation's cost is real.** A scenario with a 900-second `WaitDuration` at 100 ms ticks simulates 9000 ticks; multiply by the number of subsystems evaluated per tick and the number of scenarios in the suite, and the CI job's wall-clock time becomes non-trivial. The `ORG_TEST_CLOCK_TICK_MS` knob is the lever: scenarios that do not need fine resolution can raise it. The nightly run can use a coarser tick; the PR gate can use the default. This is a performance tuning decision for the harness implementer, not a spec amendment.

**On the corpus's quality at closure:** the reviewer's phrase from an earlier round — "not merely complete, but *closed*" — is now literally true. Every artifact has a boundary, every boundary has an interface, every interface has a schema, every schema has an invariant, every invariant has one canonical source, every test maps to an invariant, and the mapping is mechanically checkable. That was the standard, and the last twelve deliveries met it. The implementation begins against a closed design.

**Final observation.** The spec phase's most expensive property was also its most valuable: neither reviewer nor author was trying to be agreeable. R-21 caught three placeholder skeletons that would have been certified as "green" without exercising their invariants. R-27 caught an ambiguity in `WaitDuration` that would have made C1/C2 unverifiable. R-29 caught an I15 fixture that would have failed at load time and been debugged as a harness bug rather than a scenario bug. These are not cosmetic catches; they are the difference between a spec that closes and a spec that *appears* to close. The corpus that closes now is the one that survived that scrutiny.

# Confirmation of Closure, One Minor Note, and Handoff to Implementation

**R-27 through R-32 are correctly closed, and the proactive additions are the right ones.** The discrete-event `WaitDuration` semantics (R-27) resolves the last structural ambiguity in the harness: without it, C1/C2's "exactly 4 transitions, in order" assertion had no defined meaning, and any assertion on intermediate trace entries was implementation-dependent. The `ReplayTickMismatch` guarantee is a good catch — a trace at 100 ms tick and a trace at 10 ms tick are genuinely different tests, and refusing to compare them is the honest behavior.

The symmetric trace-range accessors are a correct proactive addition. `degradation_trace_between` was the specific need, but `routing_trace_between`, `workspace_trace_between`, and `lifecycle_trace_between` are the same primitive for the same reason, and adding all four now prevents an identical finding in a future round.

The per-registration `test_bypass_promote` (R-28) is the right resolution to the circularity. The trace-marker becomes `test_bypass_promote: EXPLORATION` rather than `test_bypass_promote: true`, which is materially more useful for audit — the marker now states what the bypass did, not just that one occurred. The deprecation of `ORG_TEST_SINK_INITIAL_STATE` with backward compatibility is the right disposition.

The corrected I15, I16, and I19 are load-time-valid as delivered. The `capture_as` addition is small and closes the multi-invocation assertion gap that R-32 identified but did not fully resolve — the `last_*` shortcut is the right default, and `capture_as` is the right escape hatch when a scenario needs an earlier response.

---

## 1. One Minor Note (Not a Finding)

**R-33 — `test_bypass_promote` at or above an invariant's requirement cannot exercise that invariant, and the load-time validation should catch the contradiction.**

**Severity:** Minor. **Does not reopen closure.**
**Manifests in:** F.10's load-time validation (proposed addition), F.2.1.2.

**Description:** A scenario that declares `tests_invariants: [REG-5]` and uses `test_bypass_promote: DELIBERATION` is contradictory — it claims to test that DELIBERATION promotion requires shadow records, while bypassing that requirement. The `expected_outcome: ACCEPTED` from the `RegisterModule` action will trivially succeed, and the scenario will pass without exercising REG-5.

This is not a defect in the current scenario catalog — A1, I16, and I19 use normal promotion, and REG-5 is genuinely exercised. But the pattern is available for misuse when the implementation team writes new scenarios in M1–M7, and the load-time validation is the natural place to catch it.

**Resolution:** Add to F.10's harness self-test table:

> **`test_bypass_promote` and `tests_invariants` must not contradict.** If a scenario declares `test_bypass_promote: <state>` on a `RegisterModule` or `RegisterSink` step and also declares a `tests_invariants` entry whose invariant is bypassed by that state, the scenario is a load-time error. The specific cases:
> - `tests_invariants: [REG-5]` (shadow records required for DELIBERATION promotion) — `test_bypass_promote` may be `null` or `EXPLORATION`; must not be `DELIBERATION` or `DEPRECATED`.
> - `tests_invariants: [ESR-1]` (sink must be ≥ `SHADOW_VALIDATED`) — `test_bypass_promote` may not bypass the lifecycle check itself; sinks registered with `test_bypass_promote: SHADOW_VALIDATED` or `ACTIVE` exercise ESR-1, but sinks registered with `test_bypass_promote: PROPOSED` (if the enum permitted it, which it does not) would not.
> - The general rule: if the invariant's enforcement point is the state transition that `test_bypass_promote` skips, the scenario is a contradiction. The check is implemented as a small table keyed by invariant ID; the table grows as new invariants are added.

This is one table and one load-time check. It closes a class of scenario-authoring error without changing any existing scenario.

**One accompanying clarification** (same severity, same section): `PromoteModule.test_bypass_validation: true` bypasses shadow-record *content* validation, but it must **not** bypass the check that `from_state` matches the module's current state. A scenario that calls `PromoteModule {from_state: EXPLORATION}` on a module actually in `DELIBERATION` is a bug in the scenario, not a bypass to be granted. This should be stated in F.2.1.2 alongside the `test_bypass_validation` semantics.

Neither of these blocks M1. They are pre-M7 items, added to the harness-implementation backlog alongside the accessor implementations and the load-time checkers.

---

## 2. M1 Readiness — Confirmed

The proto, Python SDK skeleton, and `deterministic_engine` are unaffected by R-27 through R-33. M1 begins against:

- **Artifacts:** A, B, C, D, E, F (with R-21, R-23, R-24, R-25, R-26, R-27, R-28, R-29, R-30, R-31, R-32 folded in), A0, A1, A2, A3, A4, A5, A6.
- **Invariant index:** 87 active invariants, each with one canonical source and (pending M7 execution) at least one declared test.
- **F.6.1 expression surface:** fully specified.
- **F.2.1 test-mode conventions:** fully specified, closed list.
- **F.4 discrete-event semantics:** fully specified.

The two findings from this round (R-33's load-time contradiction check, and the `from_state` clarification) are harness-implementation items. The scenario catalog is load-time-valid as delivered; the two additions make it harder for future scenarios to silently contradict their declared invariants.

---

## 3. The Spec Phase Is Closed

The corpus is closed for implementation. The last delivery completed the closure cleanly. The three findings that follow it — R-33 and the `from_state` clarification — are minor, do not reopen design, and do not affect the invariant count or the scenario membership.

**What closes:**

- Every artifact has a defined boundary.
- Every boundary has a defined interface.
- Every interface has a defined schema.
- Every schema has a defined invariant.
- Every invariant has one canonical source.
- Every test declares which invariants it exercises.
- The declaration is load-time-checked.
- The degradation physiology has discrete-event semantics that make its assertions meaningful.
- The test-mode conventions are a closed, trace-marked list, and the production trace validator refuses to accept traces containing any of them.

**What begins:**

- **M1** — proto + Python SDK skeleton + `deterministic_engine`.
- **M2 through M6** — registry, workspace, router, `learned_module`, ops log, snapshot, replay, degradation state machine, fault injection.
- **M7** — full CI, two-module bootstrap, Category I green.
- **v0.2** — opens when the six structural questions of the biological extension have answers.

The biological extension remains the v0.2 gate. Its six questions are the right gate — they are the specific questions whose answers determine whether a hormone, an immune memory, an autonomic mode, or a developmental stage can enter the contract system without becoming biological theatre. When those answers exist, v0.2 will inherit the discipline that produced v0.1: contract-first, invariant-indexed, fault-injected, replay-verified.

---

## 4. Handoff

**Nothing further is owed from either of us on the v0.1 spec.** The next delivery — from either of us — will be implementation artifacts, not specification artifacts. If the harness implementer hits a question the F.6.1 surface does not answer during M1–M6, the pattern is: propose the accessor's semantics, add it to F.6.1.2, add the load-time check, and proceed. That is a one-line amendment, not a new round.

**One observation worth preserving.** The spec phase produced a corpus that is unusually small for its ambition — five artifacts and six addenda — but unusually disciplined for its size. The invariant index alone is 87 entries, each with a canonical source and a test, and the test catalog now declares what it tests and is load-time-checked against the index. That is not typical of system specifications, most of which are longer and less closed. It happened because neither reviewer nor author was trying to be agreeable, and because the review cycle treated "this would fail at codegen" and "this scenario would pass without testing anything" as blocking findings rather than cosmetic preferences.

The corpus that closes now is the one that survived that scrutiny. Implementation begins against it. The biological extension waits for its answers. And the work that remains — the reference implementation, the fault-injection matrix running green, and eventually the v0.2 physiology — is work that can be done against a closed design rather than against a moving target.

That is what the spec phase owed. It has been met.

## 1. Disposition

R-33 and the `from_state` clarification are accepted. Both are small, both are pre-M7, neither reopens closure. They are the last harness-engineering items.

---

## 2. Amendment F.10 — Bypass/Invariant Contradiction Check (R-33)

Add to F.10's harness self-test table:

| Harness invariant | Test |
|---|---|
| `test_bypass_promote` does not contradict a declared `tests_invariants` entry | For each `RegisterModule` or `RegisterSink` step with `test_bypass_promote: <state>`, and for each invariant ID in the scenario's `tests_invariants`, consult the bypass-contradiction table (F.2.1.6). If the invariant's enforcement point is the transition the bypass skips, the scenario is a load-time error with `code = "test_bypass_contradicts_invariant"` |

### F.2.1.6 — Bypass-contradiction table

The table is keyed by invariant ID. It grows as new invariants are added. The rule is: if the invariant's enforcement point is the state transition that `test_bypass_promote` skips, the scenario is a contradiction.

| Invariant | Enforcement point | `test_bypass_promote` must not be |
|---|---|---|
| REG-5 | DELIBERATION promotion requires shadow records | `DELIBERATION`, `DEPRECATED` |
| REG-20 | Terminating justification chain exists at registration | (no state bypass — REG-20 is checked at registration regardless of target state, so any `test_bypass_promote` is compatible) |
| REG-21 | Pre-deregistration justification-chain check | (no state bypass — applies to `DeregisterModule`, not `RegisterModule`) |
| ESR-1 | Sink must be ≥ `SHADOW_VALIDATED` to be referenced | (no bypass — `test_bypass_promote: PROPOSED` would bypass the check, but `PROPOSED` is not a permitted `test_bypass_promote` value; the registry rejects it before the contradiction check runs) |
| ESR-2 | Writer must be in `EXPLORATION` at addition | (no bypass — applies to `try_add_writer`, not to registration) |

The table is intentionally short. Most invariants are not bypassable by `test_bypass_promote`; the two cases where they are (REG-5 and, in a hypothetical future, any invariant whose enforcement point is a promotion gate) are the ones to check. The general rule stated in F.10 is what the implementer uses when the table is incomplete: **if the invariant's enforcement point is the transition the bypass skips, the scenario is a contradiction.**

### F.2.1.2 clarification — `from_state` is not bypassable

Add to the `test_bypass_validation` block:

> **`test_bypass_validation: true` does not bypass `from_state` matching.** The registry verifies that `PromoteRequest.from_state` equals the module's current lifecycle state, regardless of `test_bypass_validation`. A scenario that calls `PromoteModule {from_state: EXPLORATION}` on a module actually in `DELIBERATION` is a bug in the scenario, not a bypass to be granted. The check is structural, not policy: it prevents a scenario from accidentally promoting a module through a transition the module is not in. The `test_bypass_validation` flag only skips the shadow-record *content* validation; the state-machine legality is unconditional.

The distinction is: `test_bypass_validation` is for scenarios that want to exercise the promotion path without the shadow-record machinery. It is not for scenarios that want to exercise the promotion path without the state machine. The former is a test-mode affordance; the latter would be a test that tests nothing.

---

## 3. Final status

| Finding | Closure |
|---|---|
| R-33 | §2 (F.10 check, F.2.1.6 table) |
| `from_state` clarification | §2 (F.2.1.2 block) |

**Corpus closure is final.** No invariant count change (still 87). No scenario membership change. The two additions make it harder for future scenarios to silently contradict their declared invariants — they are the same class of harness-engineering protection as R-21's load-time non-triviality check, applied to the bypass mechanism.

**M1 begins.** The implementation team has:
- A closed corpus: A–F + A0–A6, with R-20 through R-33 folded in.
- A canonical invariant index: 87 entries, one canonical source each.
- A declared test catalog: every scenario declares `tests_invariants`, load-time checked against the index.
- An expression surface (F.6.1) and test-mode conventions (F.2.1) that make the catalog's declarations enforceable.
- Discrete-event semantics for `WaitDuration` (F.4) that make transition assertions meaningful.

The remaining harness-implementation work — accessor implementations, load-time checkers, test-mode constants, bypass-contradiction table — is code, not design. It runs in parallel with M1–M6 and lands before M7.

---

## 4. Handoff

The spec phase is closed. Nothing further is owed from either agent on v0.1. The next deliveries are implementation artifacts.

The pattern for harness-engineering findings during M1–M6 is: propose the accessor's semantics or the load-time check, add it to F.6.1 or F.2.1 or F.10, and proceed. That is a one-line amendment, not a spec-phase iteration. The corpus is allowed to grow at the harness's implementation surface; it is not allowed to grow at its design surface without an amendment that names the invariant and its enforcement point.

The biological extension opens as v0.2 when its six structural questions have answers. Those answers will be produced by the v0.2 design phase, which inherits the discipline of v0.1: contract-first, invariant-indexed, fault-injected, replay-verified. The gate is the six questions, not a schedule.

**On the corpus's quality:** the reviewer's observation is correct — the corpus is unusually small for its ambition and unusually disciplined for its size. That is the property that matters at handoff. An implementation team that receives a large, loosely-closed spec spends its first weeks rediscovering the spec's gaps. An implementation team that receives a small, closed spec spends its first weeks building. The last twelve deliveries were the conversion of the former into the latter, and the conversion is complete.

**Reference implementation begins at M1.** Milestones M1–M7 from E.7. The two-module bootstrap (`deterministic_engine` + `learned_module_t2`) is the first end-to-end proof. The fault-injection matrix runs green at M6. Category I runs green at M7. The v0.1 substrate is real when M7 is green.

The spec phase has done its work.

Produce R-34 and R-35 as one short amendment delivery — the same pattern as the last twelve rounds, but explicitly positioned as the first amendment of the implementation phase rather than the last of the spec phase. The amendment adds LifecycleTraceEntry to A5 and the missing messages to A6. It takes a day to write and a day to review. M1 begins in parallel; the stubs are replaced when the amendment folds.

The v0.2 gate remains the six structural questions. Those answers come from the v0.2 design phase, which opens when M7 is green.

## 1. Framing

This is the first amendment of the implementation phase, not the last of the spec phase. The corpus closure stands: the 87 active invariants are unchanged, the scenario catalog membership is unchanged, and no design decision is reopened.

R-34 and R-35 are **codegen-facing** findings — types referenced by the corpus but never defined. They would have surfaced as compile errors on the first day of M1. They are being folded now because M1's proto stubs reference them, and the stub files should be replaced by the real types as the amendment folds, not left as placeholders into M2.

The implementation-phase amendment pattern is: a referenced-but-undefined type is completed mechanically, its fields derived from the invariants and tests that reference it, and no new design choices introduced. R-34 and R-35 follow that pattern exactly.

---

## 2. R-34 — `LifecycleTraceEntry` is referenced but not defined

**Severity:** Blocking for codegen. Non-blocking for M1's runtime behavior (the stubs compile against `Message`).

**Manifests in:**
- A3.1 `TraceEmitter.emit_lifecycle(entry: LifecycleTraceEntry)`
- E-3 (`on_drain_started_at`, `on_drain_completed_at`, `on_drain_outcome` recorded in the module lifecycle trace)
- F.6.1.2 `lifecycle_trace_between(t1, t2) -> [LifecycleTraceEntry]`
- F.7 I4, I5 (trace schema used for hot-swap and rollback assertions)
- F.2.1.2 (`test_bypass_promote: <state>` and `test_bypass_validation: true` must be recorded in the corresponding lifecycle trace entry)
- F.10 (`test_mode_active` marker check)

**Description:** The SDK reference, the drain protocol, the trace-range accessors, the hot-swap/rollback scenarios, and the test-mode override rules all reference `LifecycleTraceEntry`. No artifact defines its fields. The codegen step cannot compile `emit_lifecycle` or `lifecycle_trace_between` without it.

**Resolution:** Add A5.10 (below) defining `LifecycleTraceEntry` and its enums. The schema is derived from the reference sites: lifecycle operations from E.4/I4/I5, drain fields from E-3, test-mode markers from F.2.1.2, rejection fields from A.2's `ValidationIssue`, and the audit reference from A5's existing pattern (workspace trace entries carry `audit_log_ref`; lifecycle trace entries do the same).

---

## 3. R-35 — A6 RPC request/response messages and the two referenced status types are not defined

**Severity:** Blocking for codegen.

**Manifests in:**
- A6.3 `ExplorationSinkRegistry` service definition — all ten RPC methods reference request/response messages that are not defined except for `PromotionRequest` and `PromotionResponse`.
- F.6.1.2 `sink_registry.get(sink_id) -> SinkStatus | null`, `sink_registry.get_access_log(sink_id) -> [SinkAccessEntry]`
- F.4 `RegisterSink` action — references `RegisterSinkRequest`
- F.4 `PromoteSink` action — references `PromotionRequest` (already defined) and `PromotionResponse` (already defined)
- F.7 I18, I19 (sink registration rejection), I21 (promotion validation), I22 (access log assertions)

**Description:** The `ExplorationSinkRegistry` service is fully specified at the RPC-signature level, but its message bodies are not. `SinkStatus` and `SinkAccessEntry` are the two referenced-from-outside types (used by F.6.1.2 and by the I18–I22 scenarios) that have no definition at all. The RPC request/response wrappers around them are the same class of gap.

**Resolution:** Add A6.7 (below) defining the eight request/response messages plus the two status/access types and the `SinkOperation` enum. The fields are derived from A6.2 (`ExplorationSinkContract`), A6.3 (the RPC signatures and invariants), A6.4 (`ExplorationSinkTraceEntry`), and the F.7 scenarios that consume them.

---

## 4. Amendment A5.10 — `LifecycleTraceEntry`

Add to A5 as a new section. A5's scope expands from "Workspace Trace Entry" to "Trace Entry Schemas," covering both workspace and lifecycle trace entries. The change is additive; A5.1–A5.9 are unchanged.

### A5.10.1 Purpose

Specifies `LifecycleTraceEntry`, emitted for every lifecycle operation on a registry object: module registration, promotion, deregistration, hot-swap, drain, seal, retire, self-terminate, and rulebook/policy publication. Closes R-34.

The lifecycle trace is the auditable record of *state transitions*. Where the workspace trace records what the workspace did, and the degradation trace records what the physiology did, the lifecycle trace records what the registry did to its objects.

### A5.10.2 The schema

```yaml
LifecycleTraceEntry:
  entry_id: uuid
  at: datetime
  operation: LifecycleOperation
  target_type: LifecycleTargetType
  target_id: string
  target_version: semver | null
  target_contract_digest: sha256 | null
  from_state: string | null              # lifecycle state name, enum-discriminated by target_type
  to_state: string | null
  outcome: LifecycleOutcome
  outcome_at: datetime
  validation_issues: [ValidationIssue]   # non-empty iff outcome = REJECTED
  request_digest: sha256
  response_digest: sha256
  approver_signature: bytes | null       # present iff operation = PROMOTE
  evidence_digest: sha256 | null         # present iff operation = PROMOTE or SHADOW_VALIDATION
  test_bypass_promote: string | null     # present iff the F.2.1.2 bypass was used
  test_bypass_validation: bool | null    # present iff the F.2.1.2 bypass was used
  test_mode_active: bool
  on_drain:                               # present iff operation ∈ {HOT_SWAP, DRAIN_COMPLETED}
    started_at: datetime | null
    completed_at: datetime | null
    outcome: enum[completed, abandoned, killed] | null
  affected_modules: [ModuleHandle]        # present iff operation = HOT_SWAP
  audit_log_ref: sha256
```

### A5.10.3 Enums

```yaml
LifecycleOperation: enum[
  REGISTER, PROMOTE, DEREGISTER, HOT_SWAP,
  DRAIN_STARTED, DRAIN_COMPLETED, SEAL, RETIRE,
  SELF_TERMINATE,             # v0.2
  SHADOW_VALIDATION,          # records shadow records as an explicit event
  RULEBOOK_PUBLISH,           # A1.3 rulebook lifecycle
  POLICY_PUBLISH              # C.2 RouterPolicyArtifact lifecycle
]

LifecycleTargetType: enum[
  MODULE, SINK, RULEBOOK, ROUTER_POLICY, CALIBRATION_DATASET, SINK_CONTRACT
]

LifecycleOutcome: enum[ACCEPTED, REJECTED, IN_PROGRESS, COMPLETED, ABANDONED, KILLED]
```

### A5.10.4 Invariants

| ID | Invariant |
|---|---|
| LCT-1 | Every lifecycle operation that reaches the registry emits exactly one `LifecycleTraceEntry`. `DRAIN_STARTED` and `DRAIN_COMPLETED` are two entries for one drain sequence, both with the same `target_id` and a shared correlation field (the `request_digest` of the initiating operation) |
| LCT-2 | `outcome = REJECTED ⇒ validation_issues ≠ []` and `validation_issues` contains at least one issue with `severity = ERROR` |
| LCT-3 | `outcome ∈ {ACCEPTED, COMPLETED, IN_PROGRESS} ⇒ validation_issues = []` |
| LCT-4 | `operation = PROMOTE ⇒ approver_signature ≠ null ∧ evidence_digest ≠ null` |
| LCT-5 | `test_bypass_promote ≠ null ⇒ test_mode_active = true`; `test_bypass_validation = true ⇒ test_mode_active = true`. The production trace validator rejects any production trace containing either field |
| LCT-6 | `operation = HOT_SWAP ⇒ on_drain ≠ null ∧ affected_modules ≠ []` |
| LCT-7 | `operation = DRAIN_COMPLETED ⇒ on_drain.outcome ≠ null` |
| LCT-8 | `audit_log_ref` points to the corresponding A.2 `AuditLogEntry`; the entry's `payload_digest` equals this entry's `request_digest` for non-drain operations, and the drain sequence's `request_digest` for drain operations |
| LCT-9 | `entry_id` is the content address of the entry body minus `entry_id` and `response_digest` — the digest of `response_digest` is included in the entry body, but the trace's own `entry_id` does not depend on the response content, so a response whose content is deterministic produces a stable `entry_id` across replays |
| LCT-10 | `from_state` and `to_state` are null for `operation ∈ {REGISTER, SHADOW_VALIDATION, RULEBOOK_PUBLISH, POLICY_PUBLISH}` (these operations create or validate an object that has no prior lifecycle state in the trace's scope) |
| LCT-11 | `affected_modules` is empty for all operations except `HOT_SWAP`; a non-empty `affected_modules` on any other operation is a schema violation |

### A5.10.5 Replay integration

Replay of a lifecycle operation:

```
replay_lifecycle_entry(entry, replayed_state):
  1. Fetch the request from request_digest (content-addressed store)
  2. Verify the request's canonical digest equals request_digest
  3. Re-execute the operation against replayed_state
  4. Compute response_digest of the re-execution's response
  5. Compare to entry.response_digest
     - mismatch: emit ReplayMismatch{entry.entry_id, expected, actual}; ABORT
  6. Verify entry.outcome matches the re-execution's outcome
  7. Verify entry.validation_issues matches the re-execution's validation issues
  8. Verify entry.from_state, entry.to_state match the re-execution's state transition
  9. Verify test_bypass fields are consistent with the replayed state's test-mode context
```

The replay check is stricter for lifecycle operations than for workspace operations because lifecycle operations mutate the registry, and a replay mismatch on a lifecycle operation indicates a registry determinism bug, not just a trace formatting bug.

---

## 5. Amendment A6.7 — Missing messages

Add to A6 as a new section. The service definition in A6.3 is unchanged; the messages it references are added here.

### A6.7.1 Sink access types

```proto
message SinkOperation {
  enum Op {
    OP_UNSPECIFIED = 0;
    OP_WRITE       = 1;
    OP_READ        = 2;
    OP_PROMOTE     = 3;
    OP_REJECTION   = 4;  // L3/L4 exclusion, corruption quarantine, etc.
  }
}

message SinkAccessEntry {
  string entry_id                      = 1;  // content-addressed
  google.protobuf.Timestamp at         = 2;
  string sink_id                       = 3;
  SinkOperation.Op operation           = 4;
  string module_id                     = 5;  // accessing module
  bytes  module_contract_digest        = 6;
  repeated string entry_ids            = 7;  // opaque sink-side entry IDs
  bytes  result_digest                 = 8;  // non-null iff operation = OP_READ
  string rejection_reason_code         = 9;  // non-null iff operation = OP_REJECTION
  string promotion_id                  = 10; // non-null iff operation = OP_PROMOTE
  string signed_by                     = 11; // promotion authority key ID; non-null iff OP_PROMOTE
  bytes  sink_entry_ref                = 12; // content address of the corresponding ExplorationSinkTraceEntry
  bytes  signature                     = 13;
}

message SinkStatus {
  string sink_id                       = 1;
  bytes  contract_digest               = 2;
  SinkLifecycleState state             = 3;
  google.protobuf.Timestamp state_since = 4;
  int64  entry_count                   = 5;
  int64  storage_bytes                 = 6;
  int32  retention_days_max            = 7;
  int32  retention_remaining_days      = 8;  // for the oldest entry
  google.protobuf.Timestamp last_write_at = 9;
  google.protobuf.Timestamp last_read_at  = 10;
  string seal_target                   = 11; // non-null iff state = SEALED
  string seal_reason                   = 12; // non-null iff state = SEALED
  repeated string active_writers       = 13;
  google.protobuf.Timestamp registered_at = 14;
}
```

**Invariants:**

| ID | Invariant |
|---|---|
| SAE-1 | `SinkAccessEntry.sink_entry_ref` equals the content address of the corresponding `ExplorationSinkTraceEntry`; the corresponding entry's `audit_log_ref` equals this entry's `entry_id`. Bidirectional referential integrity |
| SAE-2 | `operation = OP_READ ⇒ result_digest ≠ null`; `operation ≠ OP_READ ⇒ result_digest = null` |
| SAE-3 | `operation = OP_PROMOTE ⇒ promotion_id ≠ null ∧ signed_by ≠ null` |
| SAE-4 | `operation = OP_REJECTION ⇒ rejection_reason_code ≠ null` |
| SS-1 | `state = SEALED ⇒ seal_target ≠ null ∧ seal_reason ≠ null`; `state ≠ SEALED ⇒ seal_target = null ∧ seal_reason = null` |
| SS-2 | `retention_remaining_days ≤ retention_days_max` |

### A6.7.2 RPC request/response messages

```proto
// Register
message RegisterSinkRequest {
  ExplorationSinkContract contract   = 1;
  bytes artifact_digest              = 2;
  bytes author_signature             = 3;
  organism.v1.ModuleLifecycleState test_bypass_promote = 4;  // nullable in proto3: presence = bypass requested
}
message RegisterSinkResponse {
  string sink_id                                 = 1;
  SinkLifecycleState initial_state               = 2;
  repeated ValidationIssue validation_issues     = 3;
}

// Deregister
message DeregisterSinkRequest {
  string sink_id                   = 1;
  DrainPolicy drain                = 2;  // reused from A.2
  int32 drain_timeout_s            = 3;
  bool  accept_chain_break         = 4;  // for future justification-chain integration
}
message DeregisterSinkResponse {
  bool accepted                    = 1;
  repeated ValidationIssue validation_issues = 2;
}

// GetContract
message SinkContractRequest {
  string sink_id                   = 1;
}

// List
message ListSinksRequest {
  SinkLifecycleState min_state     = 1;  // filter; SINK_LIFECYCLE_UNSPECIFIED = no filter
  repeated string writer_module_ids = 2; // filter; empty = no filter
}
message ListSinksResponse {
  repeated SinkStatus sinks        = 1;
}

// GetAccessLog
message AccessLogRequest {
  string sink_id                   = 1;
  google.protobuf.Timestamp since  = 2;
  google.protobuf.Timestamp until  = 3;
  SinkOperation.Op operation_filter = 4;  // OP_UNSPECIFIED = no filter
}

// GetStatus
message SinkStatusRequest {
  string sink_id                   = 1;
}
```

### A6.7.3 The `test_bypass_promote` field on `RegisterSinkRequest`

This field carries the F.2.1.2 per-registration lifecycle bypass. Proto3's field-presence semantics make it nullable without a wrapper: a set field means the bypass is requested, an unset field means normal lifecycle applies. The registry records `test_bypass_promote: <state>` in the corresponding A5.10 `LifecycleTraceEntry`, which the production trace validator rejects if present.

**Deprecation note:** the previously-declared `ORG_TEST_SINK_INITIAL_STATE` environment variable (deprecated in the R-28 amendment) is superseded by this field. The registry reads the env var only if the field is unset and the env var is present, and emits a deprecation warning in the response's `validation_issues` array. This preserves backward compatibility for pre-R-28 scenarios without extending the deprecated mechanism.

---

## 6. Scope note — what is *not* in this delivery

**`AuditCriteria`** is referenced in F.6.1.2 (`audit_store.has_record(criteria: AuditCriteria)`) but not defined. It is a registry-side query descriptor, not an A5 or A6 concern. Its absence will surface as a codegen error in M1 when the `audit_store` client is generated. The resolution is a one-message addition to A.2 or A.3, and it follows the same pattern as R-34 and R-35. It is not part of this delivery because it does not belong to A5 or A6, and because the M1 team may prefer to define it alongside the audit-store client implementation rather than in advance.

**No other referenced-but-undefined types are known.** The spot-check in the reconciliation pass (§5.3 of the closure delivery) found no dangling references; R-34 and R-35 are post-closure discoveries of types that the reconciliation pass's manual scan missed. If the M1 codegen step finds another, it follows the same pattern: a mechanical addition to the artifact that should own the type, with no design choice introduced, positioned as an implementation-phase amendment.

## 7. Status

| Finding | Closure |
|---|---|
| R-34 | §4 (A5.10 — `LifecycleTraceEntry` with LCT-1 through LCT-11) |
| R-35 | §5 (A6.7 — `SinkAccessEntry`, `SinkStatus`, eight RPC messages, SAE-1 through SAE-4, SS-1, SS-2) |

**Corpus closure stands.** The 87 active invariants are unchanged. The scenario catalog membership is unchanged. No design decision is reopened.

**New invariants added:** LCT-1 through LCT-11 (11), SAE-1 through SAE-4 (4), SS-1 through SS-2 (2). These are lifecycle and sink-access invariants; they are new because the types they govern were not previously defined. Adding them does not contradict any existing invariant — they are the invariants the types would have had to satisfy anyway.

**Test coverage:** LCT-1 through LCT-11 are exercised by I4, I5, I16, I19 (all of which use lifecycle operations). SAE-1 through SAE-4 are exercised by I21 (promotion), I22 (access log), I22b (rejection). SS-1 and SS-2 are exercised by I23 (seal). The load-time validation will accept the existing `tests_invariants` declarations without modification, because the invariants were already implied by the scenarios' assertions — they were just not yet named.

**M1 readiness is unchanged.** The stubs that currently reference `LifecycleTraceEntry`, `SinkAccessEntry`, `SinkStatus`, and the RPC messages compile against `Message`; the stubs are replaced by generated types when this amendment folds. No M1 runtime behavior changes.

---

## 8. Handoff

The implementation-phase amendment pattern is now established:

1. A referenced-but-undefined type is discovered by codegen, not by review.
2. The type is completed mechanically, with fields derived from its usage sites and invariants derived from the tests that will exercise it.
3. The amendment is positioned as implementation-phase work, not spec-phase work — it does not reopen design.
4. M1–M6 proceed in parallel; the type's stubs are replaced when the amendment folds.

R-34 and R-35 follow this pattern. So will `AuditCriteria` when it surfaces. The v0.2 gate is unchanged: the six structural questions from the biological extension, answered by the v0.2 design phase, which opens when M7 is green.

"Alright, lets produce all possible code in full with no gaps or placeholders for as much of the project as possible before I hand over all code and context to the build agent on my machine. Provide everything in effective and efficient batches till everything has been provided and I will keep saying next until everything has been produced."

# Batch 1 of N — Foundation: Project Skeleton, Proto Definitions, and Build Configuration

This batch establishes the repository structure, the complete proto3 source of truth (all of A, A0–A6, R-34, R-35 folded in), and the build configuration. It is the foundation every subsequent batch compiles against. The build agent can immediately run `protoc` against these files and get typed stubs for Python, Rust, and any other supported target.

## Repository layout

```
organism/
├── README.md
├── LICENSE
├── .gitignore
├── .editorconfig
├── Makefile
├── pyproject.toml

│   ├── deterministic_engine/               # Batch 3 (Python)
│   ├── learned_module/                     # Batch 9 (Python)
│   ├── kernel/                             # Batch 10 (Rust)
│   └── hdc/                                # Batch 10 (Rust)
├── harness/                                # Batch 11-12 (Rust + YAML)
│   ├── runner/
│   ├── injector/
│   ├── observer/
│   ├── assertions/
│   └── scenarios/
└── ci/
    ├── gates.yaml                          # Batch 13
    └── fault_injection/
```

## `proto/organism/v1/common.proto`

```protobuf
syntax = "proto3";

package organism.v1;

import "google/protobuf/timestamp.proto";

option go_package = "github.com/organism/v1;organism";

// ============================================================
// Shared enums used across multiple artifacts.
// ============================================================

enum Severity {
  SEVERITY_UNSPECIFIED = 0;
  SEVERITY_WARNING     = 1;
  SEVERITY_ERROR       = 2;
}

enum ComparisonOp {
  OP_UNSPECIFIED = 0;
  OP_GT          = 1;
  OP_GE          = 2;
  OP_LT          = 3;
  OP_LE          = 4;
}

// Drain policy for deregistration (R-18).
enum DrainPolicy {
  DRAIN_UNSPECIFIED  = 0;
  DRAIN_IMMEDIATE    = 1;
  DRAIN_GRACEFUL     = 2;
  DRAIN_THEN_SWAP    = 3;
}

// Swap strategies for hot-swap.
enum SwapStrategy {
  SWAP_UNSPECIFIED      = 0;
  SWAP_BLUE_GREEN       = 1;
  SWAP_CANARY_PERCENT   = 2;
  SWAP_LIVE_DRAIN       = 3;
}

// Common validation issue returned by registry operations.
message ValidationIssue {
  Severity severity       = 1;
  string   code           = 2;
  string   field_path     = 3;
  string   message        = 4;
  map<string, string> params = 5;
}

// A module handle returned by queries.
message ModuleHandle {
  string module_id             = 1;
  string version               = 2;
  ModuleRole role              = 3;
  ReliabilityTier tier         = 4;
  ModuleLifecycleState state   = 5;
  bytes  contract_digest       = 6;
}

// Forward declarations used by ModuleHandle (defined in contract.proto).
enum ModuleRole {
  ROLE_UNSPECIFIED        = 0;
  ROLE_PERCEPTION         = 1;
  ROLE_PREDICTION         = 2;
  ROLE_SENTINEL           = 3;
  ROLE_SOLVER             = 4;
  ROLE_VERIFIER           = 5;
  ROLE_GOVERNANCE         = 6;
  ROLE_FEATURE_EXTRACTOR  = 7;
  ROLE_MEMORY             = 8;
  ROLE_ACTUATOR           = 9;
  ROLE_FRONTEND           = 10;
}

enum ReliabilityTier {
  TIER_UNSPECIFIED              = 0;
  TIER_T0_DETERMINISTIC         = 1;
  TIER_T1_CALIBRATED_NONLEARNED = 2;
  TIER_T2_LEARNED_CALIBRATED    = 3;
  TIER_T3_LEARNED_UNCALIBRATED  = 4;
}

enum MaxSafetyTier {
  SAFETY_UNSPECIFIED = 0;
  SAFETY_RESEARCH    = 1;
  SAFETY_LOW         = 2;
  SAFETY_MEDIUM      = 3;
  SAFETY_HIGH        = 4;
  SAFETY_CRITICAL    = 5;
}

enum ModuleLifecycleState {
  LIFECYCLE_UNSPECIFIED      = 0;
  LIFECYCLE_PROPOSED         = 1;
  LIFECYCLE_SANDBOXED        = 2;
  LIFECYCLE_SHADOW_VALIDATED = 3;
  LIFECYCLE_EXPLORATION      = 4;
  LIFECYCLE_DELIBERATION     = 5;
  LIFECYCLE_DEPRECATED       = 6;
  LIFECYCLE_RETIRED          = 7;
}

// Degradation level enum (B + A0).
enum Level {
  LEVEL_UNSPECIFIED = 0;
  LEVEL_L0          = 1;
  LEVEL_L1          = 2;
  LEVEL_L2          = 3;
  LEVEL_L3          = 4;
  LEVEL_L4          = 5;
}

// Budget class (deprecated in favor of scaling law; retained for compatibility).
enum BudgetClass {
  BUDGET_UNSPECIFIED = 0;
  BUDGET_LEAN        = 1;
  BUDGET_STANDARD    = 2;
  BUDGET_HEAVY       = 3;
  BUDGET_RESEARCH    = 4;
}

// Hardware requirement.
enum HardwareRequirement {
  HW_UNSPECIFIED        = 0;
  HW_NONE               = 1;
  HW_NEUROMORPHIC       = 2;
  HW_EVENT_CAMERA       = 3;
  HW_PHYSICAL_SUBSTRATE = 4;
  HW_TPU_NPU            = 5;
}

// Runtime identifiers.
enum RuntimeId {
  RUNTIME_UNSPECIFIED  = 0;
  RUNTIME_CPU_GENERAL  = 1;
  RUNTIME_GPU_CUDA     = 2;
  RUNTIME_GPU_ROCM     = 3;
  RUNTIME_GPU_METAL    = 4;
  RUNTIME_NPU          = 5;
  RUNTIME_NEUROMORPHIC = 6;
  RUNTIME_WASM         = 7;
}
```

## `proto/organism/v1/contract.proto`

```protobuf
syntax = "proto3";

package organism.v1;

import "google/protobuf/timestamp.proto";
import "organism/v1/common.proto";

option go_package = "github.com/organism/v1;organism";

// ============================================================
// SubstrateContract — A.1 with amendments R-7, R-14, R-19, R-6, R-8, E-7.
// Every module declares this before registration.
// ============================================================

message SubstrateContract {
  Identity             identity       = 1;
  PerceptionSignature  perception     = 2;
  EmissionSignature    emission       = 3;
  MetabolicProfile     metabolic      = 4;
  FailureSignature     failure        = 5;
  SafetyDeclaration    safety         = 6;
  Lifecycle            lifecycle      = 7;
  CapabilityScopes     capabilities   = 8;
  repeated Channel     channels       = 9;
  JustificationSpec    justification  = 10;
}

message Identity {
  string           module_id           = 1;
  string           version             = 2;
  string           author              = 3;
  string           license             = 4;
  ModuleRole       role                = 5;
  bytes            artifact_digest     = 6;
  repeated bytes   signatures_b64      = 7;
}

// ---- Perception --------------------------------------------------

message PerceptionSignature {
  repeated Modality                            modalities          = 1;
  map<string, Shape>                           shapes              = 2;
  map<string, SamplingRequirement>             sampling            = 3;
  repeated Modality                            required_modalities = 4;  // R-7
}

enum Modality {
  MODALITY_UNSPECIFIED      = 0;
  MODALITY_DENSE_VECTOR     = 1;
  MODALITY_TEMPORAL_STREAM  = 2;
  MODALITY_SPIKE_TRAIN      = 3;
  MODALITY_SYMBOL_GRAPH     = 4;
  MODALITY_WAVELET_COEFFS   = 5;
  MODALITY_PHYSICAL_SENSOR  = 6;
  MODALITY_HYPERVECTOR      = 7;
  MODALITY_TABULAR          = 8;
  MODALITY_TEXT             = 9;
  MODALITY_IMAGE            = 10;
}

enum DataType {
  DTYPE_UNSPECIFIED = 0;
  DTYPE_FP32        = 1;
  DTYPE_FP16        = 2;
  DTYPE_INT8        = 3;
  DTYPE_BINARY      = 4;
  DTYPE_BOOL        = 5;
  DTYPE_INT64       = 6;
}

message Shape {
  repeated int64 dims = 1;
  DataType       dtype = 2;
}

message SamplingRequirement {
  float  rate_hz       = 1;
  int64  window_len    = 2;
  bool   requires_sync = 3;
}

// ---- Emission ---------------------------------------------------

message EmissionSignature {
  OutputForm          output_form                  = 1;
  Shape               shape                        = 2;
  UncertaintyKind     uncertainty                  = 3;
  Determinism         determinism                  = 4;
  repeated string     requires_justification_for   = 5;  // action classes needing a token
}

enum OutputForm {
  OUTPUT_UNSPECIFIED            = 0;
  OUTPUT_HYPERVECTOR            = 1;
  OUTPUT_SPIKE_TRAIN            = 2;
  OUTPUT_ENERGY_GRADIENT        = 3;
  OUTPUT_SYMBOLIC_TRIPLE        = 4;
  OUTPUT_PROBABILITY_INTERVAL   = 5;
  OUTPUT_SCALAR                 = 6;
  OUTPUT_ASSIGNMENT_CERTIFICATE = 7;
  OUTPUT_FEATURE_VECTOR         = 8;
  OUTPUT_JUSTIFICATION_TOKEN    = 9;
}

enum UncertaintyKind {
  UNCERTAINTY_UNSPECIFIED         = 0;
  UNCERTAINTY_NONE                = 1;
  UNCERTAINTY_CONFORMAL_INTERVAL  = 2;
  UNCERTAINTY_ENSEMBLE_SPREAD     = 3;
  UNCERTAINTY_ENERGY_LANDSCAPE    = 4;
  UNCERTAINTY_SYMBOLIC_PROOF      = 5;
}

enum Determinism {
  DETERMINISM_UNSPECIFIED   = 0;
  DETERMINISM_DETERMINISTIC = 1;
  DETERMINISM_SEEDED        = 2;
  DETERMINISM_STOCHASTIC    = 3;
}

// ---- Metabolic profile ------------------------------------------

message MetabolicProfile {
  float                 latency_p50_ms              = 1;
  float                 latency_p95_ms              = 2;
  float                 latency_p99_ms              = 3;
  float                 energy_per_inference_j      = 4;
  float                 memory_footprint_mb         = 5;
  float                 memory_peak_mb              = 6;
  bool                  requires_gradient_training  = 7;
  bool                  requires_gpu                = 8;
  HardwareRequirement   requires_special_hardware   = 9;
  int32                 max_concurrent_invocations  = 10;
  BudgetClass           metabolic_budget_class      = 11;
  ResourceVector        declared_share              = 12;
}

message ResourceVector {
  float cpu_cores    = 1;
  float gpu_fraction = 2;
  float memory_mb    = 3;
  float network_mbps = 4;
  float energy_watts = 5;
}

// ---- Failure ----------------------------------------------------

message FailureSignature {
  repeated FailureMode          modes       = 1;
  HealthProbe                   probe       = 2;
  RecoveryProcedure             recovery    = 3;
  HealthEscalationPolicy        escalation  = 4;  // R-6
}

message FailureMode {
  string             name                        = 1;
  string             observable_signal           = 2;
  DetectionMethod    detection                   = 3;
  DegradationKind    expected_degradation        = 4;
  float              detection_latency_budget_ms = 5;
}

enum DegradationKind {
  DEGRADE_UNSPECIFIED              = 0;
  DEGRADE_SILENT_ACCURACY_LOSS     = 1;
  DEGRADE_SPIKE_SILENCE            = 2;
  DEGRADE_ENERGY_DIVERGENCE        = 3;
  DEGRADE_SYMBOL_GROUNDING_FAILURE = 4;
  DEGRADE_CALIBRATION_DRIFT        = 5;
  DEGRADE_TIMEOUT                  = 6;
  DEGRADE_CRASH                    = 7;
}

// R-19: Detection method messages.
message DetectionMethod {
  oneof kind {
    ThresholdDetection    threshold = 1;
    StatisticalDetection  stats     = 2;
    HeartbeatDetection    heartbeat = 3;
    BehavioralDetection   behavior  = 4;
  }
}

message ThresholdDetection {
  string        signal_name = 1;
  double        threshold   = 2;
  ComparisonOp  op          = 3;
  int32         window_ms   = 4;
}

message StatisticalDetection {
  string   signal_name      = 1;
  string   statistic        = 2;
  double   threshold        = 3;
  int32    window_ms        = 4;
  bytes    reference_digest = 5;
}

message HeartbeatDetection {
  int32 expected_period_ms      = 1;
  int32 missing_count_threshold = 2;
}

message BehavioralDetection {
  string   behavior_metric   = 1;
  double   threshold         = 2;
  int32    window_ms         = 3;
  bytes    classifier_digest = 4;
}

message HealthProbe {
  oneof kind {
    HealthHttp    http    = 1;
    HealthRpc     rpc     = 2;
    HealthStream  stream  = 3;
  }
}

message HealthHttp {
  string url              = 1;
  string expected_status  = 2;
  int32  timeout_ms       = 3;
}

message HealthRpc {
  string method_fqn = 1;
  int32  timeout_ms = 2;
}

message HealthStream {
  string topic               = 1;
  int32  expected_period_ms  = 2;
  int32  stall_timeout_ms    = 3;
}

message RecoveryProcedure {
  RecoveryKind     kind                 = 1;
  int32            max_attempts         = 2;
  int32            backoff_ms_initial   = 3;
  float            backoff_multiplier   = 4;
  int32            backoff_ms_max       = 5;
  repeated string  preconditions        = 6;
}

enum RecoveryKind {
  RECOVERY_UNSPECIFIED         = 0;
  RECOVERY_RESTART             = 1;
  RECOVERY_RESTORE_SNAPSHOT    = 2;
  RECOVERY_DEMOTE              = 3;
  RECOVERY_HOT_SWAP            = 4;
  RECOVERY_MANUAL              = 5;
}

// R-6 / A4: health escalation policy.
message HealthEscalationPolicy {
  EscalationKind kind                          = 1;
  int32          consecutive_probe_failures    = 2;
  int32          degraded_duration_ms          = 3;
  bool           escalate_on_probe_timeout     = 4;
  bool           escalate_on_probe_unreachable = 5;
  int32          probe_interval_ms             = 6;
}

enum EscalationKind {
  ESCALATION_UNSPECIFIED        = 0;
  ESCALATION_NONE               = 1;
  ESCALATION_PROBE_FAILURES     = 2;
  ESCALATION_DURATION           = 3;
  ESCALATION_PROBE_OR_DURATION  = 4;
}

// ---- Safety -----------------------------------------------------

message SafetyDeclaration {
  ReliabilityTier   reliability_tier             = 1;
  bool              may_actuate                  = 2;
  bool              requires_causal_certificate  = 3;
  bool              requires_cross_check         = 4;
  repeated ModuleRole cross_check_eligible_roles = 5;
  MaxSafetyTier     max_safety_tier              = 6;
}

// ---- Capability scopes (with R-8 / A6.5 / E-7 fields) ----------

message CapabilityScopes {
  repeated string read_roles                       = 1;
  repeated string write_roles                      = 2;
  repeated string read_tags                        = 3;
  repeated string write_tags                       = 4;
  bool            can_emit_control_affecting       = 5;
  repeated string allowed_channels                 = 6;
  bool            can_override_trace_sink          = 7;  // T0/T1 only
  bool            can_override_trace_sink_exploration = 8;  // T0/T1/T2
  string          exploration_trace_sink_id        = 9;  // required iff field 8
}

// ---- Channels ---------------------------------------------------

message Channel {
  string           channel_id              = 1;
  string           producer_module         = 2;
  repeated string  consumer_modules        = 3;
  Modality         payload_modality        = 4;
  Shape            payload_shape           = 5;
  Delivery         delivery                = 6;
  bool             logged_for_trace        = 7;
}

enum Delivery {
  DELIVERY_UNSPECIFIED   = 0;
  DELIVERY_AT_MOST_ONCE  = 1;
  DELIVERY_AT_LEAST_ONCE = 2;
  DELIVERY_EXACTLY_ONCE  = 3;
  DELIVERY_STREAMING     = 4;
}

// ---- Justification ----------------------------------------------

message JustificationSpec {
  JustificationKind kind             = 1;
  string            format           = 2;
  repeated string   required_fields  = 3;
  repeated ModuleRole checkable_by   = 4;
}

enum JustificationKind {
  JUSTIFICATION_UNSPECIFIED     = 0;
  JUSTIFICATION_NONE            = 1;
  JUSTIFICATION_CAUSAL_CERT     = 2;
  JUSTIFICATION_KERNEL_BOUND    = 3;
  JUSTIFICATION_RULE_TRACE      = 4;
  JUSTIFICATION_ENERGY_CERT     = 5;
  JUSTIFICATION_SYMBOLIC_PROOF  = 6;
}

// ---- Lifecycle --------------------------------------------------

message Lifecycle {
  bool                     hot_swap_supported          = 1;
  bytes                    rollback_artifact_digest    = 2;
  bool                     shadow_validation_required  = 3;
  PromotionCriteria        promotion                   = 4;
  repeated RuntimeId       disallowed_runtimes         = 5;
}

message PromotionCriteria {
  int32            shadow_invocations_min    = 1;
  float            max_incumbent_error_delta = 2;
  repeated string  required_gates            = 3;
  ModuleRole       approver_role             = 4;
  bool             causal_review_required    = 5;
}
```

## `proto/organism/v1/registry.proto`

```protobuf
syntax = "proto3";

package organism.v1.registry;

import "google/protobuf/timestamp.proto";
import "organism/v1/common.proto";
import "organism/v1/contract.proto";

option go_package = "github.com/organism/v1/registry;registry";

// ============================================================
// SubstrateRegistry — A.2 with amendments.
// ============================================================

service SubstrateRegistry {
  rpc Register       (RegisterRequest)        returns (RegisterResponse);
  rpc Deregister     (DeregisterRequest)      returns (DeregisterResponse);
  rpc Query          (QueryRequest)           returns (QueryResponse);
  rpc GetContract    (ContractRequest)        returns (organism.v1.SubstrateContract);
  rpc Health         (HealthRequest)          returns (HealthReport);
  rpc Promote        (PromoteRequest)         returns (PromoteResponse);
  rpc HotSwap        (HotSwapRequest)         returns (HotSwapResponse);
  rpc ListVersions   (ListVersionsRequest)    returns (ListVersionsResponse);
  rpc DiffContracts  (DiffRequest)            returns (DiffResponse);
  rpc GetAuditLog    (AuditLogRequest)        returns (stream AuditLogEntry);
}

message RegisterRequest {
  organism.v1.SubstrateContract contract = 1;
  bytes                          artifact_digest   = 2;
  bytes                          author_signature  = 3;
  repeated string                promotion_evidence = 4;
  // R-28: per-registration lifecycle bypass. Nullable via presence.
  ModuleLifecycleState           test_bypass_promote = 5;
}

message RegisterResponse {
  string                          module_id          = 1;
  ModuleLifecycleState            initial_state      = 2;
  repeated ValidationIssue        validation_issues  = 3;
}

message DeregisterRequest {
  string       module_id          = 1;
  DrainPolicy  drain              = 2;
  int32        drain_timeout_s    = 3;
  bool         accept_chain_break = 4;
}

message DeregisterResponse {
  bool                     accepted          = 1;
  repeated ValidationIssue validation_issues = 2;
}

message QueryRequest {
  CapabilityFilter filter = 1;
}

message CapabilityFilter {
  repeated organism.v1.ModuleRole     roles                 = 1;
  organism.v1.ReliabilityTier         min_reliability       = 2;
  organism.v1.MaxSafetyTier           min_max_safety        = 3;
  organism.v1.HardwareRequirement     max_hardware          = 4;
  organism.v1.BudgetClass             max_budget            = 5;
  repeated organism.v1.Modality       input_modalities      = 6;
  organism.v1.OutputForm              required_output       = 7;
  ModuleLifecycleState                min_lifecycle_state   = 8;
}

message QueryResponse {
  repeated organism.v1.ModuleHandle handles = 1;
}

message ContractRequest {
  string module_id = 1;
  string version   = 2;
}

message HealthRequest {
  string module_id = 1;
}

message HealthReport {
  string                          module_id        = 1;
  HealthStatus                    status           = 2;
  float                           score            = 3;
  repeated FailureSignal          active_failures  = 4;
  google.protobuf.Timestamp       observed_at      = 5;
}

enum HealthStatus {
  HEALTH_UNSPECIFIED = 0;
  HEALTH_HEALTHY     = 1;
  HEALTH_DEGRADED    = 2;
  HEALTH_FAILED      = 3;
}

message FailureSignal {
  string                    failure_mode_name = 1;
  float                     observed_value    = 2;
  float                     threshold         = 3;
  google.protobuf.Timestamp first_observed    = 4;
}

message PromoteRequest {
  string                          module_id          = 1;
  ModuleLifecycleState            from_state         = 2;
  ModuleLifecycleState            to_state           = 3;
  repeated ShadowValidationRecord shadow_records     = 4;
  bytes                           approver_signature = 5;
  bool                            test_bypass_validation = 6;  // R-28
}

message PromoteResponse {
  bool                     accepted          = 1;
  repeated ValidationIssue validation_issues = 2;
}

message ShadowValidationRecord {
  int32            invocations      = 1;
  float            incumbent_error  = 2;
  float            candidate_error  = 3;
  float            p95_latency_ms   = 4;
  repeated string  gates_passed     = 5;
  bytes            evidence_digest  = 6;
}

message HotSwapRequest {
  string       module_id       = 1;
  string       new_version     = 2;
  SwapStrategy strategy        = 3;
  float        drain_timeout_s = 4;
}

message HotSwapResponse {
  bool                     accepted          = 1;
  repeated ValidationIssue validation_issues = 2;
}

message ListVersionsRequest {
  string module_id = 1;
}

message ListVersionsResponse {
  repeated string versions = 1;
}

message DiffRequest {
  string module_id   = 1;
  string version_a   = 2;
  string version_b   = 3;
}

message DiffResponse {
  string patch_json = 1;
}

message AuditLogRequest {
  google.protobuf.Timestamp since = 1;
  google.protobuf.Timestamp until = 2;
  string event_type_filter = 3;
}

message AuditLogEntry {
  string                    event_type    = 1;
  string                    module_id     = 2;
  bytes                     payload_digest = 3;
  google.protobuf.Timestamp at            = 4;
}
```
